# ShiftLead — design history

The dated record of how [`design.md`](design.md) came to say what it says: decisions, reversals,
review rounds, live findings and Paul's words, by section, in date order. `design.md` is the
present-tense specification and states only what is true now; this file is where its history went
on 2026-09-22 (the design review, TD-109) and where every later change appends its dated fact.
Nothing here is a rule. Where a bullet and the design disagree, the design is current and the
bullet is what was true on its date.

The ADRs under [`decisions/`](decisions/) and the ledger ([`technical_debt.md`](technical_debt.md),
[`technical_debt_archive.md`](technical_debt_archive.md)) hold the reasoning behind the larger
decisions; this file points at them rather than repeating them.

## Title and status

- 2026-10-06 (TD-351, grinder-ao-2, PR #1172): the split landed — `scripts/split_design.py` moved the design into thirty files under `docs/design/`, nothing reflowed and a relative link's path given `../`; `docs/design.md` became the index; the doc-bound tests read through `tests/design_doc.py`; the briefs point their greps at `docs/design/`.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-346, the designer; Paul, that day: *do it, in a quiet window*): the layout of the split decided — thirty files under `docs/design/` named `<§>-<slug>.md`, one per section and per §4 subsection, each beginning with its heading line as it stands; `design.md` the index; this history kept one file; tests through `tests/design_doc.py`; the move a script, landed when no open PR touches the file. Not built — TD-351.
- 2026-09-04: the design session that produced this document; the project was called `sessionherd` for most of that day (see §10 for the rename to agentorc).
- 2026-09-05: the last of the original open questions closed; building started the same day.
- 2026-09-21: Paul named the project ShiftLead (shiftlead.dev). The packages, state directory, units, config file and command home keep the `agentorc` name until TD-060's second step, at a release boundary.
- Until 2026-09-22 the Status paragraph read "phase 1 in progress", listing the host agent, the Claude Code adapter, the Org and Focus pages, New session and the CLI as running.

## 1. Problem

- 2026-08-07: the samscrape stranded-work audit that, with `tdgrind` (TD-274), supplied the lessons listed in §1 (reboot killed tmux, OAuth lapses, pane-grepped state, reports lost in scrollback, chat-only work lost on close).

## 2. Goals

- 2026-09-13 (TD-038): the Focus pane took VS Code's Dark Modern terminal palette as literals, not tokens, so the pane does not follow the page.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-038): the Focus pane's face became bundled JetBrains Mono (OFL, ligatures off) and it draws through xterm.js's WebGL renderer where available, DOM renderer otherwise.
- A hosted service was originally a non-goal; it became the `relay` transport (§4.5b), kept compatible from phase 1 and scheduled after phase 5.

## 3. Prior art

- 2026-09-04: the original survey (ttyd, ccmanager, claude-squad, Vibe Kanban, agent-dashboard, Anthropic Remote Control). At that point multi-host was itself a differentiator.
- 2026-09-05: ttyd's websocket-around-a-pty shape was superseded by a bridge inside the UI process, since the pty would wrap `ssh` anyway (§10).
- 2026-09-09: herdr added to the survey (not in the 2026-09-04 survey). Herdr, Inc. announced a $6M seed (Bessemer, YC) the same day; "Herdr Cloud" (no-ssh machines) was next; releases 0.5.1 (2026-04) → 0.9.0 (2026-09); 36.5k stars, ~770k installs; ~1k community plugins found by a GitHub topic, no review; 17 integrations of which six (Pi, OMP, Kimi, OpenCode, Kilo, MastraCode) push state from hooks; single binary for macOS, Linux, Windows; Claude rate-limit and context bars; features via Discussions, bugs fixed by the maintainers' own agent, an allow-list of approved contributors.
- 2026-09-10: herdr entry corrected and herdr measured as a substrate ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-10-herdr-spike.md)); not taken. Since herdr, multi-host on its own stopped being a differentiator. Its restore model is layout + `claude --resume`; its `pane.agent_status_changed` event carries only the state (the `--message` of a report is stored nowhere); community mobile apps exist, the TUI over ssh is its mobile story.
- 2026-09-10: OpenAI's Agents API entered public beta. Its stateful predecessor, the Assistants API, had sunset 2026-08-26. Environments could be OpenAI-hosted or Cloudflare / Vercel / Oracle / E2B / Modal / Daytona / DigitalOcean / Blaxel / Runloop; progress by streaming or webhooks; managed context compaction and subagent delegation; token billing, no infrastructure fee.
- 2026-09-12: the orchestrator-membership prior-art survey (supervision trees, owner references, unit relationships, capability attenuation, ACL placement) for the §10 question ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-12-orchestrator-membership-prior-art.md)).
- 2026-09-13: OpenAI's Agents API surveyed and added ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-13-openai-agents-api.md)).
- 2026-09-16: agent messaging surveyed (Claude Code cross-session messaging and agent teams, mcp_agent_mail, muster / agent-mux / muxcode) ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-16-agent-messaging-prior-art.md)).
- 2026-09-20 (TD-059): session desks and supervisors surveyed (agentboss, agent-director, multi-agent-shogun, claude-code-orchestrator, tmux_claude_codex_dashboard, orchardist) ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md)).

## 4. Architecture

- No dated facts removed from the §4 intro diagram.

## 4.1 Session substrate: tmux, one session per conversation

- undated (PR #17): Resume's supersede — closes the exited record and keeps it a day. The create-time supersede (below) extends it to a fresh start under the same name and goes one step further by forgetting rather than keeping.
- undated: `pipe-pane` continuous streaming to `~/.agentorc/runs/<session>-<created>.log` replaced tdgrind's per-tick snapshot.
- 2026-09-10 (§10 decision, §9 invariant 12): a name identifies one session within its scope (the repo, or the directory for a repo-less session).
- 2026-09-10 (TD-010 a): a live session with no tmux at all (`claude` in a VS Code terminal, found via Claude Code's registry `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`) was shown as a read-only card.
- 2026-09-10 ([ADR herdr spike](decisions/2026-09-10-herdr-spike.md)): measured on herdr that a runtime holding the ptys itself must kill every session to restart and re-launch the tool with `--resume` once a client attaches — the reason tmux owns the processes; the same spike saw a `.bashrc` `cd` relocate every pane — the reason argv runs directly, never through the person's shell.
- 2026-09-11 (TD-030, all of it): every `ao` subcommand that takes an id accepts a bare name ("so nothing that worked before changes" — a full `ao-…` id still means itself); the create-time name rules landed: live holder refused with error data, exited/closed holder superseded and replaced in place with *previous run* as a new field, unrecorded tmux panes decided on tmux's own answer, the check locked on the scope. What was wrong before: the same `ao new` would refuse or suffix depending on the second it landed in (whether the tick had adopted a stale pane), and `-2` cards appeared for a name that was simply reused.
- 2026-09-13 (TD-036 step 1): the `controllers` field landed (§4.8's membership list).
- 2026-09-13: `team` and `project` badges landed (§4.9).
- 2026-09-17: Paul had the no-tmux read-only cards (TD-010 a) removed — a card for a session a person started by hand, outside agentorc, reads as being watched, and it offered nothing to do. The Org is what agentorc started or adopted; the registry is still read only for `occupancy` (§9 invariant 2).
- 2026-09-20 (TD-077 a2): the suspended exception designed and built — a holder a person suspended over an identity alarm refuses every `create` under its name until a person lifts it; the name check answers `suspended`.
- 2026-09-20: a team would not start because a brief had reached 16,194 bytes and tmux refused the argv ("command too long", about 16 KB). Fix: past 8 KB the argv goes to `launch/<tmux name>.sh` (mode 0700) exec'd under `/bin/sh` — the one exception to argv-runs-directly.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-347 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the road of *the brief is the person's word* built — the line typed before the pasted brief, the preface at the head of the context file. Decided in the build: the adapter still writes the file, since it alone knows the conversation id it names the file by before the launch, and the host agent hands it the preface with the caller's start context already joined (`mail.start_context_file_text`), which keeps the preface one text for every tool; the record keeps the caller's text alone, so a resume joins it again rather than doubling the preface. The line is followed by a space, not a newline, since a newline typed as keys would submit the line alone. A paste refused after the line went in is known from the step that failed (`LeadTyped`), and the next try pastes the brief after the line: reading the line back does not work, since Claude Code paints the 111-character line over two rows of an 80-column pane and the composer read returns the first (the techlead's reading of PR #1158). The press on a real Claude Code session is left to the live copy, read from a member restarted after the promote: the scratch home's Claude Code launch pretrusts the directory in the profile's own Claude config, which a grinder may not write.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-348, grinder-ao-2; Paul, through the anchor, had it built beside TD-347 although its fix is in the host agent's package): **a brief whose first hook is lost, built.** *No hook since launch* is read as no hook event having reached the record. That is the host agent's own `_last_hook`, which a restart seeds for every record whose state a hook set. A hook-reported `idle` now also needs that, so a fresh record whose `confidence` starts as its adapter's `state_source` is not taken for a hook's word. §4.1 says one thing the design left open: a composer holding someone's words is waited past, uncounted, so the bound marks that record too.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-343 and TD-342, the designer; the anchor, asked by Paul why the team did nothing all day): **the brief is the person's word, and the tool is told so** — a typed line of the home's own before the pasted brief, and a fixed preface at the head of the context file, after a seat (Fable) took its pasted brief as data and held a PR ask ten hours (TD-343); and **a brief whose first hook is lost** — one send on a scraped idle after `FIRST_PROMPT_HOOK_WAIT`, then *brief not sent · no hook since launch* at `FIRST_PROMPT_BOUND` (TD-342, both of its (a) and (b)). Not taken: the brief inside the context file with a fixed *begin* as the first prompt. Not built — TD-347, TD-348.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-336, the designer; the anchor, asked by Paul why cm-grind was idle): **No prose in the argv** — the brief and the start context leave a session's command line, after contractmatch's `run_ci_tests.sh` cleanup (`pkill -f chromium`) killed the grinder running it, whose brief named Chromium. The start context goes by `--append-system-prompt-file` from a file beside the launch script; the brief is typed at the composer by the host agent's verified send, kept on the launch record until sent. Not taken: the brief inside the system-prompt file with a fixed first prompt (it would leave the conversation's first turn, Focus and the transcript without the brief, and make a `Brief changed` read a system prompt rather than a turn), and a rule in every template (a script the session does not read cannot be guarded by what it reads). Not built — TD-339.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-418, ao-paul with Paul): command-kind sessions are shown by the Org filter's word `kind:command`; the *show command runs* box is dropped — the mockup `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-10-08-org-declutter.html` (five rounds with Paul on 2026-10-08, the shots `2026-10-08-org-declutter-{before,after,after-open}.png` and their full-page pair, re-captured from the live page for round 4), built by TD-428.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-339 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the adapter's half of *No prose in the argv* built — the start context by `--append-system-prompt-file`, the prompt handed back as `first_prompt`, every launch but a resume at the composer. Decided in the build: the file is named by the conversation id, not the tmux name the design first gave, because the adapter's launch runs before the host agent picks the tmux name (it may retry on tmux's own *duplicate session*); it is removed when the last record holding the conversation is forgotten, since a Resume of that conversation hands it again; and a resume id that is not a plain file name is refused rather than written outside `launch/`.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-339 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the host agent's half of *No prose in the argv* built ahead of the adapter's, so a launch that still passes its prompt changes nothing. Decided in the build: the doorbell is held off while a brief is due, since its line would otherwise be the session's first prompt; a retry presses Enter again on a composer still holding the last try's brief rather than typing it twice (§4.2); a dialog or someone's words in the composer waits uncounted; and any `UserPromptSubmit` ends the brief's wait — a person's own first prompt included — since the hook carries the word `prompt`, never the text. The mark is the card's slot in the needs family's amber (its colour, never its ring) and a Focus header badge `#fbrief` beside *paused · usage*, kept current from the delta.

## 4.2 State feed: hooks first, scraping as a labelled fallback

- 2026-09-06: verified that a `--settings` file's hooks fire, which is what makes the per-launch settings layer viable.
- 2026-09-06 ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-06-adopt-dev-cadence.md)): decided that the host agent sets `AGENTORC_SESSION` and `AGENTORC_HOME` explicitly on the tmux session at creation, because the tmux server may predate the host agent and carry another environment.
- 2026-09-06 (Claude Code 2.1.263): measured the permission channel — the terminal dialog is not held back by a blocking `PermissionRequest` hook; it appears a few seconds into the wait with a `permission_prompt` notification, and the hook's answer still resolves it, so both channels work at once. The host agent ignores that notification while its waiter is live.
- 2026-09-06 (first-use finding): `Notification` `idle_prompt` was found to fire after a minute idle; it is ignored, since an idle session waiting for you is `idle`, not an alert.
- 2026-09-10 (TD-027): measured that an Enter reaching Claude Code before it has read the paste is dropped, so a burst of sends into a fresh or slow session lost every Enter but one; the paint-wait before Enter removes the race.
- 2026-09-11 (cadence §3): the settings layer took on dev-cadence's one SessionStart runner line (`scripts/cadence_hooks.sh --session-start`). The pre-2026-09-11 per-hook block in a worktree's `.claude/settings.json` counts as wiring the hooks, so such a worktree runs only the hooks its block names until its branch carries the runner line.
- (TD-015): the pane classifier's shape was chosen after herdr's detector caught the trust dialog that no hook reports.
- (TD-032): an unattended worker whose pane was stood down by a Remote Control takeover sat twenty hours with its PR unmerged and nothing flagged it, because the pane read as `idle`; the screen rule that reads the takeover banner as `stalled?` followed.
- 2026-09-17: the **no live member** item was added to the Ready to close checklist, read from the control graph (`controllers`) so it covers a manager, a director and a hand-attached controller alike.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-080): *pushed* became one measure computed once by the host agent. Before, the card's *n unpushed* flag, the checklist, the Inbox row and `ao team stop --close` had three tests between them. What it replaced: *ahead of the upstream*, which is *unmerged* when the upstream is `origin/main` — a launch branch tracking `origin/main` and pushed to `origin/<branch>` at wrap-up read as *308 unpushed* for ever, and a row that is always there teaches a person to ignore the row.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-095 (e)): the unseen mark read *finished · unseen* until this date; Paul's decision renamed it *idle · unseen*, because *finished* is what a member that declared itself out of work is called (§4.9a), and this is a turn nobody has looked at, not a run that is over.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-095 (f), decided by Paul): the unseen mark never applies to an `unattended` session — its result was read by its manager, the person is not expected to open it, and its slot already says *out of work* with Close session as the act.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-064, PR #431, grinder-ao-1): the unattended launch's settings layer gained `crossSessionInbound: refuse`, written to its own `<profile>[+cadence]+unattended.json`; the held-peer-message screen rule and the briefs' sentence landed with it. Paul's word: *refuse them*.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-115, grinder-ao-1): the hook stopped queueing an error reply. Until this date `agentorc-hook` treated any error from the host agent as an agent that was down and appended the event to `events/<session>.jsonl`, which the tick applies unjudged — so a hook refused by §4.8a was applied two seconds later anyway (the techlead seat's `exited (hook)` at 03:55Z on 2026-09-23, after the refusal).
- 2026-09-24 (TD-072, the designer): Ready to close gains **mail read**; the **outcomes reported** row TD-079 built on 2026-09-20 is written into the list, where it had been missing.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-026, the designer; Paul's answer the same day): the `scheduled` state — a record with a launch record and no pane, created at `ao new --at` or the form's **At** field.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-155, grinder-ao-1): `SessionStart` with `source: resume` reports `idle`. Until this date every `SessionStart` read `working`, and a one-press Resume with no prompt — the designer's record, 2026-09-25 14:06Z — sat `working (hook)` at an empty composer, headed for `stalled?` at `STALL_AFTER` and never rung by the doorbell, which rings a hook-confirmed idle only.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-169, grinder-ao-1): a queued hook event carries `at`, and a queued state older than a live one is skipped at the drain. Until this date the queue was applied unjudged after anything that had got through live, so a call that timed out (the host agent slow, not down) could land its state after a newer one — found by the review of PR #556 (TD-155), whose resume `idle` made one such pair diverge.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-201, grinder-ao-1): a subagent's `PreToolUse` / `PostToolUse` (the payload's `agent_id`) no longer sets the state, an unattended launch's layer turns off prompt suggestions, and a tool event that wakes a hook-confirmed `idle` session is logged by name. Until this date grinder-ao-1, idle after its `Stop` at 2026-09-27T05:56:04Z, was turned `working` by a hook four seconds later, read `stalled?` at 06:35Z and stayed there 13 hours with unread mail the doorbell would not ring. The event was not recorded; the suggestion Claude Code 2.1.283 generates right after a `Stop` is the suspect, and a background agent's tool events after its caller's `Stop` did the same until its notification started the next turn.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-185, grinder-ao-1): every session the host agent starts carries `AGENT_NAME`, its shown name. Until this date a session carried only `AGENTORC_SESSION`, the tmux id (`ao-agentorc-grinder-ao-1`), so dev-cadence's co-author hook (its TD-067, 2026-09-26) had no name to write; Paul asked on 2026-09-26 for the agent's name as a co-author, through the generic git hook rather than Claude Code's `attribution` setting, because the plan goes beyond Claude.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-200 (3), grinder-ao-1): `closed` is final. grinder-ao-2, closed by the anchor at 02:19:54Z on 2026-09-26, read EXITED with `closed_at` set: the tool's `SessionEnd` for the run `ao close` killed landed after the close and `_apply_event` set `exited`, which also kept the record past its day (the reconcile forgets only `closed`). `_apply_event` now ignores every hook on a closed record.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-204, grinder-ao-1): `send --wait` reads its baseline — busy or idle, and the transition counter — under the typing lock before the paste. Until this date it read them after the submit returned, and the tool's `UserPromptSubmit`, landing while the composer was still being checked, made an idle session look busy: the wait let this prompt's own turn end, then waited for a further one and reported `prompt-stalled` for a prompt that ran (the board item of 2026-09-12: two in eight sends). Found by reading `rpc_send`, not reproduced live; the test fires the hooks from inside a slowed `_type`. Accepted residual, from the review: an earlier typist's start that lands late, inside this paste, now reads as this prompt's, since no hook names the prompt that caused it.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-261, grinder-ao-1): `send --wait` follows the record it typed into, not whatever record holds its id. Until this date the wait looked the id up at every 0.1 s poll, so a record forgotten with its pane alive and adopted by the tick as a shell inside one poll was read as the same session: a wait with no timeout then never ended. Seen as `test_send_wait_three_outcomes` hanging to pytest's 120 s timeout on CI (run 36827137106), whose last case forgets a record that way with a 0.3 s tick running.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-283, grinder-ao-2; Paul's walk of the Repo page: *the "working" pill is probably misleading on an interactive session — it should be idle*): a launch with no prompt marks its pane `AGENTORC_AT_COMPOSER=1`, and its `SessionStart` `startup` reads `idle`. Until then `entry-1`, opened from Add entry and never sent a turn, read `working (hook)` with **→ Steer** in its composer.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-418, ao-paul with Paul: *using "IDLE" suggests to me a session is doing nothing*): **Waiting** — an `idle` session holding a claim whose PR is open, or with an open `ask` or `steer` to the person, is drawn *waiting*, read on the view and `idle` in every payload. A session in `ao wait` is mid-turn and reads `working`, so the third case first named in the review, *waiting on another session*, is read only as a held PR's ask — the mockup `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-10-08-org-declutter.html` (five rounds with Paul on 2026-10-08, the shots `2026-10-08-org-declutter-{before,after,after-open}.png` and their full-page pair, re-captured from the live page for round 4), built by TD-428.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-296 #6, grinder-ao-2): Claude Code's first-run screens — the text-style picker and *Select login method* — read `needs-you` by two screen rules (`first-run-theme`, `first-run-login`). Until this date a session launched on a config the tool had never used sat at them with no hook fired and read `working` from its birth, offering Steer; a live look of 2026-10-03 found it on an Add entry → **Open a session** on an empty config.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-306, grinder-ao-1; filed by grinder-ao-2 in the review of #1002): a screen rule's verdict on a hook-fed session lasts as long as its screen — once no rule matches and no hook is fresh, the record goes back to the state the verdict replaced (a hook's, or `stalled?`; a hook that sets the state drops what was kept), or to `idle` when no hook has reported. Until then a scraped `needs-you` (a `/theme` or `/login` screen dismissed on a quiet session) stayed until the tool's next hook.

## 4.2a Profiles: tool · account · model

- 2026-10-07 (TD-151, grinder-ao-1): built — `spend.sums` keeps the rows' `turns` and each window's `begins`, and `spend.reading` gives each window `turns` and `pace` (`{per_hour, unit, at}`); a pace that moves with the clock alone is kept current at the host agent and pushed with the next move of the spend.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-151, the designer): a metered reading's windows carry `turns` and `pace` beside `spent`.
- 2026-09-11 (TD-031): landed the model-in-use observation — the card's third part shows the model actually in use when the adapter can tell it, and `opus-5 (profile)` when only the declared one is known.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-122, the designer; Paul comparing the chip with the account's usage page): the reading made the account's — one poll per `(adapter, account)`, every profile sharing it carrying the same reading and back-off; reserves stay per profile. Found when four profiles split by role (TD-118) on one account turned one working poll into four refused ones.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-128, the designer; Paul 2026-09-24: *make a note to handle API (pay per token) budgets at some point*): `billing` on the profile — `subscription` or `metered` with optional prices — and a metered profile's spend summed per profile over day / week / month into a reading of the usage shape. Rejected: a team- or project-scoped budget (Paperclip's generic row), by §9 invariant 9. Steered to Paul. The build is TD-151.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-128 reconciled; a cloud session with Paul, after #547 merged while the session's park PR sat unmerged — memory *a park is a merged PR*): the sum made **per account**, not per profile — a key is an account, and two profiles on one key each summing their own would each spend the whole amount; prices per token kind, `cache_read` and `cache_write` beside `input` and `output`, an absent cache price charged at `input`; a metered profile never polled; a metered window never `limited`. Kept from #547: `billing` on the profile, the string units, day / week / month in the home's clock, no team or project scope.

- 2026-09-27 (TD-151 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): `billing` and `prices` on the profile, checked in `profiles.load` (an unknown billing, prices on a subscription, an unknown kind or a price below zero is the file's error), the label saying *metered*, and the Claude Code adapter answering a metered profile's usage poll with the reason `metered` without asking the endpoint. Until slice 3 the home keys on that reason as it does on any other — it keeps no window, so nothing is ever `limited` from it; the home's own read of `billing` before the cap rule comes with the summed reading.

## 4.3 Adapter contract

- 2026-10-08 (TD-425, grinder-ao-1): every launch sets `CADENCE_ATTENTION_SCOPE` beside `AGENTORC_SESSION` — `own` for an unattended session, `machine` for an interactive one, from the record's mode at that launch. Until then dev-cadence's start hook found the scope by running `ao status --json` itself, a synced file calling `ao` against §8's rule (TD-159's ADR); the script already read the variable first.
- 2026-09-13: the word *turn* was taken from OpenAI's Agents API (ADR decisions/2026-09-13-openai-agents-api.md) because it named a split agentorc already had and could not say in a sentence: session durable, turn one piece of work. Send to `idle` starts a turn, to `working` steers it — one control, not two, because the difference is what the person is doing, not what the code does.
- 2026-09-14 (§4.10): `mail()` added to the adapter protocol; when an adapter has no native delivery path the core falls back to a pane write (a `send`), and §4.10's message/control line becomes a convention the adapter's brief keeps rather than a gate the host agent enforces.
- The adapter status table was written "at design time", each row to be verified before its adapter is built; Claude Code and `shell` were phase 1.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-122): `label` added to the contract (the tool's display name, for the chip), and `usage()` said to be called once per account and to carry every window the endpoint reports, a per-model weekly one labelled with the model.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-122, the build): `account_for(profile)` added to the contract as an optional method — the core cannot read a profile, so the adapter says which account the poll keys on; without it the poll keys on the profile, as before.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-035, the designer): the 2026-09-12 decision with Paul — tool-specific wiring of a repo's start hooks belongs in the adapter, not in the repo — written into the contract as *A repo's start hooks are every adapter's to run*: through the tool's start hook, else the runner at launch with its stdout prepended to the first prompt; the anchor check's source for another tool decided with that adapter (TD-112). Nothing to build until a second adapter exists.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-128, the designer): `spend(profile)` — per-turn tokens and cost from the tool's own records for a metered profile; the prices are the profile's, never the core's.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-128 reconciled, with Paul): `spend(profile, cursors)` with four token kinds — cache reads folded into input would report a bill several times the real one — read from a byte cursor per transcript across the profile's config directory, sessions agentorc did not start included.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-154, the designer): `read_transcript` joined the contract beside `transcript_path` — the adapter renders its file into neutral entries and the core draws them, so a second adapter's transcript draws on the same page.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-188, the designer): `context` on the contract — the session's context size from the tool's records, Claude Code's from the last assistant entry's `usage`.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-190 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): `context()` is built as `{tokens, at, window}` keyed by the profile *name*, the shape `model_in_use` already had, where the design of 2026-09-26 wrote a `Context` type and a `Profile` argument: the core cannot build a `Profile` (it lives in `agentorc`), and every optional method the core calls takes the name. The windows are a table of model-id prefixes in the adapter: 1M for the Fable, Mythos, Opus 5 and 4.6–4.8 and Sonnet 5 and 4.6 families, 200k for Haiku 4.5, none for anything else.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-295, grinder-ao-2): `context()`'s window is the one Claude Code hands its status line (`context_window.context_window_size`), kept by `agentorc-hook --statusline` under the home's `statusline/context/<tool session>.json` and stamped by the redraw that first saw it; its `current_usage` tokens replace the transcript's when later. The model table is the fallback only, and its Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6 rows drop to 200k, since those reach 1M only through the `[1m]` suffix that `message.model` hides. The open question to Paul (*does Focus's 231k of 1M match `/context`?*) is answered by construction.

- 2026-09-27 (TD-151 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): Claude Code's `spend` built — every transcript under the profile's config directory read from its cursor to its last whole line (a line still being written waits), subagents' transcripts included, a cursor past the end read from 0. Two things the build decided: the result is `{turns, cursors, reason}` — the design's pair with the reason beside it, as `usage_for` carries one; and one API response is one turn, because Claude Code writes a response as one entry per content block, each carrying the response's usage — a read keeps the last entry per `message.id`, so a response whose entries straddle two reads would be counted twice, which slice 3's ledger is to guard against. From the review of #674: a metered profile must name a `config_dir` of its own, shared only within its account, or `profiles.yml` refuses it — reading the tool's default directory would bill a subscription's turns to the key.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-283 part 2, the designer): `start_context` on `launch` — text a session holds from its start that is no prompt. It came from Add entry's **Open a session**, which until then put `entry.md`'s lines in the composer in front of the person's words (Paul, 2026-10-02: *I am torn whether this is good for transparency or just in the way*). Three carriers were compared: the lines sent as an opening prompt (a turn runs before the person has spoken, and the fresh session reads `working`, which part 1 had just cured); the lines joined to the person's first Send by the page (they live only in one browser's draft, so a first message typed in the terminal or from another browser loses them); and the tool's own start context, chosen — Claude Code's `--append-system-prompt`, present in 2.1.289's `--help`. Not built.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-283 part 2 slice 1, grinder-ao-2): the carrier is built. `create` takes `start_context` and keeps it on the record and the launch record. It refuses one for an adapter without the flag (`shell`), and counts it under `ARG_LIMIT` as a prompt is counted. A resume that names none carries the conversation's own, so the one-press Resume needs no new field. The check named first held in print mode against 2.1.289: a conversation given the flag answered with its text, and answered again on a `--resume` given the flag once more. A compaction rewrites the messages, never the system prompt. The page (`/api/entry/session`, **Told at start**) is slice 2.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-283 part 2 slice 2, grinder-ao-2): the page is built. `/api/entry/session` hands `entry.md`'s filled lines to `create` as `start_context`, and answers the person's words alone as the composer's text. `entry.md`'s first line says *their first message is their words for it*, where it said *their words follow these lines*. Focus draws **Told at start** above Session, its heading the count of non-blank lines.

## 4.4 Host agent

- 2026-10-07 (TD-384 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): work orders built in the repo reading — `sessionorc/workorders.py` runs the repo's own `nudge_user_attention.py --report --json --fetch` (a plain read when the fetch fails) and keys each open decided line, `fyi` left out, `board:<key>` by the reader's own `item_key`, loaded from that file; `_read_repo` reads them with the PRs every `REPOS_EVERY` as `work_orders`, a failed read keeping the last with the error; `ao repo` lists them first among the pickable rows and counts them.
