The build is TD-368.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-357, the designer; Paul's read of ao-grind that evening — *24 pickable* while all three members were out of work, the 24 the anchor's and dev-cadence's — filed by the anchor): **In a team's lanes** — a derived reading beside the repo's kinds: what of the *pickable* and *design-first* entries a lane of the team's records takes (`lane_matches`, rule 6's rule), the rest of the *pickable* by owner, and a member out of work with unheld work in its own lane; one reader for the card, the Repo page and `ao repo`. The kinds stay the repo's. Build TD-361.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-361 slice 1, grinder-ao-2): *In a team's lanes*' reader built — `ledger.in_lanes(entries, records, held)`, `pickable` and `design_first` in the lanes, the `rest` by owner (`unowned` for an entry with none) in falling count then name, `design_first_rest`, and `out_of_work` by record; `teamrun.repo_lanes` runs it per team servicing a repo, over its records in the repo that were not resumed as another, with what a live record of the repo holds claimed. `ao repo` reads it; the card and the Repo page are slice 2.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-361 slice 2, grinder-ao-2): the lanes line drawn — on the team card under the kind bar's legend and on the Repo page's count line once per servicing team (`org.lanes_line`, `lanes_line.html`), the *pickable* and *design-first* segments' hovers in §4.5a's words, the out-of-work mark tinted with its ids on hover; a count whose kind has nothing open is plain text, not a link to a list the Repo page does not draw (the independent review's finding).
- 2026-09-26 (TD-176 slice 1, built): the repo facts' PR read became **two reads** — the open list, and every PR updated in the last month with a limit of 1000 — because this repo opens about forty PRs a day and the designed `--limit 200` page covered five days of a month's window. The windows became **rolling** (the last day, 7 and 30 days) where the design had said *in the home's clock*: a calendar week read near zero every Monday morning, which is not the balance question the counts answer. The history is read with `--first-parent`, so a merged branch's own commits never open and close an entry twice.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-223 and TD-198, the designer; Paul the same day: *should we consider retiring "pickable" and using ledger.py to determine it based on "blocked by" instead?*): **pickable became derived** in the ledger reading — cadence §2.4's rule, the one dev-cadence's `scripts/ledger.py` applies — and the written `**Pickable:**` line (TD-118) retires. It had been read as written, so dev-cadence's ledger, which writes no header lines, read `pickable: ''` throughout: rule 6 matched nothing there, and grinder-dc-1 sat idle beside a pickable TD-070. The reader is agentorc's own and a test holds it equal to the repo's script: running a repo's script from the home, the entry's option (a), was turned down because the home would run a repo's code on every ledger change and a repo without the script would read nothing. The page's kinds are tested *for you*, *design-first*, *pickable*, *other*: *pickable* had won and counted 7 designer entries among 19 (TD-198). From the fact-check, run on the ledger of that day: removing the lines as first written would have turned about thirty entries pickable, the anchor's among them, so the migration gives each written *no* a `Kind:`, an `Owner:` or a `Blocked by:` first and shows each lane's matches unchanged. Build: TD-228.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-176, with Paul): **Repo facts** widened — PRs read with `--state all` and counted *opened* / *closed* per window; the ledger's *opened* / *closed* per window from the file's git history; the four kinds (for you = `Owner: paul` or `Kind: decision`); the phases derived on the view; and the **doing log**, the last fifty `doing` calls per team, `doing.jsonl`, the `doing` RPC and event.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-176, in a cloud session with Paul): **Repo facts** — open PRs by `gh pr list --state open` every five minutes per remote and the ledger's entry headers by a `sessionorc.ledger` reader, kept in `repos.json` and pushed as a `repos` event; an outage keeps the last reading and says so, the seat counter's rule. Board counts stay with the board reader the Inbox already runs.
- Until 2026-09-20 the usage poll ran every minute; TD-087 moved it to five minutes because a minute buys nothing against a five-hour window while spending an allowance the tool itself shares. The stated cost: a session at its cap shows `limited` up to five minutes later rather than up to one.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-087): usage fetch failures gained a reason. Before, *rate-limited*, *no credentials*, *no network* and *no profile* were one silence, and the silence cost more than a missing chip: `limited` is read from the same reading, so a session at its cap was not marked while the endpoint refused us. The `Retry-After` from a 429 is honoured uncapped because "a server saying *an hour and a half* knows something our ceiling is guessing at"; our own doubling stops at an hour.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-087): the usage reading moved to `usage.json`. It had lived in memory, so each promote forgot it and polled at once — eight times in one day. The first poll after a restart now waits until the held `fetched` plus the cadence.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-231 and TD-230, the designer; Paul: *our percentage used for the claude sub seems to be stale — our page reads 88%/95% but actual is 93% used*, *add how long ago the reading was made in the display*, *investigate the 429s*): usage became **reported first and asked for last**. Until then the one source was a poll of the tool's undocumented usage endpoint, every five minutes per account, and on 2026-09-28 it was refused for six hours: the held reading stood at 88% while the account was at 93%, the chip said *stale* and not how stale, and the gate, reading the same number against a line of 95%, could see no crossing. The anchor's research that day found no documented way to read a subscription's limits but the status line's `rate_limits`, and found the 429s widely reported with no usable `Retry-After`. So a session's status line reports what it is told, the endpoint is asked only for an account with no reading under fifteen minutes old and left alone an hour after a refusal, every reading carries its age, and the gate projects a reading older than `max_age` from the rate it last saw, pausing on the projection and resuming only on a reading. From the fact-check, which read the tool's documentation of the status line: a redraw repeats the last numbers, so reports are merged by what a window can do and a report's age moves only when the session has had a response; a pause on a projection must be able to end without a report, since paused sessions send none; `max_age` is a key of its own, `usage:`, because every key under `usage_gate` is a profile's name. Turned down: the rate-limit headers on model responses (undocumented), and asking the person each time a reading goes old (a night's question nobody is awake for). Build: TD-233.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-233 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): the usage endpoint became the fallback it was designed as. Until this date the host agent asked each account every five minutes and, after a 429, waited the endpoint's `Retry-After` floored at five minutes or doubled to an hour; on 2026-09-28 the endpoint refused for six hours with `Retry-After` 0 or none, so the doubling learnt nothing. Now an account is asked only when its reading is older than `USAGE_FRESH` (fifteen minutes) and at most once per `USAGE_FRESH`, and a 429 cools it off for `USAGE_COOL`, a fixed hour, a longer `Retry-After` kept; its end rides on the reading as `cool_until`, since the review found that a promote inside the hour would otherwise ask at once from the held reading's old `fetched`. `USAGE_EVERY` and `USAGE_BACKOFF_MAX` went. Until the report (slice 2) exists this is a fifteen-minute poll; the hourly ask for a window only the endpoint gives waits for the report, since until then every window comes from the same ask. Already true and not changed: each host asks for its own sessions' profiles, and `limited` skips a window past its reset.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-233 slice 2, the host agent's half, grinder-ao-1): the `usage_report` RPC, `sessionorc.usage` (the merge, the history, the hourly ask for a window only the endpoint gives) and each window's own `at`, `source` and `history` in `usage.json`. Two choices the design left open, written into §4.4 *The reading*: the endpoint's answer sets the windows it names outright, since it is the account's own number and a stale high report must not outlive it — the merge by the highest applies to reports; and two resets within five minutes are one window, since the status line's epoch seconds and the endpoint's instant never quite agree and a rolling window drifts. The caller of the RPC is the session the envelope names, so the identity check covers it as any other method. Not in this half: the status-line command and the adapter's `usage_report` (agentorc's), and a node's report to the home.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-233 slice 2, the adapter's half, grinder-ao-1): `agentorc-hook --statusline` and the launch layer's `statusLine`, with a refresh of sixty seconds. The field names were read from the installed client (2.1.285) rather than the documentation alone: `rate_limits.five_hour` and `.seven_day`, each `used_percentage` and `resets_at` in epoch seconds, and `cost.total_api_duration_ms`, the session's running total of API time, which is what the command reads for `fresh`; §4.3's `usage_report` is therefore a plain function returning that total and the tool's session id beside the windows, and the command, which holds the last-sent file, sets `fresh` from it — a new process under the same name counts from nothing, so a restart is fresh. The displaced line's `padding` has to be the layer's, since the tool draws with the layer's, so it is read at launch and the layer file gains a `+pad<n>` variant. A status line of ours named in a person's settings is passed over rather than chained, or the command would run itself. `limited` is read from the account's reading, so §4.4 *The poll, as built* now says *while nothing reports* of its fifteen minutes (the techlead's read of #770). Not built: a node's report to the home.
- 2026-09-30 (TD-233 slice 2, a node's report, grinder-ao-1): a node sends each report its sessions make on to the home as the link method `usage_report {id, account, windows, fresh}`, off the RPC's path and dropped with no link. The design left open whose account key the home uses; the node's, as `spend` already does, because the node holds the profile's credentials and a profile of one name may be another login at the home — keying it at the home would have let a laptop's `default` raise kmaster's account, and gains no protection, since the node names its own records' profiles anyway. So the home takes the key only in the record's adapter's namespace and for a record of that link's host, and a profile a home session runs under keeps the home's account, as a reading is per profile. The home shows the account while the node's session lives and never asks the endpoint for it. Not built: the home's send of the reading back to its nodes.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-233 slice 2, the home's send, grinder-ao-1): the home sends each account's reading back to the nodes that keyed a live session to it, as the link method `usage_reading {account, reading}`, on a change and once per new link. The design said only *as it sends the metered sums*; the sums travel as sums because a metered `pct` is the profile's, but a subscription reading is the account's, so the reading itself travels. How the node takes it was left open: not outright, since a report of its own may land between the home's merge and the send, and not as a report, since the home's reading carries the endpoint's answer, which may lower a number — so window by window, the newer confirmation or the later reset winning, with the node's own `reason` and cool-off kept because only the node asks for its account. Two gaps the techlead's read of #783 found were closed with it: a node's profile named metered at the home had its reports dropped, and after a home restart a node's account started clean, its history lost; the latter needed the passes before the node's first report to keep that profile's held reading, which they had dropped as no longer shown. A node's account the home does not show (its profile a home session's, or a second node's key) is kept while its session lives, since it is still sent back.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-066): one oversize `list` reply took every `ao` on the machine down at once — asyncio's 64 KiB default frame was smaller than a day's records, and the raise happened in the reader, which could say nothing about the cause. `FRAME_LIMIT` (8 MiB) on every stream, and the agent refuses to write a longer line, followed.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-062): version skew became a designed property. Until then the install was editable, so a merged RPC change was live at once (one broke `ao progress` for every worker for 31 minutes); once promoted, a merged change reaches every session's `ao` before the running host agent knows it. Drop-unset-parameters on the client and drop-unknown-parameters (`ignored: [...]`) on the agent closed the window for existing methods; new methods or changed meanings still need the promotion. The `ignored` line is accumulated across every call a command makes "since the call that skews is rarely the last one"; a caller bug against a same-age agent looks identical from the client, so the agent's log line (naming the method) tells the two apart.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-063): `client.CALL_TIMEOUT` (120 s) added. The client had no bound on reading a reply, so an RPC that never returned blocked for ever: a CI job died after fourteen minutes naming the test *before* the one that stopped, and — the half that mattered — a live worker sat in `ao send` that never returned while its manager read it as `working`. The bound was applied one layer down from `_route_act`, where the reply is read; `AgentStuck` (a subclass) lets the one reconnecting caller distinguish a timeout (wedged agent, do not remake) from a drop (restart, remake).
- 2026-09-21 (TD-062 (c)): `built_from` / `started_at` added to `host`. A promote installed a wheel that was a flat `0.0.1` whatever it held, so nothing could say `main` had moved past the running copy. `pdm_build.py` now writes `sessionorc/_build.json`; `ao status -v` and `ao service status` print the commit and how far `origin/main` is ahead. An agent from before this reports *unknown*.
- Board write-back (Snooze / Done on `user_attention.md`) was proposed upstream as dev-cadence PR #83 (the carve-out from cadence §4's branch → PR rule) and depends on `nudge_user_attention.py --report --json` from dev-cadence PR #82. Not built in agentorc.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-069 step 3, grinder-ao-1): the write-back's RPC was built, `board_edit`. Until this date §4.4 said only that the host agent commits in *the main checkout*, and was silent on a checkout that cannot take the commit. The build refuses on four conditions: another branch checked out (the anchor works on branches in its checkout, so a commit there would land on someone's feature branch and ride its PR), a board with uncommitted changes, a git operation under way, and a line that no longer holds the item the person was shown. The Inbox's controls wait on the rows (draft PR #472), which the techlead held until this half existed (2026-09-23).
- 2026-09-23 (TD-122): the usage poll keyed on the account — once per `(adapter, account)` through one profile, cached per account, served under every profile sharing it, backed off per account, dropped when no live session's profile names it.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-120 step 2, the designer): the build line named as this repo's instance of the general `check` — one of the promote's three readings (§5 `promote:`, §6 *Promote*).
- 2026-09-24 (TD-069 step 4, the designer): the board write-back gains one **add** — *Put on the board* on an FYI row — the only line this system adds to a board, at a person's press; the rule *edits a pressed item and adds none* became *acts at a person's press and on nobody's behalf*.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-126, in a cloud session with Paul; Paul's shape decided that morning): the board write-back gained **Reply** — the person's words appended to the item's own line as ` — Paul, <date>: …`, one commit, and mailed as well only to a live session holding the lease on the line's reference, as a `handed` note. Paul's decision said *appended under the line*; the session found that dev-cadence's reader and the SessionStart hook match one `- [ ]` line each (`ITEM_RE`), so a second line under an item would be read by nothing, and the reply goes on the line itself. The item's `session`, `host` and `refs` were asked of the reader's JSON rather than parsed here (§4.4: never a second parser); the file writer is agentorc's own `sessionorc.board`, not the reader, which corrected the entry's point (c). The three standings — *still on*, *moved on*, *gone* — key on the lease first, so a reply reaches whoever holds the work even when the line's author is gone. Compared 2026-09-25 with the designer's PR #531, written the same afternoon before Paul's 12:30 standing decision: it puts the reply under the item and asks the reader to join continuation lines, mails a plain `note` with no outcome owed, and has no standing; the comparison also found that the SessionStart hook clips a due line at 200 characters (`MAX_ITEM_CHARS`), so on the line or under it the reader must print the reply for the hook to carry it — added to the ask on the board.

- 2026-09-25 (TD-128 reconciled, with Paul): a metered account's reading is a sum, not a poll — #547 said the home sums and not where the sum lives; decided: a daily ledger per account, `spend.json` beside `usage.json`, the cursors per host per transcript moving after the row is written, a rewritten transcript read from 0 with its ledgered turns dropped by `at` and, on a shared timestamp, by the entry's own id, kept thirteen months; a node's turns home as the `spend` link method with a reply, the reading back as a `usage` notification, the node's gate adding its own turns offline. The build is TD-151.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-140 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the board write-back's **add** built — `board.add` and `board_edit` with `action: add`: one line in the board's format above the first item (under *Needs the user* on a board with none), committed as `agentorc: board <head> (from <entry>)`, the entry dismissed after the commit lands; the Inbox's button is slice 2.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-142 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the write-back's **Reply** built, file half only — `board.write_back(..., "reply", reply=…)` and `rpc_board_reply`: the words appended to the item's line and committed, `sent` empty and a `note` saying no standing is known yet. Two calls the design left open: the name signed is the checkout's git `user.name` (the design wrote *Paul*, and no setting holds a person's name), and a reply typed over several lines is joined onto the one line rather than refused. `board_edit` refuses `action: reply`, which is `board_reply`'s.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-151 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): a metered account's ledger and summed reading built at the home — `sessionorc.spend` (`spend.json`, the three windows in the home's clock, `pct` from the profile's amount) and the tick's `SpendMixin` pass; the gate reads a metered profile's amounts as a line at the amount less a team's priority, the poll and the cap rule skip it by its billing, the note at eight tenths is filed once per window, `ao status -v` prints a `spend:` line. Three choices the design left open, written into §4.3 and §4.4: the adapter's `billing_for(profile)`, since the core cannot read a profile; the turn carries its `response` and the cursor its tokens, so a response straddling two reads counts what it adds (the techlead's (1) and first limit on #674); a batch read from the cursor drops entries before the last ledgered `at` (the second limit). The techlead's (2) checked on kmaster's own transcripts: `claude --resume` appends to the transcript it resumes, so the test stays per transcript. A node's turns (slice 4) and `ao gate`'s amounts (slice 5) not yet.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-151 slice 4, grinder-ao-1): a node's metered turns reach the home's ledger — the node runs the spend pass over its own sessions and sends `spend {account, profile, prices, turns, cursors}`; the home ingests under that host's cursors and answers with them, the seeding and the account's sums; `usage {account, sums}` is pushed when a named profile's `pct` moves a point or a window rolls; offline the node's figure is the held sums plus its own unacknowledged turns. Written into §4.4 and §4.4a from the build: the frame carries the account's sums, not a reading (`pct` is the profile's); the node's first `spend` on each link is a read, and the reply to it is what the design had as the push on `hello`; the seeding travels as cursors and a long batch in pieces cut at a line; the frame carries the node's prices, since the home may not define the profile; each host's top bar shows its own sessions' accounts; a metered profile whose only live session is command-kind is not read while that is so (the techlead's read of #681).
- 2026-09-28 (TD-151 slice 5, grinder-ao-1): a metered profile's amounts through `set_settings`, `ao gate` and the Settings page's metered card, and the metered chip. Written into §4.7 from the build: the billing is asked of every adapter when no session runs under the profile, so an amount can be set before the first session; money on a profile with no prices is refused (it would make no line); `gate` answers the amounts as written. The chip's hover lists tokens by kind per window; the turns' rate on hover is not built.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-151, grinder-ao-1; the techlead's read of #689): a metered profile only a node defines reads as metered at the home, from the node's `spend` calls and the prices they carry, so `ao gate` takes its amount; it has no card on the Settings page, which draws the home's own `profiles.yml`. Until then the home's adapters alone decided, and such a profile's amount was refused as a subscription's.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-151, grinder-ao-2): the metered chip's hover names each profile's own amount per window — `usage_accounts` keeps a profile's `amounts` beside its lines, and `_usage_profiles` / `usageProfiles` write *api [day amount $5, week amount 2M tok]*. The turns' rate is still not on the hover: the design names it and does not say what it is a rate of (turns an hour, or over the window), and `spend.sums` carries no turn count.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-213, the designer): the board write-back gains a **second add**, the person's answer to an orphaned question (§4.10). Until this date *Put on the board* was the only line this system added to a board. Both are written at a person's press.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-208, the designer): a row the page read from origin offers no edit. The round first had the write-back fast-forward the checkout (`--ff-only`) and steered Paul on it; the fact-check set it against *the tree is a person's* (§6 *Promote*) and the reader's board-level *behind*, and the design was turned to read-only rows, the fast-forward going to TD-222 as the person's decision.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-255 slice 1, grinder-ao-2): the write-back's **Decide** built — `decide` in `board.ACTIONS`, `edit_line` and `message` making `board_edit.py decide`'s edit and commit subject, refused on a line already decided, and `rpc_board_edit` taking `answer` with the item's `answers`. Met in the build: a Reply on a line that carries `Answers:` was appended after it, and dev-cadence's reader then read the reply as part of the last answer (*Lift it. — Paul, 2026-10-02: say more*), so the answer buttons would have offered it. The design had the reply go ahead of `Decided:` only; it now goes ahead of both fields, and the test reads the line back with the reader itself.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-222, the designer; Paul's decision of the same day on the pull, §6): the board write-back moves off the person's checkout — the edit is committed on origin's head in a detached tree of the host agent's own (`~/.agentorc/boards/<repo>/tree`) and pushed under `ALLOW_MAIN_PUSH=1`, and the checkout catches up by the pull. Until this date the edit was committed in the main checkout and never pushed (TD-069's shape, cadence §4.5's words: *the anchor's next push carries it*), so every write-back left the checkout ahead of origin until the anchor pushed, the next merged board change made the two differ both ways, and a row read from origin could not be acted on (TD-208). The refusals for the checkout's state (another branch, a dirty board, a rebase under way) go with it; *origin unreachable* and *the line is not on origin* come; the item is found on origin's head by its text, the line number a hint. Steered to Paul as the default over (a) the host agent pushing the checkout's own commit and (b) leaving it to the anchor's push; Paul's reply the same day: *go with the default*. The cadence §4.5 sentence is dev-cadence's to change (the board). **The same night** (the anchor's finding on #892): the push itself cannot land — the repo's ruleset (*PR required*, cadence §4.7) refuses a direct push to `main` from anyone, and `ALLOW_MAIN_PUSH=1` passes only the pre-push hook; the anchor's push of Paul's two steer replies was *declined due to repository rule violations* and rode PR #902. The road became a branch of the host agent's own and a pull request it opens and squash-merges at once through the forge's API, the same on a repo with no rule. The alternative, a bypass on the ruleset for the pushing identity, would let every session in the checkout push `main` under the override — the backstop the ruleset is — and went to Paul as the steer's alternative, not its default. Built by TD-264.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-262, the designer; Paul: *I restarted the dc team but it says it was closed by me*): the record gains `closer` — who asked for the close, read from the call's envelope as the acting gate reads it, or the tick's rule — beside `closed_for`, which stays the tick's restart mark. Until this date `rpc_close` wrote `closed_at` and took no caller, so a person's Close, a manager's `ao team stop --close` and the tick's closes were one fact on the record. Built by TD-265.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-265 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): `closer` built. `rpc_close` takes the envelope's `caller` and a `closer`, which is honoured only from a call with no session caller (the tick's own, or the home's over the link), so a session cannot write *closed by the tick* or another's name. Home-owned as §4.4a lists it: `_route_act` writes the home's copy of a node's record as it routes the close, and hands the word to the node in the params. A person's Close made at the node itself never crosses as an act, so the home's copy keeps no closer for it and reads *closed*. The words that draw it (the card, `ao status -v`, the team card, the Start's lane line) are TD-265's rest.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-298, grinder-ao-1): a host agent on a scratch home keeps to its own tmux server and its own repos. A look agent's `agentorc-agent serve` on `AGENTORC_HOME=~/.cache/aolkE` had filled its registry from dev-cadence's machine roster and fast-forwarded the real agentorc checkout four seconds after it started; and `serve` built a bare `Tmux()`, so `AGENTORC_TMUX_SOCKET` kept nothing apart. `serve` now honours the variable, and an unset `repos_registry` reads `repos.txt` under any home but `~/.agentorc` (§5).
- 2026-10-04 (TD-142 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the write-back's **Reply** gained its mail half at the host agent — after the commit, `board_reply` sends each live record with a declared lease on one of the line's `refs` one `note` from the person, `about` that ref and marked `handed`, its text `board: <head>` and the signed reply; a holder of two refs is mailed once; a refused send is said in the result beside the committed line. The reader's `PR #1020` is matched as the claim's `#1020`. The techlead's read found the node case: `board_reply` is served where the repos registry holds the repo, so at a node the mail would have been written to the node's replica; the mail half became `board_reply_hand`, a home edit (`modes.HOME_EDITS`) the node forwards. The page's half — the row handing the reader's `refs` to the press, and the standing — is left to the next slice.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-264, grinder-ao-1): the board write-back built on origin's head — `~/.agentorc/boards/<repo>/tree`, reset to `origin/<default>` at every press, the item found there by its text (the reader's line a hint), the commit pushed on `board/<repo>/<stamp>` and landed by a PR the host agent opens and squash-merges, all inside forty seconds; the checkout is never written, so its branch, a dirty board or a rebase refuses nothing. Three calls the design left open: the PR's branch is deleted by a push rather than `gh pr merge --delete-branch`, which acts on a local branch the tree does not have; a tree whose git common directory is not the checkout's (another clone of the same name) is remade; and *not on origin yet* is said only when the checkout's board is ahead (uncommitted, or a board commit origin lacks) — a checkout merely behind still shows a line origin has since done, which is a moved line. On the page the rows read from origin are no longer drawn disabled and the read after a press fetches; a stopped fetch drawing the line from the write-back's result is left.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-418, ao-paul with Paul): the page's kinds become seven — *for you · blocked · design · live check · pickable · evaluation · other*, tested in that order (Paul's review of the design: *blocked* whatever the Kind, so TD-077, an anchor decision on TD-075, and TD-052, a live check on TD-112, are *blocked*), and *other* is a residue that should be empty, an entry in it a flag of the reader. Round 1 of the review fixed *blocked* to the code: `Blocked by:` holding anything but a `decision (…)`, whatever stands beside it; `decision (anchor)` alone and an unblocked anchor `Kind: decision` are *evaluation*, the anchor's call. The member's own count follows rule 6 (`lane_matches`) whatever the page kind, the bar the page kinds: they differ where a lane takes what the team's sum leaves out — the designer's `decision (designer)` beside an open id, the `anchor` lane's evaluations and `decision (anchor)` beside an open id, a bare `free-pick` lane's `Owner: paul` build (round 2: the anchor seat's own count that evening was 26, not the mockup's 14). On 2026-10-08 the four-kind bar read *10 design-first* while the designer's lane held none: all ten were designed entries waiting on their builds, eight of them builds that were themselves merged live checks (TD-426), the other two builds not yet done, and *other* hid 13 evaluations and the live checks not yet live. A member's own count, *n/k*, joins the lanes reader; the team's sum had read *18 pickable* on the grinders' card when most was the anchor seat's lane — the mockup `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-10-08-org-declutter.html` (five rounds with Paul on 2026-10-08, the shots `2026-10-08-org-declutter-{before,after,after-open}.png` and their full-page pair, re-captured from the live page for round 4), built by TD-428. Reviewed in four independent Sonnet rounds the same evening (six findings, then five, two, and three wording follow-ons, all adopted); the author closed the loop after round 4, since rounds 3 and 4 each found only a consistency follow-on of the round before, as TD-103's did.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-264 (d), grinder-ao-1; the techlead's read of #1036): a `gh pr merge` that fails is read first by the forge's word on the PR (`gh pr view --json state`), and only when that cannot be had by comparing origin's board with the edit's. #1036 compared the boards alone, so a merge the forge took while origin's board changed elsewhere in the same moment read as not landed: a Done refused as a moved line and an add as unreachable, though each had landed, and a retried add would have written its line twice.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-264 (c), grinder-ao-1; from the review of #1036): a squash the forge refuses while origin moved under it and the item still holds — a neighbouring line's change — was said as *origin could not be reached* though a retry would pass, against this section's *origin moving in between refuses nothing unless the board line itself moved*; it is now made once more on origin's new head inside the same bound (`board._Neighbour`, `_twice`) — only on the forge's own word that the PR did not merge (`_pr_state`, which `_pr_merged` became) and a change to origin's board, so a forge that cannot be asked never doubles an add (the review of #1096). The tree's commit takes the checkout's resolved `user.name` and `user.email` as `-c` options (`_identity`), so an `includeIf "gitdir:…"` identity that does not reach `~/.agentorc/boards/<repo>/tree` still authors it. The repo's hooks still run in the tree: this repo's (a pre-push that guards the default branch) passes a `board/…` branch, and a hook that refuses a detached HEAD stays the first live press's to see.

## 4.4a Home and nodes: one session graph across hosts (first half)

- 2026-09-16: section written. Phase 2 as first planned connected hosts hub-and-spoke (the UI host kept one ssh link to each host agent; host agents never talked to each other); that served a person and not a session acting across hosts. Mail was the first feature to need a cross-host session graph — Paul: *"we need to solve the cross-machine design now or the comms between agents are left funky"* (TD-057).
- 2026-09-16 (Paul): one host agent (kmaster) is home, the others are nodes; an agent whose `hosts.yml` names no `home:` is its own home, "which is phase 1 exactly".
- 2026-09-16 (Paul): *"won't the inability to reach home mean the UI will not render?"* — it would have; hence the offline fallback of `ao` / `ao ui` on a node to that node's own sessions.
- 2026-09-16 (Paul): the alternative offered — the laptop acting as its own home while offline — was rejected as the split-brain failure that rules out a mesh. Paul also ruled the hosted relay itself out of scope; the link is written as the protocol a relay would speak.
- 2026-09-16 (third review): field ownership by owner, never last write; a request's identity comes from the channel it arrived on, never from a field (the home ignores any host a client sends).
- 2026-09-16 (Sonnet review, narrowed by the third review): policies that stop run on the node, policies that start run at the home; the two accepted partition harms (extend does not resurrect; shorten is not seen until reconnect) were stated then.
- 2026-09-16: "When a host cannot reach home" written (Paul); spooling with the verdict returned later named as a possible later slice.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-057 step 2): the offline table written, "because step 2 builds the node before step 3 builds the link, so until then a node is *always* out of reach of its home and this table is its whole behaviour". Step 2 built and tested `models.apply_home` / `models.apply_node`; step 4 is what calls them across the link. The two decisions the rule did not make — reports refused rather than kept locally, and a person's mail refused too — were made here.
- 2026-09-17 (review of PR #198): three home-owned fields (`sends`, `seen_at`, `wake_refilled_at`) are merged, not overlaid, in both directions — an overlay "would erase the offline half and say it had worked".
- 2026-09-17 (TD-057 step 3a): the link's protocol decided "so both ends are written from one text": the forced-command bridge, `nodes:` in `hosts.yml`, frames with `re`, `FRAME_LIMIT`, `hello`/`ping`, `LINK_PING`, `LINK_SILENCE`, backoff 1 s → 60 s with jitter, the three diagnoses. The section was headed "What step 3a sends".
- 2026-09-17 (TD-057 step 4a): teams across hosts built — a team lands on one host (`host:` or the host the start runs on), the `host_dir` RPC (a `stat` link method) checks each checkout once before any name check, `ao team stop` degrades per member.
- 2026-09-18 (TD-057 step 5): mail across hosts built — a node forwards (`forward {rpc, params, caller, token}`), the home answers as `id@node`, replies re-addressed by `naming.readdress`; the doorbell is the forwarded `wait`, cancelled by token. From step 5 the offline table describes a node with its link down; with it up every refused cell is forwarded.
- Step 4b.1 (TD-057): permission prompts across the link built — a person's answer is `decide` routed as an act; the mark is `host_unreachable` on the view's `pending`, an overlay like `unreachable`; `decide` on a dropped link is refused like any act.
- Step 4b.2 (TD-057): decided there is no spool of hook events — on reconnect the node sends a full snapshot and that is the whole of it, since every consequence of a hook the home reads is a node-owned field and nothing at the home consumes an event. A live home record that disagrees on identity with a report is refused and logged. A node derives no reports while its link is down. Since 4b.2 a read the node serves alone carries the unread line from the count the home pushes with each record's intent.
- Step 4b.3 (TD-057): decided, and not built, that a node does not show the org (its `list`, `get` and UI stay this host's sessions with the link up too) — showing it would be a read across the boundary a person's forwarded request is held to (*Addresses*, PR #219). Built: the nightly backup tarball `backups/store-<date>.tar.gz` (first tick of each local date, off the loop, mode `0600`, temp name then rename, newest seven kept, a failure is a log line and tomorrow's retry); the `host_files {host, dir, paths}` RPC over the `files` link method with its `FILES_MAX` (16) / `FILE_CAP` (256 KiB) bounds and `modes.HOME_ONLY`.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-077; decided by Paul as the condition for TD-075's trial): `person: false` on a node's `nodes:` entry. Until this date a request arriving over a link with no `caller` was the person everywhere — designed, reviewed (#217, #219) and tested (`test_a_persons_forwarded_act_reaches_only_the_nodes_own_records`), right for a laptop and wrong for an agents-only container. A `nodes:` entry may carry `person:` from this date; an entry with neither `person:` nor `container:` keeps the behaviour until then (`person: true`).
- 2026-09-19: `{socket: <path>}` as a `link:` form (a container node's per-node socket at the home) noted as not yet built.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-052 step 7): a hook-confirmed idle is rung on the host whose agent holds the mailbox; a node, holding no inbox, rings nothing; across the link the ring will be that. The same step gave the node ownership of `wrapup_at` and `doorbell_failed`.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-057): a supersession on a node is done again at the home — the `supersedes` field `{id, mail, at}`, a same-id replacement taken whole except `suspended`, `superseded_by` for a record continued under another id.
- TD-066: the writing end refuses an oversize frame rather than leaving it to be discovered at the reading end.
- TD-100: `gated` added to the node-owned fields; `set_settings` row added to the offline table (served for a person on the node, refused for a session).
- 2026-09-25 (TD-100 (4), in a cloud session with Paul; the ADR *settings audit*): `settings.yml` became **home-owned and replicated** — the `settings` link method, a whole-file notification to every linked node after each write and on `hello`; the offline table's `set_settings` row went from *served, link or no link* to *forwarded / refused*, the node's gate reading its replica. Four Sonnet rounds forced it: the first found that a reserve set on the home would never reach a node's sessions because each host's gate read its own file.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-154, the designer): `NODE_READS` grew from two to three — `transcript` reads the file the node's tool wrote, through `read`, as `tail` reads its pane; the file is never copied to the home.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-293, the designer; found by grinder-ao-2 in the review of #949): *The New session form on another host* — the form's readings are the picked host's, by the team start's three reads (`host_repos`, `host_dir`, `host_files`) and two new ones (`host_occupancy`, `host_worktrees`); the role is parsed at the home from the node's files, not resolved at the node. Not built (TD-294).
- 2026-10-03 (TD-294 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the reads built — the link methods `occupancy` and `worktrees`, `root` on `stat`'s reply (and on `host_dir`'s for this host), the home's `host_occupancy` and `host_worktrees` (a container node's place read here too), both in `modes.HOME_ONLY`; the page and Start still read this host's disk.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-294 slice 3, grinder-ao-2): Start on another host built — the role, its brief, the ledger and the team's reader are resolved from the picked host's `.agentorc.yml` and brief files (`host_dir`'s `root`, `host_files` through the team start's `teams.reader_on`), and a Project block names the repos on that host. Until then a role started on another host carried this host's brief and ledger.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-300, grinder-ao-1; the techlead's read of #993): *A look's screenshots on another host* — `host_shot` placed beside the other host-level reads: ungated, answered by a node for its own registry only (not forwarded, and not in `modes.HOME_ONLY`), not among `identity.READS`; `exists` false past the 4 MiB bound, so the row draws such a name alone.

## §4.4a (second half) — A container node; A node's records at the home; Acts across the link

- 2026-09-13 (§10): a devcontainer that runs an `agentorc-agent` beside a tmux server was ruled a host.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-057 step 3b): "A node's records at the home" designed and built — snapshot, then `report`/`gone`; `REPORT_EVERY` and `REPORT_WRITE` at 5 s; adoption of unseen records; `remote/<host>/`; the reachability overlay. The first thing the link carried.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-057 step 3c, after two Fable reviews and Paul's steer to the long-term shape): the container node designed — the home brings it up, installs the agent at its own version, keeps it running. The link socket, `ao host up`, the supervisor, occupancy and reach built the same day as 3c.1–3c.5.
- 2026-09-17: the reason the repo's `mounts` are dropped was contractmatch's own mount, which carries `.netrc`, a kubeconfig and a Modal token — the person's whole credential set. The tmpfs-at-same-target rule came from contractmatch's `postCreate.sh`, which creates a directory under such a mount under `set -e` and would have died on a missing path.
- 2026-09-17 (Paul): "the home installs the client, so it installs tmux" — hence the generated tmux layer rather than an edit to the project's Dockerfile.
- 2026-09-17: the tmux layer's first shape was a local devcontainer *feature* beside the generated file. Built, then refused by the devcontainer CLI: a local feature must sit under the workspace's `.devcontainer/`, which is the repo's, so it would have written into the person's checkout — the very thing the feature was to avoid. Replaced by two generated definitions (`base.json` tagged `agentorc-node-<name>-base`, then a one-stage Dockerfile `FROM` that base).
- 2026-09-17 (3c.4): cross-container occupancy built — `occupants()` at the home reads every container node's records over the directory while its link is up; a create routed with `--host` is checked at the home first, then at the node. `volatile: true` semantics for a person-stopped container (supervisor never starts the container, still starts the agent inside a running one) built as part of 3c.4.
- 2026-09-17 (3c.5): Reach built — `host_link.reach` from one look at docker, `docker exec … tmux attach` for the Focus terminal and `ao focus`, the `attached-container+` VS Code link.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-062): the promote gained one step, writing the wheel it installed to `~/.agentorc/wheels/`, from which a container node is provisioned. The image was required to supply Python 3.12+ because a feature cannot put an interpreter in every image the same way.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-057 step 4a): "Acts across the link" designed and built — the `act` link method, address rewriting in the reader's form, the gate reading one graph, home-owned edits applied only once the node has taken them in the same call, `create` with a `host`.
- 2026-09-18 (found live): a node provisioned before steps 4a, 5 and 4b stayed linked through five promotes, answering *unknown link method* to everything newer, because a promote changes no protocol number. Fix: a build is named by its wheel's content, said in `hello`, checked at `hello` and on every tick; a behind node is marked `stale` and re-provisioned and restarted by the supervisor, link up or not, after one grace (because a promote writes the wheel a second before it restarts the home, and a process about to be stopped must not start an install it cannot finish).
- Step 4 (org): `host:` on a team definition was a change to `org.yml`'s schema and `teams.plan`, so that `ao team start cm-grind` from kmaster lands the manager and the grinder in the container and the host limits the briefs encoded fell away.
- Step 4b.1: `ao tail` and `ao explain` on a remote record read its pane on its node via the `read` link method (`NODE_READS`); before this they were refused by name as not built.
- Step 4b.2: the general `intent` push (home → node) and `derived` reports (node → home) added. The per-call push in home-owned edits was kept beside it, because it is what makes an edit on an unreachable host a refusal rather than a divergence.
- 2026-09-19: `identity_ack` added to the `act` allowlist, its person-only check still running at the node (§4.8a).
- 2026-09-27 (TD-147, grinder-ao-1): *Settings, replicated* built — the `settings` link notification carries the home's `settings.yml` whole to every linked node after each `set_settings` write, and to a node once per dial; the node writes it as its own file (`_take_settings`). `set_settings` joined `modes.HOME_EDITS`, so a node forwards it while linked and refuses it offline in the home-owned edits' words. One choice the design left open: the dial's frame goes once the node's snapshot is taken, not inside the `hello` reply, since nothing is pushed to a link before its snapshot.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-316, grinder-ao-1): `ao host rebuild` and `ao host forget` refuse while the node holds a live record, naming them by team, with `--wind-down` and `--force` as the two ways on. Seen 2026-10-03 23:38 (TD-299): a rebuild of contractmatch ended grinder-cm-1 and manager-cm-1 mid-run with no wrap-up, their records gone `exited` as if the host had rebooted. The design said a rebuild is *on purpose* and what a gone container means, and not what a rebuild owes the sessions it ends.
- Not built (still, as of 2026-09-22): the terminal over the link for a machine node's session — *Later* in TD-057.
- Considered and rejected (undated, with the full reasoning): the UI host as a store-and-forward router was the least code but made the UI a second writer holding state and left unanswered which host gates a cross-host act; a mesh of host agents kept partial tallies and budgets that disagree after a partition, forced a sleeping laptop to accept inbound connections, and could not have a relay layered on it later; the relay as the bus now meant hosting the home elsewhere with a hosted service's login, tenancy and operations before there was a product. Prior art cited: Kubernetes control plane with node agents, NATS leaf nodes, IMAP over SMTP.

The home was named **Herd**, then **Team**, and became **Org** when the team definitions of §4.9 landed. The 2026-09-04 card drew Allow / Deny, Close session, Forget and Switch profile / Wait inside the slot; the 2026-09-21 anatomy moved them into the foot, which was new work for the page. - 2026-09-04, generalised 2026-09-06: the security decision "never a bare public port" — the UI is root-equivalent (it types into a shell as you), so it is reached over a private network or an authenticated tunnel, never a bare public port.
- 2026-09-06 review: the browser-mechanics bullets (live state over `/events`, card order, permission countdown, tail hygiene, keyboard focus, errors, VS Code links) were written in this review.
- 2026-09-18: the Org's **Urgent first / Pinned** toggle was dropped (Pinned kept cards where they were dragged); team cards took over arranging the page, the toggle had been left ordering cards inside one team, and the place to work through what needs a person became the Inbox (TD-069). Since then nothing about card order is stored in the browser. The one-order sort was set the same day.
- 2026-09-19: the Inbox (`/inbox`) was designed and step 1 of TD-069 built, mail only: the three sections and their order, the snoozed list, the count, the team filter (`team:name`, `team:` for what carries none) and a free-text filter over sender, text and `about`; the row controls of §4.5a; the page rendered and polled from one server-side split (`inbox_sections`); the whole message with no scroll box. Paul on keeping the Org's needs-you marks beside the Inbox: *in case someone enjoys whack-a-mole*. Paul's rule the same day: a state or an alarm mark must never look pressable.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069 step 2, built the same day): the state rows — pending permission with Allow / Deny on the hook channel, question, `stalled?`, `limited`, exited with unpushed work, and the identity alarms of §4.8a — joined *Needs you* through the same `inbox_sections`; a state row adds the session's `doing` line. Board items were left as step 3.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-079): the Inbox became a queue — reading never changes a row, a row leaves only by an answer or by resolving with a trail, FYI carries a quiet count, and an answered question is followed to its outcome in a fourth section, *Waiting on them*. TD-079 step 2's FYI count landed the same day.
- 2026-09-20: the Steering section's identity-alarm row (an alarm while it is with a techlead, §4.8a *Who answers first*) was designed and left unbuildable until TD-075.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-082, design round 2, after Paul's day with the page at full width; mockup `Inbox.dc.html`; built the same day): the Inbox had been full width until this day. Three things were wrong and one rule fixed each: (1) a message's text wrapped at a measure of its own inside an empty box — the page became one centred column, 1100 px at most (a grid of rows was drawn and turned down: it breaks the order); (2) sections were boxes with a closing paragraph explaining them — a section became a heading with an **i** mark holding that paragraph, and the page's closing paragraph went with it; (3) where one row ended and the next began was in doubt — a row became a card with a hover state and a focus ring. Also that day: times had drawn as `…` until the row's script ran (which is all a screenshot, a slow phone or a script error ever saw) — the server now renders durations in words; FYI had a second fold control — the fold became the native disclosure's heading; the FYI *new* mark was added with TD-079 step 2's FYI count.

The six-row anatomy was designed from this. The first sketch's grey *finished* pill was the sketch's mistake — a declaration is not a state. The stylesheet's shared rule for idle / exited / unreachable was split; the `--done` token left the card; *ready to close ✓* and *closed by you* had been green and became neutral text with a grey rule. Built in slices the same day: the state colours, the six rows with the slot's order, the foot (the next act first, quiet), the team header, `open_in:`, *mine* and its sort. Left open in TD-095: Paul's look at the live page.

The first build's foot had a filled white button that was the loudest thing on a card after the *needs you* ring, and its borderless, dimmed neighbours read as switched off — hence "quiet never looks disabled": outlined next act, plain links, dimmed means disabled only, Allow the only filled button.

- 2026-09-23 (TD-069 step 3, grinder-ao-2, on the techlead's answer): board items joined the Inbox's *Needs you*, counted, with Snooze and Done on §4.4's write-back (grinder-ao-1's `board_edit`). The read half was built first and held back: a counted row a person cannot clear from the page breaks *a row leaves only by an answer*, so the rows waited for the write-back. The Inbox's +1 day and +1 week count from today, not from the item's old date, since an overdue item snoozed a day from its old date would still be overdue.

- 2026-09-23 (TD-046, `grinder-ao-2`, later): *Pop out* built as designed; the build's choice of how a browser knows what it popped out is a `BroadcastChannel` between its own tabs (a popped window announces itself and answers an Org tab's ask), and the window raise is `window.open` by name with an empty URL, so the existing window is found without a reload.

- 2026-09-23 (TD-124, Paul: *many people do not like to have to use the mouse; would that be browser specific?*): the keyboard designed for screens 1, 2 and 6 — single keys when nothing editable has focus, the ring as keyboard focus on a card or a row, `g` then a letter or a digit between teams, `?` the overlay. Paul approved the map 2026-09-23 (*I like the plan for the hotkeys*); the designer wrote it into the design the same day.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-129, design round 3, written in a cloud session with Paul): the Inbox's filters became **the rail**. Paul's first shape was a tree — *All*, then each team, then that team's sections as leaves; the session pushed back that the leaves are the page's own headings a second time and that a leaf filtering to one section would hide the queue, and proposed flat facets with the sections as jump anchors. Paul's answer set the shape: the sections must filter too (*FYI items from the Guardians team*), and every line is a toggle, his workflow being one team's *Needs you* worked through, then the next team's. Hence three groups of toggles, OR within and AND across, counts following the picks, the picks in the URL, the typed `team:` syntax retired. The message page `/inbox/<id>` and the find rule (every word, any order, over the row's whole visible text) were designed the same day; the preview pane of TD-129's option (b) was left undesigned — the entry opens as its page at any width. Mockups `Inbox` (with the rail), `InboxRail`, `InboxMessage`, `InboxPhone`. The build is TD-135–137. Paul's second pass the same evening, on the artboards: the clear press reads *Clear filters*; every count on the page is *n of all* while anything is picked or typed and a plain number otherwise; the first group is *Urgency* (he offered *State* or *Severity* — *State* is a session's word and the name of a kind, *Severity* is wrong for Steering, which is on a clock, not grave); the message page answers under the entry and puts the thread after it, so nothing scrolls unless the whole thread is wanted; the thread's data was checked against `MailEntry` (`root`, `reply_to`, `outcome`, `passed_up`) and found to need one home-side read, `thread`, because the person inbox keeps no sent list; the phone's chips are the rail's Teams list, picked first, and the *Filters ▾* chip opens a full-screen sheet rather than a page of its own. His third pass: the counts had used the mail-client convention (a line counted under the other groups' picks, saying what pressing it would add), which drew *cm-grind 1* beside a page holding nothing of cm-grind; the rule became *every count is a count of rows on the page now*, an unpicked line in a picked group reading *0 of all*. The title row's needs-you pill and *showing …* line were dropped as noise beside the rail.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-130, in a cloud session with Paul; Paul's question of 2026-09-24, *is it small for comfortable reading?*): the type scale designed as six tokens on `:root` — body 14, small 12, caps 11, title 16, mono 13 and 12.5 — with line heights 1.5 and 1.55, heights following the scale in `em`, and no lower-case text under 12 px; what it replaced was a 13 px body with the reading text set below it (mail bodies 12 and 12.5, small print 11, pills 10) in some fifty literals. A compact / comfortable density setting was weighed and turned down in favour of the browser's own zoom. Every artboard was regenerated at the scale and a ladder artboard added in both themes. The build is TD-144.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-183, the designer; Paul: *have the team cards themselves be clickable to expand or collapse them*): any team folds, by a click on its header. Until then only a team with nothing live folded, by the *n sessions* button, and `syncTeams` held *a live team never folds* — a page of running teams could not be tidied. The folded live row keeps the counts and every mark, and a needs-you member rings the row without opening it: the page never opens what a person closed. That last choice went to Paul as a steer (the alternative: the row opens itself).
- 2026-09-26 (TD-181, the designer; Paul at the dc-grind card after its wind-down: *the unfolded card should show the same info as the running team — some items will become stale and that's ok*): screen 1's one-row team, unfolded, is a running team's card — the three facets and compact members. The summary had been built only for a team with a live member (`team_summary(…) if … and live`), so dc-grind, all three exited, unfolded to bare cards while samscrape-grind, concluded with idle members, kept its summary. The raw-file-only option was rejected: one JSON object per line is not a reading surface. Build: TD-165 (the adapter's read, the RPC, the node read, the CLI), TD-166 (the page and the button).

- 2026-09-28 (screen 9 **Transcript**, TD-166, grinder-ao-1): the page built — `/transcript/<id>` (`ui/transcript.py`, `transcript.html`, `transcript_turns.html`) reading the `transcript` RPC once at load; *earlier turns* fetches `?part=1&before=<offset>` and puts the fragment in the button's place; the Focus header's **Transcript** and the exited banner's *or read its transcript*. Two readings the build made: the head's count is the turns shown, since the read goes backwards and never counts the whole file (the mockup's *63 turns* would need a second pass over it); and **VS Code** is drawn only for a record on this host, since another host's path is not a path here and a container's reach link names a directory. Opening the page does not mark the session seen: it is a read.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-207, the designer): **the board's horizon** on screen 6. Until this date the Inbox read boards with `--due-only` and said so nowhere: on 2026-09-27 Paul looked for two items a session had left him, each due 2026-10-04, and found nothing (*we need to add a setting for this so it is obvious to the user*). The read drops `--due-only`; items ahead sit in an uncounted fold, *Board, due later*; one line says the horizon and what waits beyond it; the horizon was `person.inbox.board_ahead`, a number of days, default `7d`. **Revised the same day on Paul's word**, relayed on the PR before it merged (*make it settable, e.g. … show all, show past due, show next 10 per team*): the setting is a mode, `person.inbox.board_show` — `next:<n>` per team (the default, `next:10`), `due`, `<n>d`, `all` — what a mode shows ahead is drawn under *Board, coming up*, and the fold, *not shown*, holds what it hides. A due item is shown under every mode, and *past due* counts today. A second word the same evening added *due this week*, overdue and the next seven days, which is `7d` by name.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-220 slice 2, grinder-ao-2): the Inbox's board read dropped `--due-only`; each row says whether it is due now (`board_due_now`, the reader's `surfaces_at_start` read from its fields: an undecided `fyi` item is never due, as the old read never surfaced one) and `board_horizon` sorts the rows by the mode. What is counted is unchanged: the cache hands the pages only the due rows. One reading the design did not say: the `<n>d` window is measured from the reader's `today`, which each row carries.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-220 slice 3, grinder-ao-2): *Board, coming up*, the *not shown* fold and the line drawn on the Inbox (`board_horizon.html`, put back whole by the poll as `html.horizon`) and on the Repo page's *Waiting on you* (`horizon_of`: the Inbox's lists cut to the repo); a row coming up reads its due words in the quiet colour; the rail counts it under *board items* in section `coming`, picked with *Needs you* and in none of its numbers, and the fold's rows once it is opened; **show** opens the fold for the page view. The line is drawn whenever a board was read or any row is held, so a reader's note beside rows still draws them.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-220 slice 4, grinder-ao-2): **board items shown** on the Settings page's **You** — four radio choices, the two numbers beside theirs (10 and 7 until typed; typing one picks its choice, and the days choice reads *due this week* at 7), saved with the card through `/api/settings/you` as `inbox: {board_show}`, which `set_settings` validates.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-220 slices 5–6, grinder-ao-2): the grinder preset says *on the board, due <date>* when a summary or message names a board line, and dates a line meant for today as today. Building the last tests found the board row's **Snooze ▾** counting +1 day and +1 week from today, so on a row coming up it brought the date nearer; it now counts from the later of today and the item's own date (the row's `data-due`).
- 2026-09-29 (TD-232 slice 1, grinder-ao-2): a row in *TDs in motion* carries its entry's priority (`motion_rows`: `priority`, *high*, *medium*, *low* or empty) and draws it as the priority bar's chip, **H** / **M** / **L**, in a slot of fixed width that stays empty for an unmarked row; rows sort by phase, then priority, then reference.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-232 slice 2, grinder-ao-2): the Doing list reads *age · doer · words* in columns — `_short_age` on the server and `fmtShortAge` in the page in the same words, the page re-drawing the ages once a minute from `data-doing-at` with the reader's clock as the tooltip; the doer's width `doer_w` (the longest name, at most 18) as `--doer-n`, each column sized in its own mono font's `ch`. One reading the design left open: *below 720 px the words wrap under them* is built as the words wrapping in their own column, the age and doer columns holding.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-208, the designer): **boards are read against origin** on screen 6 — the Inbox's read passes the reader's `--fetch`, a checkout merely behind is read from `origin/<default>`, and one note above a repo's rows says where the board was read and what that hides. Measured 2026-09-27: dev-cadence ten commits behind, agentorc four, samscrape one; three of six boards differed from origin. Found in the round: a write-back is never pushed, so after one Snooze the next merged board line makes the checkout two-sided and the reader shows the local board; the note says so in the warning colour, and whether a write-back should push is TD-222, Paul's to decide. The read gains a bound of its own (45 s, then a plain read), one read at a time, and a plain read after a press.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-208, the designer, before the merge): a second fact-check after TD-220 was built — the note's case is read from `fetch_note`'s fixed phrases, the parenthesis after *board DIFFERS from* among them, since three cases share one opening; the entry's Location says the read as TD-220 left it.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-221 slices 1–2, built by `grinder-ao-2`, *Boards are read against origin*): the board read passes the reader's `--fetch`, stopped at `BOARD_FETCH_TIMEOUT` (45 s) and followed at once by a plain read when stopped or failed, each board then carrying the reader's own *fetch skipped (<why>)*; each row keeps the reader's `source` and `fetch_note`. One fetching read runs at a time, in the background, and requests are answered from the last reading; the read after Snooze, Done, Reply or Put on the board is a plain read of that one board laid over it, and a fetch that began before the press keeps the pressed board's rows. The first read is plain and waited for — the design had not said, and a page that waits 45 s on a dead remote at start is the thing the bound is for. Built together, since slice 1 alone would have had every minute's request wait on the network.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-221 slices 3–4, built by `grinder-ao-2`, *Boards are read against origin*): the **origin note** and the read-only rows. The case is read by one table of the reader's fixed phrases (`ORIGIN_PHRASES`, `origin_case`), and a test fails on a `FetchResult` sentence in the reader that it does not know; the note sits above the repo's first board row in each list as drawn, since *Needs you* interleaves the repos with the mail and the horizon's lists are separate. Two things the design had not said: a Snooze, Done or Reply pressed on a board read from origin that reaches the UI anyway is refused (409, the same words) before the write-back, unless a plain read of that one board, kept out of the cache, finds the checkout pulled since the reading (the review of #774: otherwise a pull left the press refused until the next fetching read); and an unknown phrase draws no note, the test being the guard. The field for the case is asked of dev-cadence on the board.
- 2026-09-30 (TD-227, grinder-ao-2; the review of #824): the Repo page unfolds the Technical debt list its hash names and scrolls to the row — the team start row's links landed on a row folded past the fourth, which a browser cannot scroll to.

- 2026-09-30 (TD-247, the designer): the seat slot gains *on call — comes when a member needs a reading* for a manager on call (§6 rule 3 `team`); not built (TD-259).
- 2026-10-01 (TD-222, the designer; Paul, 2026-10-01, to the anchor: *it seems like main on the anchor falls behind consistently — should we have our host agent pull it routinely if no session is running on the anchor? … lets have it do when git allows and the anchor session is idle*): screen 6's *the host agent does not pull* (TD-208, three days old) reversed — a checkout merely behind is fast-forwarded by the home's tick when git allows and the anchor session is idle (§6 *Pull*), the *behind* note carrying the pull's standing as its tail; and *a row read from origin is read-only until the checkout is pulled* ends with the write-back's move onto origin's head (§4.4): every row is actionable whichever file it was read from, the read after a press becoming a fetching read of that one board. The disabled row stays drawn until TD-264 lands.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-262, the designer): the card's slot names the closer — *closed by you*, *closed by <session>*, *closed itself*, *closed by the tick · <why>*, bare *closed* for a record with none — and a closed or exited record that declared keeps its declaration after the ending. Until this date the slot read *closed by you* for every closed record (dc-grind, 2026-10-01: three cards said the person had closed a team its manager wound down forty seconds after the start), and the ending hid the declaration.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-266, the designer; Paul, with a screenshot of the Org: *there is a closed session in the No team section that I do not see how to remove — is this an oversight?*): row 6's `closed` leads with **Forget**, as `exited` does, with Details second; until this date a closed card led with Details and its Forget sat on the Details page's banner. The menu draws what applies: on a closed card, or any whose pane is gone, *more ⋯* keeps Message…, Restart and Forget and leaves out (not dims) the seven that act on a process or a pane. The slot's hover and the Details banner say a closed record is forgotten by itself a day after the close. Resume stays on the banner. Built by TD-267.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-271, the designer): row 5 (b) of the card's anatomy carries the wait after a declaration, or alone (§4.5a *waiting* mark); the Org mockup's tdgrind-4 and the Inbox mockup's orphaned steer show the words.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-278, grinder-ao-2; Paul's walk of the Inbox: *It looks like the Answers are not being parsed correctly*): the Inbox ran the board reader of the first registered repo carrying it — samscrape's, synced 2026-09-17, before dev-cadence TD-036 gave the reader `Answers:` — so no board row drew its answers. The copy is now picked by what it can do: dev-cadence's own source, else the copy synced last; and a copy lacking a field the page draws is named in the board note.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-277, the designer): screen 3 gains *The form reworked as one thing* — who, where, the job, who may act on it; mockup `NewSessionReworked.dc.html` beside the shipped `NewSession.dc.html`.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-303, the designer; Paul the same day: *Currently a "Needs you" item seems to stay in "needs you" once answered - it should move out to "waiting on them"/other so it does not appear to be waiting on me*): screen 6 gains *A board item the person answered waits on them*. Until this date the Inbox kept a decided or replied board line under *Needs you*, counted: the design read cadence §3.5's *stays due* as *stays the person's*. dev-cadence's reader had said otherwise for a decided item since its TD-036 — `waiting_on: session`, the tag *decided … — waiting on a session*, and a decided item left out of its push count — and the Inbox did not read that field. Seen on samscrape's backup item, decided Oct 3 and at the head of Needs you (64). Two choices of the round: a Reply moves the row as a `Decided:` does (cadence says a reply *decides nothing*, and the reader keeps `waiting_on: person` for it, so that half is the Inbox's own reading of `replies`); and the row comes back after three days with nothing done (`BOARD_WAIT_DAYS`), a constant and not a setting. Compared and not chosen: a mark at the home for each press, cleared when the line's text changes — it would copy board state into the home (screen 6: *none copied into another*) and would miss an answer written outside the Inbox. Not built.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-305): *A board item the person answered waits on them* built. `board_rows` stamps each row `answered` (`decided` from the reader's `waiting_on`, its `decided` where a reader gives none; `replied` from `replies`), the answer's date and `stale`; `inbox_sections` puts an answered row that is not stale under *Waiting on them*, in no count, and `board_horizon` leaves it out of *coming up* and *not shown*. The decided row's *not done: it stays on the board as its session's work order* line went, the *waiting on …* words saying it; the **answers** and **Go with it** help say the row moves and comes back in three days.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-418, ao-paul with Paul; his screenshot of the live Org and seven items, then four more rounds): the Org declutter — **+ New ▾** for Shell and New session; *mine* and *show command runs* become filter words; the rollup's Agents line an *i*, *answer needed* hidden at 0, Agents and Needs you alone while one team is live, the facets' heads and bars one height; the team header one row with a flow chip, its definition in the *i* panel; ✉ n folded or not; the lanes line an *i*, each member's own count in its state pill, solid when it is out of work with entries waiting; the *waiting* pill and *idle* meaning it holds nothing; the kind bar's seven kinds; the + card's *New session*; Doing's bare head and `0m`; a team with nothing live draws only the facets that hold something, none where all three are empty (narrowing TD-181 at Paul's choice, after the draft had reversed it); the page names a seat by its role, *the techlead*, never *the reader* — the mockup `docs/mockups/reviews/2026-10-08-org-declutter.html` (five rounds with Paul on 2026-10-08, the shots `2026-10-08-org-declutter-{before,after,after-open}.png` and their full-page pair, re-captured from the live page for round 4), built by TD-428.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-264, grinder-ao-2): the read after a press, when stopped, draws the board as the write-back left it. *Stopped* covers both our bound and the reader's own *fetch skipped*: the reader bounds its fetch at 30 s, so that is the usual stop. It had drawn the checkout's line, from before the press, until the next fetching read. Now the board is read plainly from the host agent's own tree, which the press reset to origin's head after its PR merged, and drawn under the *behind* note. The rows did not need to be built again from the press's result: the reader still parses the board, and the tree already holds it.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-144, grinder-ao-2; Paul's look of 2026-10-04 at the live page: *bigger*): `--t-body` raised from 14 px to 15 px and nothing else moved, as the *Type scale* says; the card's rows, slot and foot keep their pixel tokens, so every Org card is still one height (read headless at 1440 and 390 px on a scratch home).

Until this date the bar was one row of fixed height that never wrapped, so at a phone's 390 px every page was 620–720 px wide, its last buttons off screen, and anywhere the bar was short of room flex squeezed the chip to nothing, so `fitUsage` hid every account behind a bare *+n*; Settings' *board items shown* boxes, drawn at `.setin`'s 100% inside a label that never wrapped, widened its page too. A live look of 2026-10-03 found it.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-292 slice 4b, grinder-ao-2): **Send to reviewer** is drawn on an open `ask` look whose sender's team has a techlead seat, the page reading the seat from `org.yml` and passing it to `inbox_hand`; a look with a reviewer is listed with the snoozed as *with <seat> since <time>*, its handed `ask` under *Waiting on them* as a `look`, and the seat's reading comes back above its answers as *<seat> read it: …*. Until this date the host agent's half (slice 4a) had no button.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-303, the designer): **Inbox section: Waiting on them** gains the answered board row — Reply, Done and Open board, no Dismiss, no Snooze, no answers — and the **answers** and **Reply** rows say where their row goes after the press. No new control. Not built.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-337, grinder-ao-2): the team card's **stops** note built. The page keeps the `teams` of the home's `settings` read beside `person` (`uiconf.team_until`), and reads it again with the `host` reading the deltas use and after a Settings Save of a team, so the header follows a Save without a reload. A live team's header draws *stops <t>* by `stop_note`. A member's compact line adds *· stops <t>* only where its `run_until` reads differently from the team's time. The two are compared as drawn, so a stamp a second off is not drawn. The line adds *· wrapping up* once the member is asked. A team's Save stamps its members while the Save is still in flight, so the events stream reads the settings again when a member's `run_until` moves; otherwise the line would compare the new stamp with the old time. The team card's help lists no header notes, so it is unchanged.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-340, grinder-ao-2): *A control answers the press* built. Every `.btn` has one `:hover`, one `:active` and one `:focus-visible` rule. They are driven by two custom properties: `--btn-wash` (the `--hover` layer, laid over the variant's own colour as a background image) and `--btn-shade` (a filled `primary` darkens instead). The ghost and card-footer hover rules were folded into these, and the footers' `background` shorthands became `background-color` so the wash is not reset. A press marks its row pending in the frame the request leaves: dimmed, `aria-busy`, its controls disabled, and the row taking no pointer, because a link and the Snooze menu take no `disabled`. The row's age, or a board row's due words, gives way to the verb from `PRESS_VERBS`. A row the press takes away leaves at once, with a toast that waits (`AO.toast(…, wait)` and `settle`). A refusal comes back on the row's error line, which every row now carries, from `AO.pressErrs` until the row's next press or a reload. The verb table also covers controls the design's list leaves out (Pause, Log TD, a promote or work row's Dismiss), so no pending row shows a wire name. Two choices kept today's behaviour: Reply and an answer keep their row pending until the refresh, and a state row leaves on any answer but Suspend and a decide.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-338, the designer; Paul the same day, checking off board rows): **A control answers the press** (§4.5 screen 6) — one hover, pressed and focus look for every `.btn` variant on every page (§4.5a *a control's look*), the row marked **pending** in the frame of the press with the control's verb under way, and a row the press takes away gone on the press with a toast that waits for the landing and the row back, marked, on a refusal (§4.5a *Inbox row: pending*). Not taken: answering the board write-back's request once the edit is accepted and reporting the landing as an Inbox event — the row is gone at the press already, and a failure reaches the person the same way, so the home records no landing in flight. Not built — TD-340.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-335, the designer; seen by grinder-ao-1 on a scratch home, pressing TD-297): the team card gains a **stops** note — a live team's `teams.<team>.until` on the header's second line, once, by the card's formatter — and the member's compact card draws its own only when it differs, with *· wrapping up* once asked. Of the entry's three shapes, the header was chosen over a note on every member's line (one setting, drawn once, where the *over its line* and *started … for* notes already are) and over narrowing the **stops** row to the full card (which would have left a team's time on no Org card). Not built — TD-337.

Until this date the row drew the head line and the answer buttons, and the context, caveats and the reason for the default were in the board file alone. Compared and not chosen: the block always drawn under the head (a queue of sixty rows becomes sixty essays); the block closed everywhere (the person presses an answer without the case for it, which is the fault the entry names); **Recommended:** lifted beside **Go with it** (a control's words built from a line's prose, which the page never does — the default is the reader's field). **Put on the board** asks for no block: cadence §3.3 allows a tool a head line alone. Not built.
