## 4.5b Reachability, and the shape of a hosted service
- (undated, original draft): the section opened with "Why this is not 'install Tailscale'": for one person the private network is fine, but the deciding question is how someone who has never opened a port would use this.
- 2026-09-16 (§4.4a): the `ssh` transport row was revised — every non-home host agent became a node that dials the home over ssh (`agentorc-agent link`) instead of the UI holding an ssh per host.
- 2026-09-17 (§4.4a *A container node*): the container-on-the-home's-own-machine node was added to the `ssh` row (per-node link socket, directory bind-mounted in; home brings it up, installs its own version, supervises it).
- Phase 1 note: the terminal bridge "today spawns `tmux attach` locally under `/term/<id>`"; `agentorc-agent rpc` as a stdio bridge was noted as "already true" at the time of writing.

## 4.5c Product direction
- 2026-09-06: written as "a paragraph, not a plan". At the time the claim that a hosted "run Claude Code for you" is what every model supplier already sells, and that neutrality is the moat, was a prediction.
- 2026-09-13 (amendment): the prediction came true — OpenAI's Agents API (public beta 2026-09-10), Anthropic's Managed Agents, AWS Bedrock AgentCore and Microsoft's Foundry Agent Service all sell a managed cloud agent runtime on token billing with no infrastructure fee. Conclusion recorded: the runtime is commodity; the relay sells the neutral view, not a runtime ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-13-openai-agents-api.md)). The spec now states this in the present tense.

## 4.6 Transport and terminal mechanics
- 2026-09-05: the section's decisions came from a review of the `sessionorc` layer before build.
- 2026-09-05: the original rule was one long-lived ssh from the UI to *each* host; "phase 1 accepts this and tests it (it is what a hand-typed second `tmux attach` does today)" was the reasoning for the `window-size latest` decision.
- 2026-09-09 (TD-022): scrollback moved to tmux's own history reached through tmux (`mouse on` per session, Shift+PageUp/PageDown via bridge message), replacing a local xterm.js buffer.
- 2026-09-11 (TD-029): the reconnect contract's three rules were added because the reconnect loop did not terminate: backoff had been resetting on open, the server closed 4404 only when the record already said the pane was gone, and the page waited for the next tick to learn a session was killed or closed.
- 2026-09-16 (§4.4a): the ssh rule was revised — the UI holds the link to the home host agent only; other host agents dial the home; the protocol, backoff and `unreachable` diagnosis carry over to that link, which multiplexes requests by id. Terminal attaches to a session on another host kept their own ssh.
- (TD-096, undated in this chunk): the read-only attach for `unattended` sessions was added, enforced in the UI process rather than the terminal widget.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-164, the designer): **the mouse is the browser's** — no `mouse on` on the attach, no tracking, a plain drag selects, Shift+click extends (xterm.js's own, checked in the vendored source), the wheel a scroll message with a line count that the bridge turns into `copy-mode -e; send-keys -X -N n scroll-up`; the read-only attach's wheel-only carve-out goes with tracking; a program asking for the mouse itself gets none, and there is no per-session escape.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-175, the designer): the round log beside the run log, keyed by name, pruned with it.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-174, grinder-ao-1): **the mouse is the browser's** built. `attach_argv` chains no `set-option`. `scroll_argv` takes `lines`: `copy-mode -e ; send-keys -X -N n scroll-up|scroll-down`, capped at 500, and a page when `lines` is absent. The page catches `wheel` on `#term` in the capture phase, before xterm.js would turn a notch into arrow keys, and sends one `{scroll, lines}` per animation frame, carrying the fraction left over (`AO.wheelStep`). `WHEEL_ONLY`, `AO.isWheel` and the read-only carve-out are gone, so a read-only attach drops every key frame. Calls made: a session an earlier attach set `mouse on` on keeps it until it is started again, because the attach now sets nothing, not even an unset. The htop/`less` check the design asked for could not be run from an unattended build, and it is on the board.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-296 #12, grinder-ao-2; the techlead's ruling on the steer): *The mouse is the browser's* says a program that asks for the mouse gets it. Until this date the paragraph said such a program *gets no mouse, since tmux forwards none with its mouse off*, a runtime fact TD-174's build could not look at and left for the first live look; the look of 2026-10-03 sent a program asking for SGR tracking and the pane received the press and release (`\x1b[<0;26;6M`). The sentence changed, not the forwarding: blocking it would take the mouse from programs that ask for it, for a selection Shift+drag already gives. A read-only attach's bridge drops the reports with every other key frame.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-421, the designer; Paul, through Add entry: *need to be able to go to a link by clicking it in the session terminal … the vscode terminal has this*): *A URL in the pane is a link* — xterm.js's web-links addon, `http`/`https` only, underlined on hover, Ctrl+click / Cmd+click opens a new tab with `noopener`; a plain click or tap does nothing, so TD-071's rule holds (one act on what is written, on a modifier press); a read-only Focus has it; no person setting. TD-422 builds it.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-422, grinder-ao-2): *A URL in the pane is a link* built — `@xterm/addon-web-links` 0.11.0 vendored (its SHA-256 matched to the npm release), loaded in `AO.focus` beside the fit addon on every Focus, read-only and popped-out too; `AO.paneLink` is the handler: Ctrl or Cmd held, and `http:` or `https:` only, opened with `noopener`.

## 4.8 Capabilities, report channels, and role presets

- 2026-10-08 (TD-378, the anchor; Paul's decision on the board, *Pin 1h, restart lapsed, trial 200k*): this repo's `.agentorc.yml` sets `roles.grinder.context: {bound: 200k}` as a one-week trial. Until this date the repo set no number and its grinders took the package's 300k. On 2026-10-01–08, requests over 200k carried 35% of the weighted cost ([ADR 2026-10-08](decisions/2026-10-08-prompt-cache.md)), and TD-460 reads the trial back.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-381, the designer): the `anchor` preset row — a seat on call in the main checkout, lane `anchor`, no grants.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-002, grinder-ao-2): `attach`, the Focus composer's **Attach**, joins the person-only list: a file put where a session will read it is the person's, and no session uploads through the page.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-310, the designer): *Role names*' clause on a designer's whole brief in a team with no flow deleted with the carve-out — a repo's designer brief is its supplement, as every role's is.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-108 step 3, the designer; Paul the same day: *the fold goes to the designer, not to him*): **the fold was dropped.** The design review of 2026-09-22 asked for ten person-only inbox RPCs to become one `inbox_act {entry, action}`; grinder-ao-1 looked before building and found the ten in four routing classes that `modes.py` and §4.4a key on the method name, and §4.8a already said `identity_ack`'s wire name stays. By this date the person-only RPCs numbered 25, gated by 18 `is_person` checks written into the RPCs and two helpers (`_person_entry` for five, `_promote_person` for two), each refusal worded by hand. Decided: the names stay, `mail.PERSON_ONLY` is the one list, one check opens each RPC (in the RPC, since the link's `act` bypasses the dispatcher), and a test holds list and code to each other. The build is TD-317, which also settles where `agent_wake.py`'s passengers go (the techlead's read of #642).
- 2026-10-04 (TD-317 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the person-only gate built — `mail.PERSON_ONLY`, the 25 names, and `agent_common.person_only(caller, act, section)` as the first statement of each, `_person_entry` and `_promote_person` calling it. Until this date the 25 were gated by 18 checks written into the RPCs and the two helpers, each refusing in its own words (*… is a person's own: refused to a session*, *… only by a person*, *… the person's own bookkeeping*, one with no section at all); now one shape, *<caller> cannot <act>: a person's own act, refused to every session (design <section>)*, `entry_add`'s pointer to `ao finding` kept in its act. `tests/test_person_only.py` calls each with a bare agent and every other parameter `None`, and reads the source for the other direction.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-120 step 3, Paul's decision, the anchor): the `designer` became an `unattended` member with a `design-first` lane, from the interactive design seat of 2026-09-23. Paul, watching it work a list of four rounds without waiting on anyone: *if that is its directive should we make it unattended? … ideally, obvious designs would not be shown but just done.* The three tiers — obvious → done with an FYI note; a defensible default → a bounded `steer`; taste, scope, spending → an `ask` with answers — are the mail kinds the Inbox draws, so nothing new was added to §4.5a. The on-demand interactive member and the role-labelled header button that step 3 first proposed (draft #515) were dropped: starting the team starts the designer.
- 2026-09-10: section written. Motivation: four unattended workers ran on kmaster the first day the Team showed more than one, and the Team could not say which TDs any of them held, had finished, or had filed on the side, nor stop one worker from `ao kill`-ing another. A `role` field the host agent keys on was considered and rejected — one grinder filed TD-025 while grinding, which no single label describes.
- 2026-09-10 (TD-028 step 1): the `control` gate landed (then named `orchestrate`); the host agent checks it before the method runs. Caller rule of the day: the caller is the session id the CLI sends (§4.7); a caller id with no record is a session holding no grant; an RPC with no caller is a person. Superseded 2026-09-19 by §4.8a (the caller is what the channel says, not the envelope; the id was an environment variable, which is why the gate was never a security boundary).
- 2026-09-11 (TD-028 step 2): the `progress` and `finding` RPCs landed, ungated; a refused entry is not an error, the reply names it `refused`.
- 2026-09-11 (TD-028 step 3): derived reports landed (`sessionorc.reports`) — branch `tdNNN-*` → claimed, merged PR → done, ledger rows → finding.
- 2026-09-12: membership (`controllers` on the target) designed; prior-art survey in ADR 2026-09-12. Per-repo boundaries rejected (§10). Motivation: with one manager "may act on others" and "on which others" were the same sentence; `guardians` getting its own manager, a ui and a backend manager on one repo, a read-only cross-repo status session and a director made them different sentences.
- 2026-09-13: membership approved by Paul; the record, the gate, create-adds-creator and `set_controllers` landed (TD-036 step 1). Interactive sessions became out of every controller's reach as a gate (TD-041; §9 invariant 5). Roles began resolving to a profile (§4.9). Repo-defined roles landed (TD-040 step a). Defaults at launch landed (TD-036 step 4): preset over repo, `--controller` over both, a stale configured name dropped with one stderr line.
- 2026-09-14: waking a manager designed (TD-049, from Paul); `ao wait` built in the CLI the same day (later, TD-052 step 3, a thin call to the host agent's `wait` RPC so the host agent can decide mail wakes). The "worker sends to its manager" reading was rejected because of delivery, not direction: since 2026-09-14 a worker may message its manager (§4.10) and a message reaches an interactive session (the invariant 5 carve-out); the run-out-of-work tests were designed (§4.9a).
- 2026-09-17 (TD-053 step 1): `ao progress none --why` / `out_of_work` landed.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-055 step 2, 3): `orchestrator` renamed `lead` and the grant `orchestrate` renamed `control`; the old names read for one release, never written; the aliases are simply deleted when the release is over.
- 2026-09-17 (TD-056): a claim became a lease (`LEASE_TTL` 12 h, `--force`), the lesson from mcp_agent_mail in the messaging ADR. Until then the at-claim `note` to siblings (TD-052 step 4) was the stopgap.
- 2026-09-18: an idle worker's card read *last: ▸▸ bypass permissions on (shift+tab…* — the pane preview of a TUI is the tool's chrome, the case for a `doing` channel.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-074, decided by Paul): the `doing` channel and role `icon:` designed and built; a team card's header showed its manager's `doing` line.
- 2026-09-19 / 2026-09-20 (TD-076; TD-060 gave the project the name `shiftlead`): Paul decided, then confirmed, the names: `lead` becomes `manager`, the TD-075 go-between is `techlead`, the bare word `lead` is retired and never reused; `director` keeps its name (proposed; the glossary round was to settle it and nothing argued for a change). Reasoning: `lead` was a role name, a structural key of every team definition, a word `ao team` prints and the badge on every record since 2026-09-17, so a word that silently came to mean the other session would make every one of those lie. The renamed-roles table's `orchestrator → lead` entry was repointed to `manager`; the retired-words table was added because `lead` is wanted (a merely unknown word is one a repo's `roles:` may define tomorrow) whereas `orchestrator` is not. Step 2 (2026-09-20) changed the preset row and every config example's key; the default session name `<team>-lead` was kept. Step 3 (2026-09-20) built `label:` in both `roles:` layers, `ao roles`, the badge, the team header and Inbox row, and the `/api/teams/<t>/stop` answer's key. The design-doc sweep landed 2026-09-20: running text says *manager*; dated history keeps the word of its day.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-075 step 1): the `techlead` preset and brief built; until then the word was in the reserved-words table and a role of that name was refused with a reason. The table stays, empty.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-083): `ao progress restart --why` / `restart_wanted` designed (built 2026-09-21); a restart wanted became a wake.

- 2026-09-20: the at-claim `note` was removed from the briefs; the lease was checked on the live system, not in a test — with `tdgrind-ao-1` holding `TD-078`, `tdgrind-ao-2`'s `ao progress claim TD-078` answered *TD-078 is claimed by ao-agentorc-tdgrind-ao-1 since 2026-09-20T20:43:08Z (a lease, design §4.8): pick another reference, or claim it anyway with `--force`*.
- 2026-09-20: session names in the team definitions renamed (`orchestrator-ao-1` → `manager-ao-1`, `tdgrind-ao-N` → `grinder-ao-N`), as new sessions, on a restart that is Paul's word to give.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-095, PR #389; built the same day): Paul reversed the 2026-09-19 decision after seeing both one above the other — the manager's `doing` line is on the manager's card, the first in the group, and the team header stops repeating it. - 2026-09-21 (TD-098): the `auditor` preset designed; built 2026-09-22 (step 3).
- 2026-09-22 (TD-107, from the design review): the renamed-roles, retired-words and reserved-words tables deleted, code and design — "for one release" had no meaning with no release and one user. `orchestrator` and `lead` are unknown roles, `lead:` an unknown team key; a record badged with either keeps its badge as text, labelled by its own name. Step 2 removed `GRANT_ALIASES`: `orchestrate` is an unknown grant, refused in a request, a role's or a team's `grants:`, and a stored record holding it is loaded as written and holds no control (none existed on kmaster).
- The first real `ao team start` broke on a brief that named one night (TD-042): two sessions came up whose brief told them to stop at a time already past, and one did so within a minute — the origin of "a brief describes the job, not the run".
- The first manager was described as "a session, not code" running "for a few evenings" before any rule became a §6 policy.

- 2026-09-22: a repo's brief became a **supplement** filled into the template's `{repo}` slot, never a whole-file replacement (TD-114; Paul's question that evening: *is there a reason all of the boilerplate ao information is kept in each repo vs being kept here?*). Motivation: three repos (agentorc, samscrape, dev-cadence) each carried a 2,000–4,000-word copy of the manager and grinder mechanics, and three defects found the same night — the hand `ao new` crash restart made stale by TD-103, the seat fill that cannot carry `{context}`, the temporary 95% usage sentence — had to be fixed in every copy. The whole-file override had existed since TD-040 because the repo briefs predate the presets; its one remaining case, prototyping a mechanics change before a promote, is served by the supplement's precedence (*the repo's rules win where they disagree*). Paul asked whether to keep a full override; the design says no.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-114 step 1, grinder-ao-2): the supplement built — `{repo}` and *This repo's rules* in the five templates, `brief_text(supplement=…)`, a definition's `brief:` and `ao new --brief` filling the slot, the start's repeated-heading note; until then a `brief:` replaced the template whole (TD-040's rule).
- 2026-09-23 (TD-116, grinder-ao-1): `decide` joined the acting RPCs — `control` plus membership, as `send` — on Paul's decision of the same day. It had been ungated since TD-028 step (1), because §4.8 listed it so and the UI answers as a person with no caller; until then any session could answer any other's permission prompt, and a node served a session's `decide` on another while its link was down. The same change made a controller's answer refill no wake budget and put its name, not *you*, on the trail.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-120, the anchor; Paul): *Role names* gained the `designer` — the person's interactive design seat, an org- or repo-defined role with its own brief, not a preset.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-119, grinder-ao-1): a session may no longer answer its own permission prompt. TD-116 had gated `decide` "exactly as `send`", and `send` onto oneself passes the gate, so a session blocked on the hook could `ao allow $AGENTORC_SESSION` from a background task or a subagent and approve its own tool call. The anchor decided the same day to refuse it, whatever the session holds, at the gate and in a node's offline table alike — the same class as `set_grants` on oneself.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-150 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): a derived entry refused over a declared claim now leaves its PR beside it as `review_pr` (`Session.note_review`, called by the tick and by a node's derived report — never by an RPC, whose refused entry still says `refused`), set while that PR is open, cleared at its merge or its close unmerged (`reports.derive`'s `reviews`, by number, and the checked-out branch's closed PR marked `PR_CLOSED`); a re-claim keeps it, a done or dropped carries none. Invariant 10 is unchanged in substance: the session's own fields never move — this is the one derived annotation a declared entry carries.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-161, the designer): `prompts:` on a preset — `{label, text}`, layered as every key is, replaced whole per layer, verbatim text with no substitution; `ao roles` prints the labels.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-162, the designer): `message:` on a preset, with built-in defaults for the five presets from §4.9 / §4.9b (a `designer` is not a preset and writes its own in `roles:`); the definition's line, never the session's — Paul's first thought was the profile, which is tool · account · model and says nothing about a job.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-171, grinder-ao-2): *A role says when to message it* built — `message:` in `ROLE_KEYS` with the five built-in defaults and the 120-character check, `ao roles`, the view's `message_line`, the composer's first line (*Message it about <line>*), the Message controls' `title` head, and the team header's **who for what** from `teamrun.role_holders`. The header's example phrases (*questions → manager-ao-1 · PRs and the architecture → …*) were not derivable from the designed sentences; the header draws the sentences themselves, *<Label>: <line>* for a role several sessions hold (a steer to the techlead, whose answer was the design's sentence over its example: a shorter field would be a design change). The paragraph's example is replaced by the rule.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-170, grinder-ao-2): *A role has saved prompts* built — `prompts:` in `ROLE_KEYS` (a list of `{label, text}`, the label one line of 24 characters at most, checked when read and named by role and entry, replaced whole per layer; the built-ins carry none), `ao roles`' labels, `/api/roles` and New session's options, the chips beside Focus's Send (a press is `send` with the text, Shift+press fills the composer; hidden with the composer on `exited`, `closed`, `limited` and `unreachable`; none on an unattended record), and New session's chips filling the Opening prompt. On the phone the composer's row is laid out as a column with the chips first, so they wrap above the text box.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-188, the designer; Paul: *460k tokens is a lot … evaluate the compact skill or another method*): `context: {bound}` on a preset, 200k for the worker presets, none for manager, techlead and plain; a definition, not a setting.
- 2026-09-26 (TD-175, the designer; Paul at a manager's card reading *18 unpushed*: *is making round-by-round commits on the manager's branch the right design?*): **A session's round log** — the round line kept, git dropped: `ao log` into a host-agent file keyed by the record's name, so a restart reads its previous run; the launch branch a launch artefact the manager commits nothing to; Ready to close's unpushed signal judged right and the log wrong. Rejected: the record's trail (an Inbox thing, home-owned, not a memory), a `note` to itself (mail wakes and is counted), and keeping the git log with the branch pushed each round (a save-point branch still).
- 2026-09-27 (TD-190 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): `context: {bound}` built — in `ROLE_KEYS`, checked by `normalize_context` (a count as the reading writes it, `200k`/`1M`, or an integer; `none` removes it), 200k on grinder, hunter and auditor, carried by `ao new`, a team start and the New session form onto the record as `context_bound` in tokens, home-owned. The design named no record field; `context_bound` sits beside the observed `context` rather than inside it, since one is the node's reading and the other the home's intent (§4.4a's owners). The card's reading turns red past it and `ao status -v` says *bound 200k (over)*; rule 5's line is the next slice.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-191, grinder-ao-1): the round log built — `log` and `log_tail` RPCs; `ao log "<line>"`, and `ao log --tail n` with `--id` for a read; the manager template's round step says `ao log` and its wind-down pushes nothing; a restart's first read is `ao log --tail 20`; the Focus Session card's *rounds* line. The file is keyed by the record's base id (`ao-<repo>-<name>`, §4.1), the design's *name in its repo*: `<name>` alone would have shared one file between two repos' sessions of the same name. A line is cut at 500 characters, where `doing` cuts at 200, since a manager's round names several members. `log_tail` is a node read (`NODE_READS`), since the file is on the disk of the host where the session runs.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-202, grinder-ao-1; Paul: *when a grinder chooses a TD, are they working them by priority (critical/high) first? Seems like they should be*): *Choosing in a free-pick lane* written — the order, the reasons to pass a higher entry over, and the priority in the claim's first `ao doing` line; `ao repo` became the tool, listing its pickable entries High first with their priority. dev-cadence's `ledger.py --pickable` sorts the same way but does not read the header lines (TD-118), so it lists entries a grinder may not take; the template names `ao repo` instead.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-199, the designer): *a promote changes every team's rules at once* was true of the package and not of a running member, which keeps the brief it started with; the sentence now says when the change arrives (§6 rule 7).

- 2026-09-30 (TD-247, the designer; Paul the same day: *ideally we would not need [a manager] and all of its required jobs could be scripted or given to the agent host*): *the manager is a session, not code* gains its next step — the four jobs the brief still gave it that read fields and scripts (the cadence check, the held-path check, the relay, the outcome chase) are §6 rules 10–12 and rule 4's owed clause, and the judgement that is left is a **seat on call** (`on_call: true`, §4.9), filled on §6 rule 3's `team` trigger. The anchor's read with two Sonnet readers (TD-247's *Why*) found a manager already every team's default and not required, and manager-ao-1 at 686k of context finding nothing each hour. Not built: TD-258 (the policies), TD-259 (the seat).
- 2026-10-01 (TD-259 slice 5, grinder-ao-2): the manager on call's brief is its own package template, `briefs/manager_on_call.md`, composed in place of `manager.md` when the definition says `on_call` (`repoconfig.ON_CALL_BRIEFS`, `Role.compose(on_call=…)`). Two files and not one that branches on its record: a seat reads its brief at every fill, and the standing brief's round, wind-down and chase are about 2,500 words it would pay for each time and must not act on. `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md` and the primer's row are held paths and follow with the default's flip.
## 4.8a Who is calling: identity on one host

- 2026-09-17: the outage that motivates *observe before enforce* — one merged RPC change locked every worker out of `ao progress` for 31 minutes; a wrong ancestry rule would do the same with a worse cause, hence the `off | observe | enforce` modes and `observe` as the default for the release that introduced them.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-077; decided by Paul): the section was added. Until this date every gate, §4.10's marks included, was what §4.8 says of the control gate — a guard, not a security boundary: a session could send as another session, and as the person, by changing or unsetting one variable (`AGENTORC_SESSION`), and the hook RPC trusted its `session` parameter the same way. Between hosts §4.4a's channel rule had always applied; this section brought it to one host.
- 2026-09-19: the host agent began reading `#{pane_tty}` beside `#{pane_pid}` in the tick's pane list (the third signal needs it).
- 2026-09-19 (first CI push of the build): CI taught the third precondition of the cgroup clause — that the agent must be the service's own process, its parent systemd. A test runner sits inside *some* `.service` together with the person standing in it, and so does any agent a worker starts from inside an `ao` pane; with the clause on there, the person read as *unknown*.
- 2026-09-19 (the build's red-team): `host_files` was found on the never-gated list — a session that left its `caller` out read a checkout as the person. It was taken off the list and judged like any act, and a test now holds every name on the list to the rule that a never-gated read decides nothing on its `caller`.
- 2026-09-19 (step 2, built): the card's identity mark (a `<span>` in its own token colour, never pressable, never the amber needs-you ring, newest alarm on hover); the Inbox row under *Needs you* with one control, **Acknowledge** (`identity_ack`); `ao status -v` printing the mode and the detached-process check once per host; the Org's teams line saying *identity: observe* or *identity: off* and nothing under `enforce`.
- 2026-09-19: the test suite passed `caller=<id>` from the pytest process in 112 calls on that date — the count behind "over a hundred", and the reason the fixtures' agent runs `identity: off`.
- 2026-09-20: kmaster's tmux server was replaced under a running agent, which moved the cgroup check. The host agent now re-reads the server's pid and start time every `ID_RECHECK` seconds and recomputes the check only when the pid has moved.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-089, decided and built): the CLI's no-host-agent case — exit 3's sentence decided by `AGENTORC_SESSION`, then by a `ppid` walk over `/proc/<pid>/environ`, used for the sentence only and never as an identity.
- 2026-09-20 (Paul's direction): the alarm row's answers were redesigned. Paul read the one-control row for what it was: *"Acknowledge" seems like a dismiss; identity items should have options like "Suspend agent" or "Log TD", and should go through the chain of command — techlead if defined, then to me.* The row gained four controls (Dismiss, Log TD, Suspend, Open); *Acknowledge* was renamed **Dismiss** with the wire name `identity_ack` kept (it is in `NODE_ACTS`; a rename there is a protocol change that buys a person nothing). The trail word changed from *acknowledged by you* to *dismissed by you*, built the same day at the home and on a node's routed act alike; the control's label is the page's half.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-077 a2, built): **Suspend** — `suspend`, the `suspended: {at, by, why}` mark, the kill without wrap-up, no `unsuspend`, and the three person-only roads out.
- 2026-09-20 (review of PR #301): two rules came out of the Suspend review — the mark is written at the home wherever it is pressed (`suspend` is a `HOME_EDITS` call for a node), because a node that wrote the mark itself would kill the session and have the home's next copy wipe the mark, leaving it stopped, unmarked and free for any session to take its name again; and `remove` is a person's road too, since the acting gate would otherwise let a controller walk it for an `unattended` member, freeing the name and letting the suspect be started again unmarked.
- 2026-09-20 (designed with the above, not built): **Who answers first — the chain of command** — a techlead is offered the alarm first, bounded by `ALARM_ANSWER` (15 minutes), only on a host that enforces and carries no person; buildable only after TD-075's `techlead` grant and step 4's `person: false`. On kmaster, which carries a person, every alarm goes to the person as before.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-077 b, built): **Log TD** — `identity_log`, the message to the record's first live controller, and the debt extension: an entry from the person marked `handed` is a debt on its addressee, `MailEntry.owes` gains the case, `owed()` counts inbox entries too. Before this, TD-079's debt existed only on a session's own question to the person, so work the *person* handed a session had no debt to settle.
- 2026-09-21 (review of PR #318): a handed entry that still owes cannot be deleted by the person's `inbox_delete` — it is the first debt-bearing mail that RPC can reach — so the delete is refused in words; the debt ends by an outcome or by the person's Dismiss, which tells the session.
- 2026-09-21 (built): a container node's identity mode is said at the home in its `nodes:` entry; `ao host up` carries the word into the node's `local:` and restarts the node's agent when what it wrote has changed.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-115, grinder-ao-1): **a hook just after its pane ended** became its session's for `PANE_GONE_GRACE` (ten seconds). Found live the same night: a techlead seat's last hook at `/exit` twice raised *outside claimed ao-agentorc-techlead-ao-1 on hook* (03:33:22Z and 03:55:29Z, 2026-09-23), each within twenty seconds of the run's end and a few after the pane left the tick's list — the ending process's hook connected when the walk met no pane pid, and the gone pane was no longer on the list for its session id or terminal to match. Once the tick closes idle seats and members (TD-103 slices (3) and (4)), every such close would risk the same alarm. The events queue's hole was written under *What this does not stop* in place of a build.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-225, grinder-ao-1): the grace also starts when the list shows **another pane under the same record**. Every identity alarm on record was a session's own hook from outside, most at a restart or supersede: those reuse the session's name, so the record never left the list and its old pane was never kept as gone, and the old run's `SessionEnd` matched neither pane. A hook that still alarms now logs its peer, the record's listed pane and the gone one with its age, to tell a hook later than the grace from one that was never the session's.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-362, grinder-ao-1; filed from TD-360, its premise read on kmaster by the techlead): **a pane's own cgroup scope is the fourth signal**. tmux, built with systemd support, starts each pane in a `tmux-spawn-<uuid>.scope`, so no pane was in `agentorc-agent.service` and the detached check's *unknown* clause never fired. A session's process that shed ancestry, session id and terminal, such as a Claude Code background command orphaned by its setsid'd tool shell, read as *outside*: the person. It is now matched by its pane's scope, as a gone pane's hook is since TD-360. The *unknown* clause widens only to the scopes of our server's panes that no live record holds: a listed pane that is no record's, and a gone pane inside the grace. It was not widened to every `tmux-spawn-*` scope, since a person's own tmux server makes scopes of the same name (the techlead's narrowing of the grinder's steer). The sentence that `KillMode=process` keeps every pane inside the service was corrected.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-360, grinder-ao-1; filed by grinder-ao-2 from TD-339's live check; the techlead's answer to its steer): **a gone pane is matched by its own cgroup scope too**. Every ao-grind restart after TD-341 still alarmed *outside claimed <itself> on hook*. The peer's pid was below the new pane's, so it was the old run's `SessionEnd`, spawned as its pane was killed, not the new run's first hook. Claude Code starts a hook setsid'd with no terminal, so the orphan carried neither the gone pane's session id nor its terminal. On kmaster each pane runs in a `tmux-spawn-<uuid>.scope` of its own, which the orphan keeps. The grinder's default, refusing a hook that would be ignored with no alarm, was set aside: it classifies by the claim, and §4.8a never refuses silently.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-341, grinder-ao-1): **taking the name moves the old pane to the gone table at once**. ## 4.9a Winding down: a team that runs out of work

- 2026-10-09 (TD-110, the designer): **the home's note says more** — rule 9's wind-down note gains claims left, restarts by why, standing alarms, open questions to the person and each profile's usage against the start's reading (`teams.<team>.usage_at_start`); no `ao team report`, no board line. Built by TD-468.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-053 step 4, the anchor): **the three guards against false exhaustion were settled: one kept, one moved to the home, one dropped.** The design had left them unset, to be chosen against a running team: the manager re-reading the ledger before a team-wide wind-down, and an exhaustion declared soon after a start reported rather than acted on. By this date rule 9 (TD-240) had made *finished* the home's reading, with `FINISHED_SETTLE`, and rules 6 and 8 read the lanes themselves. So the second opinion is the home's and not the manager's. The early bound was dropped: the running teams' early declarations were correct (grinder-sam-1 at 90 s after a full search; samscrape-grind-anchor at 24 s on an empty lane), so a bound would have sent the person right answers. `RESTART_EARLY` stays the restart's alone.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-384 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): the grinder brief's paragraph built. *A decided board line comes first*: claim `board:<key>`, read the line, then one of the four outcomes, each a PR that closes the line with `board_edit.py done --why`, then `ao progress done board:<key> --pr <n>`. The brief's *Out of work* counts an unclaimed decided line as work.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-380, the designer): a team is not out of work while a decided line is unclaimed; *What a session does with a decided line* — claim `board:<key>`, then close it, do it, ledger it for its owner, or ask back, by PR.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-321, the designer; found by the anchor in TD-249's live check, PR #1032; Paul chose to count the work rather than split every slice into its own entry): **a merged slice became work the record sees.** The rule of 2026-09-30 already said *an entry worked in slices is new work when its PR is a new one*, and nothing could write it: `progress` holds one entry per reference, `done` closes it, and a derived `done` is refused beside a declared claim (§9 invariant 10), so grinder-ao-2's run that merged #1025 and #1027 on TD-309 left `done: []` and stood one run from a *repeat*. Decided: a claim carries `slices`, written by `ao progress done <ref> --pr <n> --slice` and by the tick from a derived `done` on the claim's reference; a run's `done` holds them; a reference with a new slice is not `left`. Both writers, because a slice merged seconds before the `restart` is declared (#1027: 25 seconds) is not yet derived, and a session that forgets the flag is still counted. The build is TD-325.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-325 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the record and the reading built. `ProgressEntry.slices` (`add_slice`, one item per `pr`, a derived one turned declared when the session says it), kept by a re-claim and by the last slice's plain `done`; `rpc_progress(slice=True)` (`_slice`: refused but for a declared `done` with a PR on a declared claim, the claim left as it was); `_note_review` holds a derived merged PR on a declared claim as a `derived` slice; `_reported(s, now)` puts each slice since `created` in `done` as `{ref, pr, slice: true}` and keeps a reference with a new one out of `left`; `_restart_reading` reads a declared slice as the run's word. `tests/test_slices_done.py`.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-325 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the words built. `ao progress done <ref> --pr <n> --slice` (refused at the CLI without `--pr` or with another action, sent only when asked, its reply's line printed); the Focus panel's Reports row prints *claimed · slices #a, #b*, each a link from the field; `ao status -v` a *slices:* line, a derived slice marked `~`; the grinder preset's brief and `ao --skill` say it in one sentence. TD-325 and TD-321 archived.

## 4.9c Flows: the path an entry takes through a team

- 2026-10-08 (TD-410, the designer): Apply creates a manager on call the run lacks as a Start does, closed with no pane. TD-413 builds it.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-400, grinder-ao-2): **a seat the run lacks is a difference** built. `teamrun.differences` drops the `in_checkout` skip, so a seat launch (the techlead, the anchor, a `seats:` entry) with no current record is a `start` marked `seat`. A live team that runs no flow is compared on its seats alone, through `flow_changed` and `flow_view`, which the Org page's flows read now makes for every team. `apply`'s start of the anchor reads `checkout_held` first. If the checkout is not free, Apply makes the held create with the reason, and its line reads *<seat>: waits — <why>*. `teamrun.definition_changed` picks *definition changed* over *flow changed*, for the header, `ao team list` and `ao team flow`. Settled at the build: `ao team flow <team>`, bare and with `--apply`, now reads and applies a team that lists no flows, on its seats. Only a flow named on such a team is refused, where the bare form was refused before. The scratch home showed the mark, Apply and the held record; rule 3's fill after the checkout is cleaned is TD-386's, not driven here, since it launches an agent.

A stage now carries its **stage brief**, and a member's brief has three layers — the role template (mechanics only; its path paragraphs moved to the stage briefs and, for a team with no flow, to `<role>.stage.md`), the stage brief (the path), the repo's supplement (repo facts only); `designer` became a preset with a template, since the built-in `td` names it. A switch, or a definition edit that changes what a start compiles, is applied by the client: sit-outs wound down by the home and marked `closed_for: {why: flow}` (rule 8 and `wound_down` pass over them), starts as Members… add starts, and a **relaunch** that replaces the member's launch record (and re-records its `brief`) and asks for rule 7's restart; an edit to a stage brief is rule 7's *brief changed* as it stands. Fable's two rounds on the rework: round 1 found that a seat fill replays its launch record (so the relaunch had to be the launch record, not a kept copy), that `{flow}` naming members made every Members… add a false *flow changed*, that the agent cannot refuse a flow value (it reads no definition), that a sat-out designer's lane would raise rule 8's *team start*, and that node members could be restarted on their word; it also cut a supplement heuristic, a `package:` path scheme and per-member hover. Round 2 found that `closed_for` is cleared by every close and so has to be written by the home after its own wrap-up close, that a relaunch must re-record `brief` or rule 7 watches the old stage brief, and that the merge must be able to remove `review`.

An org overlay's `review:` is not named: the overlay is read by every repo's teams, a team with no flow among them, so it is no repo's to delete.

A record with none (a node member's: the home never reads a node's files) is compared on its lane and reader alone. A member still winding down from a sit-out reads no difference until the home has closed it, and is then started.
