## 4.10 Messages between sessions (first part: through the person inbox)

- 2026-10-09 (TD-459, the designer): **a lapsed cache is started again, not rung** — a member the doorbell has decided to ring, idle past `CACHE_LIFETIME` (1 h) with a context over `CACHE_FLOOR` (100k), is restarted on its brief by rule 7's tick restart under its precondition, `why: cache`; the restart is the wake; seats, nodes and a person's session left out; `read_when`'s idle sentence says which. ADR 2026-10-08 option 3, Paul's decision. Built by TD-467.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-347 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): **the bell** built — `doorbell_held`, `mail.DOORBELL_HELD`, the ring judged at its turn's `Stop`, a read or a person's act lifting it, and *doorbell held · n unread rings* on `ao status -v`'s mail line. Decided in the build: a ring's count is remembered before the line is typed, because the tool's `UserPromptSubmit` for the ring landed before the submit confirmation returned and the ring's turn went uncounted (found by the build's own test, intermittently); a ring is judged only once its prompt has been seen, so a stray `idle` between the ring and its turn counts nothing; and the run lives in the host agent, so its restart starts the count again rather than holding a bell on a run it did not see.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-343, the designer): **a ring is answered by a read, or the bell stops** — after `DOORBELL_HELD` rings each answered by a turn that read none of its mail, `doorbell_held` silences the bell until the session reads or a person acts toward it. Not built — TD-347.
- 2026-09-13 (§10): manager-to-manager messaging was closed "by accident rather than by decision" — the `control` grant (named `orchestrate` until TD-055 step 3) plus `controllers` was the only gate, and keystrokes (`ao send`) the only delivery, so messaging had been folded into control. That accident was the tell that led to the split: of the four kinds of traffic only manager-to-worker worked; a worker reached upward through `progress`/`findings` (declarations, not a channel); two managers were peers and TD-036's gate refused them each other; two workers coordinated by side effects (a ledger row, a branch name, a PR claiming the reference). Diagnosis: "a conflation, not four missing features."
- 2026-09-14: section written. First draft said a message never starts a turn (an idle session stayed idle with mail waiting; a sender needing work to begin used `send`). Paul called it too blunt — it left mail useful only to a session already blocked in `ao wait`, i.e. managers, and two workers could message each other into inboxes neither would read — so a message may wake, metered by a budget.
- 2026-09-14 (Paul): "Where the line actually is, since it is thinner than 'mailbox versus pane'" — naming the delivery mechanism made the distinction look like an implementation detail; mediation and attribution are the two properties actually claimed.
- 2026-09-14 (Paul): "Portability: the mailbox is core, surfacing it is the adapter's job" — mail joins the adapter contract rather than assuming one tool's shape.
- 2026-09-14 draft: the first draft said the wake budget was restored by a controller's send and by any turn mail did not start; review found the loop that permits (worker mails manager, manager's `ao wait` returns, manager `send`s, the worker's `send`-started turn "resets" it, worker mails again, every turn counting as work) and cut both.
- 2026-09-14 draft: let a worker message "a person" and amended invariant 5 to allow it, but gave no edge that reached one. Fable review 2026-09-16 replaced it with the per-org person inbox rather than inventing a *creator* edge.
- 2026-09-16 (Fable review, round one): the wake budget defined as mail-caused wakes in a rolling window; a `wait` returning because of mail counts, so managers are not the unmetered half of every loop. Round one charged `ao wait`'s mail returns, but `ao wait` ran wholly in the CLI, watched only the caller's members and never its own inbox, and the host agent never learned why it returned — a manager out of budget would have been woken by every message.
- 2026-09-16 (second review): the host agent decides each wake at the reachable moment; `ao wait` becomes the host-agent RPC `wait`. Second review also removed `out_of_work` from the "pending stop beats mail" list — the first 2026-09-16 text listed it there and contradicted §4.9a.
- 2026-09-16 (rounds two and three): a `send` made inside a mail-caused turn was charged to the controller's budget and refused when spent, with the wrap-up prompt exempt.
- 2026-09-16 (fourth review, reversing the second): a controller's `send` is never charged. The rule only doubled the price of a cycle already bounded by the manager's wake charge, added a refusal mode (a manager mid-turn could not instruct its own worker), an exemption, a definition of where a manager's turn ends, and a hole — `ao wait --timeout 0` put a manager outside a mail-caused turn and let it `send` for free.
- 2026-09-16 (fourth review): "a decision not to wake advances nothing" — the `mail_decided` mark stays put when the budget is spent, otherwise a refilled budget would never ring for mail that landed while spent. Also: a refusal at the person inbox names `user_attention.md` with a `Due:` date, so it is a redirect, not a dead end.
- 2026-09-16 (Paul): "Other tools' own messaging is left alone" — Claude Code's cross-session `SendMessage` is not switched off; people already use it (ADR decisions/2026-09-16-agent-messaging-prior-art.md).
- 2026-09-16 (Paul): the doorbell — a fixed line the host agent rings, never the sender's words — was added because the rules said a message *may wake* but named no way to do it for the one adapter that exists.
- 2026-09-16 draft: had the `Stop` hook *block* the stop while mail was unread. Fable's review cut it: it saved one paste and a few seconds, and cost a synchronous round trip to the host agent on every `Stop` of every session (`Stop` is fire-and-forget with a 3 s timeout, `hook.py`) plus `stop_hook_active` handling and stop-beats-mail checks in a second place — "the one change that could slow every session, for the smallest gain."
- 2026-09-16: Paul asked for a reminder when a session keeps reporting with mail unread; review reshaped it from a count of reports (which misses a worker that reports only at claim and at done) to a line on every `ao` command's output.
- 2026-09-16 (TD-052 step 2): a session's `reply` to a person's message, naming no addressee, lands in the person inbox.
- 2026-09-16 (TD-052 step 3, as built): mail's half of the wait cursor is the `mail_decided` watermark; a wait returns mail as headers only; a person's session in `wait` is never returned by mail; the `wakes` list `{at, cause, charged, covered}` on the record.
- 2026-09-19: an `ask` to the person stopped expiring — until then it expired read or not, like any other kind.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-052 step 7, as built): doorbell on every tick as its own task; `wrapup_at` stamped by a `send` marked `wrapup` (Wrap up on the card and Focus, `ao team stop`) and cleared by the next plain send; `_decide_wake` recorded `via: doorbell`; one ring per idle stretch; the retry into an empty composer only; `doorbell_failed` (`{at, error}`) in `ao status -v`; the line is `sessionorc.mail.unread_line`.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-324, grinder-ao-1; Paul the same day: *change the inbox limit to be something large, like 200 for need/steers and 1k for fyi*): the person inbox's depths became two counts. Until this date one set — every entry unread or an open question — was held to 200 in all and 100 from one sender, and a person's read set no `read_at`, so a note counted until it was dismissed: on 2026-10-04 techlead-ao-1's verdict on held PR #1040 was refused with 100 slots held by its own notes, and the anchor dismissed them by hand. Now the open questions (`ask`, `steer`, `conflict`) refuse a question and a pass-up at 200 and 100 from one sender, and every other entry refuses any other send at 1000 and 500 from one sender (the per-sender figure the anchor's, so one looping sender leaves room). §4.10's figures had also said *20 from one sender* since 2026-09-28, when the code went to 100; they were corrected with this change.
- Until TD-090 a `/compact` made a healthy idle session read as `stalled?`, which got no doorbell.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-327, grinder-ao-2; found by grinder-ao-1 pressing TD-057's live check): `ao wait` timing out with mail still unread, mail it did not wake on or an earlier wait reported, printed *nothing unread — end your turn* above the unread line that contradicted it. The poll that found nothing is now one that also leaves nothing unread: the end-the-turn line needs the reply's unread count at 0 as well.

## §4.10 (middle: what a person is asked; system notes; one way of being closed; pause; deleting is declining; suggested answers; which to send)

- 2026-09-18: Paul asked for suggested answers on questions put to a person (became TD-070).
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069, decided by Paul; Sonnet design review the same day): needed / steering / FYI introduced. Before this date an `ask` to the person carried the same bound as any other — 24 hours by default — and expired read or not, so the person was shown a countdown and the asker then moved on. Paul: *either user input is legitimately needed or it isn't.* The two cases want opposite rules, and one kind with a timer blurred them.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069 step 0): two new gates in the send path — an `ask` or `steer` naming the person names nobody else, and a `conflict` never names the person. Until then nothing but the recipient cap stopped the person being one addressee among several.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069): `asker_gone` is new with the needed rule. Until then only an *addressee's* going closed an entry; an `ask` that cannot expire needs the other half, or a forgotten worker's questions stand forever. The resume rewrite (earlier in §4.10) had already moved a superseded record's questions to the new id, which is why a superseded record is not a gone asker.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069): from this date the person inbox's two depths (200, 20 per sender) count every entry that is unread or an open `ask`/`steer`, one set, each entry once; before, reading freed the slot.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069): `closed_reason` introduced as the one way of being closed. The fields that existed before it (`closed_by`, `closed_at`, `expired_at`) are kept and still written. Entries written before this date have no `closed_reason`; they read as closed when `closed_by` or `expired_at` is set, which was the rule until then.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069, decided by Paul): Pause on a `steer` in the person inbox; `inbox_pause` RPC; Resume moves `bound` later by the held time and clears `paused_at` in one step. A `steer` gets no Snooze because snooze and pause on one row invite the wrong press.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069): Dismiss removes an entry outright "as `inbox_delete` does today"; Delete on an open `ask`/`steer` became a decline instead of a strip.
- 2026-09-19 (TD-069): the advice line (*you have n open asks to the person: is this one needed, or a steer?*) added as a line from the home, not a gate; the reason given: a worker that marks every preference *needed* brings the old (pre-TD-069) inbox back, with no timer to clear it.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-070; Sonnet design review the same day): suggested answers designed — `answers` up to `ANSWERS_MAX` (4), `ANSWER_CAP` (80), `Cf` stripping, shape check on raw JSON, `answer` index checked against `text`, `--pick <n>` between sessions, no confirm on a press. The observation that a wrong press is "true of a mistyped Reply today" was the argument for no confirm.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-143, the designer): a person's Drop from Focus tells the session by a `system` note, as Go with it and Pause do.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-213, the designer): **a question about a reference outlives its asker.** Until this date closing or forgetting a record closed every open `ask` and `steer` it had put to the person (`asker_gone`, 2026-09-19), whatever the question was about. On 2026-09-28 at 06:56Z ao-grind's wind-down closed grinder-ao-1 and designer-ao-1 and with them the steer `m-43f5e3922686` (TD-149 (1)) and the ask `m-d20bbf79bdbe` (TD-180), before Paul had seen either (Paul: *I do not see a steering item from ao-grind in the inbox*); he answered TD-180 to the anchor, which wrote it into the ledger. Now a question whose envelope carries `about` is orphaned and stands; an orphaned `steer` waits on the person from its bound instead of lapsing; the asker's name coming back adopts it; the answer to an orphaned one goes on the board and to the lease holder, as a board reply does (TD-126). Considered and not taken: the wind-down refusing the close, the manager's brief boarding each question, the home writing the board line at the close, the question held under the team.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-215, grinder-ao-1): the home's half of the orphaned question is built — `_asker_gone` orphans an open question whose `about` has a reference's shape (`orphaned` and `adopted_at` on the entry), an orphaned `steer` loses its bound instead of lapsing, a create under the asker's id adopts it (at the create, and on the sweep for a record the home did not see created), and the adopted `steer`'s lapse note names its default. Until TD-216 builds where an answer goes, a person's reply or *Go with it* on an orphaned question is refused in words; Delete declines it.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-216 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the answer's road is built — Reply, a suggested answer and *Go with it* on an orphaned question write the board's second add (`agentorc: answer … (from m-…)`) and a `handed` note to each lease holder, replacing TD-215's refusal. Two build facts written into §4.10: `orphaned.repo` falls back to the record's directory (a session in a main checkout names no `repo`), and the board must be in the home's registry, so an asker on a node is refused. Pause on an orphaned `steer` stays refused. A holder note the mail gate refuses is said beside the committed line (the result and the entry's outcome text) and never refuses the press; the gate is all-or-nothing, so one refusal among several holders sends to none (review of #717). The row is slice 2.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-216 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the orphaned question's row is built — the standing (`sessionorc.mail.orphan_standing`, `LEASE_TTL` moved beside it), an orphaned `steer` counted under *Needs you* once its bound is cleared, no Pause and no Open, Snooze once no clock runs, the refusal drawn on the row; `ao inbox` prints the standing to a person, and `ao msg --reply-to` and the page's Reply route print the home's `note` for an answer that left no reply entry. Two build facts written into §4.5a: the standing's words for two holders and for an asker with no repo.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-271, the designer): **An orphaned `steer` does not lapse** reversed to **lapses to its default** — at the bound the entry closes `lapsed`, the adopted form's note is written into the closed asker's mailbox, and the lapse is rule 8 work; nothing becomes the person's question (TD-213's cleared bound stands only for entries it already cleared). *The name coming back adopts it* keeps the closed record's mail at every superseding create (a Restart's and a fill's did); *Where the person's answer goes* makes the asker's own name a holder — the closed record gets the `handed` note too.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-296 #14, grinder-ao-1): the question a picked reply closes keeps the reply's `answer` on every copy. Until this date only the reply carried it (the orphaned question's path aside), so the Inbox's *Waiting on them* row, which reads the question, said *you answered* where §4.5a promised the answer; a live look of 2026-10-03 found it.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-296 #15, grinder-ao-2): rule 9's `system` note — *<team> finished and the host agent wound it down* — carries its team in the envelope's `team`. Until this date a `system` note carried none, so the Inbox filed cm-grind's wind-down note, naming #102–#106, under *No team*.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-304, the designer): the closed subset's lists nest by indent — a board item's detail block carries bullets under a bullet, and a flat list would print the inner ones as text. Not built.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-312): built — `render._list`: an item indented deeper than the one above opens a list inside it, a shallower one closes back out.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-070, grinder-ao-2): `ao msg --reply-to <id> --pick <n> "text"` sends the picked answer, a blank line, then the text, on one reply with `answer` kept, and the home accepts that shape. Until this date `--pick` refused any text (*sends the answer itself: leave the text out*), and the home took only the answer word for word, so a reader that wanted to pick and add a line sent two messages or typed the answer and lost the index (manager-ao-1's friction note, 2026-09-21).

## §4.10 Messages between sessions (tail: the Inbox queue, outcomes, kinds, bounds, lifecycle, surface)

- 2026-10-07 (TD-371, the designer; Paul the same day, answering a snoozed look: *It seems very odd to have to hit "unsnooze" first — I was expecting the snoozed items to just get their due dates moved … and they would still show the recommended answers*): **a snooze is a date, not a hiding place** — a snoozed entry or state row keeps every control its kind has, a press there answers and ends the snooze, **Snooze ▾** on it picks a new time with *now*, and **Unsnooze** (2026-09-19 to 2026-10-07) is retired. The build is TD-373.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-319 slice 3, grinder-ao-2): **the Telegram card and the `?row=` landing built.** The design said the landing scrolls to the row and marks it. It did not say what to do when the Inbox's rail picks, which this browser remembers, hide that row, or when the row is snoozed. The build clears the picks in that case and opens the snoozed box: the person followed a link to that one row. §4.10 says so. The card's two help paragraphs, **tell me on Telegram** and **Send a test**, were written by the build, as the entry said they would be.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-319 slice 2, grinder-ao-1; the techlead's read of #1086 asked for it): **the burst's quiet is ten minutes from its *and more* line.** *Until the window clears* could be read as *until fewer than six sends sit in the last ten minutes*. That reading sends another *and more* each time the oldest send leaves the window while rows keep coming, which is a stream of messages saying *open the Inbox*. The build reads it as ten minutes after the *and more* line, when everything sent before it has left the window, and §4.10 says so.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-319 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): **the send built, and a backlog is never told.** The design said when a row is told but not what becomes of one already standing when the switch is turned on, or that stood through a home that was down: told at once, six at a time behind the burst's *and more*, would be a message about something the person could already see. The build tells a row only while its hold ended within `NOTIFY_LATE` (5 min), which covers a restart and leaves out a backlog, and §4.10 says so.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-092, the designer; Paul the same day: *we use telegram elsewhere, make sense to keep using it here*): **the Telegram channel designed.** The entry of 2026-09-20 offered web push or an ntfy topic and the review of 2026-09-22 endorsed ntfy; Paul chose Telegram, which samscrape's host health check already pushes to. What the design added to the entry's rule (*only what Needs you counts, on a transition into it, never twice, not while the page is focused*): the home is the sender, since the Inbox's sections are composed in the UI, which runs wherever it is installed (§10) and so may run more than once; told is narrower than counted — what stops a session or a team, not a promote row that would ring at every merge; a minute's hold; a message carries no text a session wrote; and the token never enters the host agent's environment — a child under `doppler run` holds it for one request. The build is TD-319.
- 2026-09-13 (§10): Paul's rule for the two-controller conflict — the person is the tie-break — is what the per-thread exchange bound generalises.
- 2026-09-14: the first text said a message `about` a session was "visible" to its other controllers, which could not be squared with *nobody reads another session's inbox*; review asked which, and a copy (needing no new read path) was chosen. `ao wait` landed 2026-09-14 (TD-049).
- 2026-09-16 (Paul): the three-stage lifecycle of an entry — unread, read, pruned — with "reading does not delete" as its first rule.
- 2026-09-16 (prior art, Claude Code's cross-session messaging): delivery damping (identical-repeat refusal, per-sender rate limit) and the fixed `ao inbox` header naming each sender's standing were taken from it. The first damping text said *drops*; the fourth review changed it to *refuses with a reason*, since the mailbox bound already forbade silent drops.
- 2026-09-16 (Sonnet review): a `conflict` is an `ask` for every rule in the section; a person's message into a thread is never counted and resets it.
- 2026-09-16 (second review): "both participants" did not cover a copied thread or a `conflict` (three or more participants), so the exact counting rules were written — per root on every record, first reply free and closing, later replies count as notes, `reply_to` must be in the replier's own inbox, refusal on sender's or a named addressee's tally, copies never refuse.
- 2026-09-16 (third review): a copy never sinks a send; `copies_failed` recorded on the sender's entry.
- 2026-09-16 (fourth review): `sends` recorded on the record — the 2026-09-16 `conflict` had asked a session to quote "each instruction verbatim and who it came from", which it could only guess, since the host agent then recorded nothing of a `send`'s caller. Same round: the pair tally became a rolling window rather than a lifetime count; the `ask` bound is wall-clock, never turns; an open `ask`/`steer`/`conflict` is never pruned; the **Message** control was added (Reply alone left a person able to originate mail only from `ao msg`); the mail rules displace rather than add in `ao --skill`'s 120-line budget.
- 2026-09-16 (Sonnet round two): `sends` moves with a resume, since a `conflict` cites entries by id.
- 2026-09-16 (TD-052 step 1): every session keeps an `outbox` so sender-side marks are on its own record. TD-052 step 3: `ao wait` became the host agent's `wait` RPC and gained mail as a second return. TD-052 step 8: `inbox_delete` refused to every session, its own inbox included. Retention, damping numbers and the exchange bounds were left unset "like every number in this section" until step 6.
- 2026-09-17: first night any team ran with mail — eight hours, a lead over three free-pick grinders. Measured (TD-052 step 5): 55–65 entries per inbox, all `note` but one `ask` and eight `reply`s; busiest pair 48 reply-less entries; no thread past 2; 19 unread at most; bodies 1.6 KB average, longest at the 4 KB cap; the lead woken by mail 31 times (about 4/h); the one `ask` expired unanswered at its 30-minute bound; nothing reached the person inbox; records 106–249 KB before pruning. Same night: the manager was blocked four times behind Claude Code's *Held message from another session* panel, each answered by hand with `ao keys`.
- 2026-09-18 (TD-052 step 6): the numbers were set from that measurement — thread 40 (measured 2), pair 300/24 h (measured 48 in 8 h, ~145/day; "this one is twice"; separate from the thread constant because one number for both would have refused a healthy lead and grinder by their second day), mailbox 100 (measured 19), person inbox 200 and 20 per sender (unmeasured, provisional), retention 12 h (TD-066), wake budget 30/h (measured 4).
- 2026-09-19: the `steer` kind was added to the kinds table ("a preference, with the default I will take"). Review of TD-069 step 0: on a resume, `from` is rewritten on the copies the conversation itself owns (the outbox and the person inbox's copies), so an `ask` to the person is not closed `asker_gone` a day later.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-079; Paul, after a day with the page): *Reading an item should never change it, or make it disappear; every item should require an answer of some sort — snooze and dismiss are answers.* Before this, a state row vanished when its record's need went away by another road — "it disappeared when I read it" was opening the row's session, which resumed it. The attention trail was added so the home records the ending. Same day the identity alarm's *Acknowledge* was renamed *Dismiss* (wire name `identity_ack` unchanged), and *dismissed by you* became the trail's word for it. Found that day: five `note`s from 2026-09-17 and -18 sat in a folded, uncounted FYI, unseen, one reporting a broken shared venv — hence the second, quiet FYI count and the self-opening FYI section. Paul on outcomes: *if I give an answer, how do I know the work was completed — or do we just assume it gets done because we have a manager?* — hence the outcome debt, `--outcome … --for`, `OUTCOMES_OWED_MAX` 10, and (step 3) the `owed:` line in `ao status -v`.
- 2026-09-20 (TD-081, built by its step 1): resume under the same name. Until then `_take_name` started the new record with an empty inbox, outbox, tallies and `sends` — right for a new conversation taking over a dead one's name, wrong for a resume — while `_supersede` looked only for *another* record in `exited` holding the tool's session id, and so found nothing.
- 2026-09-20 (envelope `team`): from this date the home stamps the sender's `team` on every envelope; entries sent before it show under *No team*.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-088): the trail's *the session exited* / *the session was closed* words come from the record's own state beneath any word an act wrote; an `unpushed` row is a row of an exited record, so only a close ends it that way.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-064; Paul: *refuse them*): `crossSessionInbound: refuse` written into the settings layer on an unattended launch, after the held-message panel blocked a manager again that day; the classifier had read the panel as the generic *idle*, so the adapter's screen rules now name it `needs-you` with a `question`.
- TD-069: snooze on a person-inbox entry (`snoozed_until`, `inbox_snooze`); its open gap — a state row has no mail entry to carry a snooze — was closed by `attention_snoozed_until` / `attention_snooze` in the home-owned trail store (TD-079).
- TD-072 gave the principle *briefs are skimmed, a refusal is not*, which is why the outcome debt is enforced by `ao` replies, `ao progress none` and Ready to close rather than by a brief alone.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-039, the designer): the conflict-specific judgement written as *A conflict, worked* — the worker stops on the contested step and waits, the first reply from either controller is the ruling and the worker is never the arbiter, a resolved conflict is a thread and not a `finding`, and nobody writes a board line: the escalation on a `bound_hit` or an expired bound is an `ask` to the person carrying the thread, where the bounded-exchange rule had said *the refused sender writes the `user_attention.md` line itself* since 2026-09-16 (c3314df; the 2026-09-14 text said only that the exchange *becomes a `user_attention.md` line*) — changed because the Inbox became the queue of what waits on the person (TD-079, 2026-09-20) and the board the place for dated items (TD-069 step 4, TD-125). Steered to Paul as a default. The test the entry's *done when* asks for is TD-134.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-072, the designer): an unread `note` or `reply` on an `exited` or `closed` record ages out on the retention window from the exit — *never ages out* became *never ages out while the record lives* — the window and not the exit itself because resume carries mail forward; open questions untouched.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-127, in a cloud session with Paul): *How a message to a person is written*. Paul's first thought had been a *details* option showing the original under a summary the sender humanises; the anchor's recommendation — one text whose first paragraph is what the person needs, a fold for the rest, markdown rendered safely, the presets saying so — was confirmed with two changes from the session: the renderer is the UI's own closed-subset renderer rather than a vendored library behind a sanitiser (*no raw HTML* as a property of the code), and a link, the one pressable thing text can make, is drawn only when absolute, `http(s)` and off-origin, with its host shown after its text. Two backstops were added so an unshaped message still reads as a row and mail is never refused for style: a fold at the last sentence before 300 characters, and a one-line warning from `ao msg` at 60 words. The build is TD-138 (the renderer, the fold) and TD-139 (the presets, the placeholder, the warning). Compared 2026-09-25 with the designer's PR #532, written the same afternoon: the two agree on the shape rule, the fold, the own renderer and the presets; two things were taken from it — the Focus Inbox panel's entries fold the same way, and the placeholder is the Message / Reply dialog's, not Focus's prompt composer's; what it lacked and this design keeps: the backstop fold, the `ao msg` warning, the off-origin link rule and the host after the link.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-138): the renderer and the fold built; `ao inbox` still prints the source as written, and `ao msg`'s 60-word warning is TD-139's.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-158, the designer): **When it is read** — one sentence per addressee from `mail.read_when(record, kind, now)`, in the doorbell's own precedence, shown in the composer and on every `ao msg` reply; a person's message refills the budget so *budget spent* is a session's sentence only. Refusing a note to a seat was rejected: it would turn information into a question to get it delivered.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-139, grinder-ao-2): the senders' half built — the paragraph in the same words in the `techlead`, `manager` and `grinder` presets, `ao msg`'s warning (`FIRST_PARA_WORDS`, counted on the Inbox row's own `fold`, printed to stderr after the send, for `person` and a `--source` reply), and the Message / Reply dialog's placeholder *First what you want, then why.* The designer's brief (`docs/briefs/`, held for the techlead's read) goes in its own PR.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-134, grinder-ao-1): *A conflict, worked* — the worker ends its turn to be rung by the reply, where it had said *ends its turn in `ao wait`* (TD-153 made waiting on mail the end of the turn); and a worker that asked the person itself tells both controllers the ruling in one `note`, which the text had left unsaid and the test found: a worker holds no copy of its own conflict, so it cannot `reply` on its thread.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-153, grinder-ao-1): *Waiting on mail is ending the turn* written down. The doorbell had been built since TD-052 step 7 and nothing a session read said to rely on it: the presets' *never wait for input* read as *never be idle*, and the designer's run of 2026-09-24 20:28Z to 2026-09-25 13:56Z held 110 foreground `ao wait`/`ao inbox --unread` loops and not one doorbell, because a working session is never rung. The presets' clause became *mail it, then end the turn*, and the empty poll says so in one fixed line.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-168, grinder-ao-1): **When it is read** built — `mail.read_when(s, kind, now, *, person, seat, bound, unreachable)`, `read_when` on `msg`'s reply and the `{ask, note}` pair on every record's view. Three calls the design left open: an ask's bound clause follows every sentence but the seat's note; an addressee on a host whose link is down reads *unreachable* from the home's link state, not only from a record whose state says so; the `scheduled` row waits for TD-152's state.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-180, the designer): **an entry handed to a seat** — the person's `ask` carrying `entry: {repo, type}` and `handed`, sent by `entry_add`, closed by its outcome and not by a reply, with no bound; `--thread` takes its id for the seat's questions; *When it is read* gains the row for a person's answer on a handed entry's thread to a seat on call.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-218 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the handed entry's count and its answer built — `MailEntry.entry` and `handed_entry` (a `handed` `ask` from the person; a handed `note` and an Overrule's `reply` stay out of the count), `asks_waiting` counting it while it owes, less while an open `ask` of the record's own to the person has it as `root`; `read_when(…, refills=True)`, the view's `read_when.refill`, the `msg` reply's sentence and the person inbox's `on_handed`, which the Reply dialog reads.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-218 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): `entry_add {repo, type, text, teams}` built — person-only and a home edit (`modes.HOME_EDITS`); the repo by name or checkout path in the home's registry, the seat from the first of the caller's `teams`, since the host agent reads no `org.yml`; the `ask` lands `handed`, carrying `entry`, with its bound cleared; `read_when(…, lapses=False)` names no bound. Refused in words: a session, an unknown repo, no servicing team, no techlead seat, a type other than `debt` or `feature`, empty words.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-218 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): a handed entry closes by its outcome alone — `--thread <handed id>` puts the seat's question on the entry's thread and settles nothing (it had written `asked_again` on the entry, so it stopped owing and never counted again; the review of #760); `inbox_dismiss` naming a handed entry held in a session's inbox ends the debt and tells the holder by a `system` note; the seat's idle nudge names owed handed entries apart, with the `--outcome` line, rather than *questions waiting — run `ao inbox`*.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-219, built by `grinder-ao-2`): the row an entry handed to a seat waits under is drawn — from the `inbox` read's `handed` list (TD-218 slice 3), under *Waiting on them* whatever its holder's state, since a seat that exited is filled again (not the counted *asker gone quiet* of an answered question), and under *Needs you*, counted, once `blocked`. It names the holder and opens it, has no Reply (the seat's questions are rows of their own) and **Dismiss** names its id; `/inbox/<id>` finds it, so the toast's link no longer reads *gone*.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-259 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): the mail sweep spares an `ask` to a closed record that carries `seat`. Until this date a closed addressee expired its open `ask`s on the next sweep whatever it was, so a question to an empty seat was lost whenever the fill that tick was refused — by `FILL_CEILING`, a paused profile or a down link — since the fill runs before the sweep and nothing else kept it; a seat that had `exited` kept its questions, one the tick had closed did not. What the build settled: the `ask` is marked pending on the closed seat as on an exited record, so the asker's card says the same thing for both; its bound still expires it, and Forget still does.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-274 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): an orphaned `steer` lapses to its default — at its bound `_lapse_or_expire` closes it `lapsed` and writes *steer m-… about TD-n lapsed: the default was "…"* into the closed asker's mailbox, uncharged; until this date the home cleared `bound` and the entry stood as the person's question. An answer to an orphaned question (`_answer_orphan`) sends its `handed` note to the closed asker's record beside the live holders', the toast adding *left in <name>'s mailbox for its next run*. An unread entry that lands on a record after it ended runs its retention from its own arrival: from the record's end, a team wound down more than twelve hours before the bound lost the note at the next sweep. Rule 8's half (`work_waiting.questions`) and `keep_mail` on every superseding create are slice 4.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-292 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): `shots` on the envelope built. Each path is matched whole against `docs/mockups/reviews/<name>.png` (name letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`) and refused otherwise rather than cleaned, because the page serves that one directory; a leading `./` and a repeat are dropped. The Inbox row (slice 3) and Send to reviewer (slice 4) were not built yet.
- 2026-10-03 (TD-292 slice 4a, grinder-ao-1): `inbox_hand` built, the host agent's half of **Send to reviewer**: the `handed` `ask` to the seat the caller names (the page reads `org.yml`, the host agent does not), `look: <id>` on it, `snoozed_for` on the look, and `looked_by` written from the handed entry's outcome. The look is found from the handed entry, so an outcome after an Unsnooze still lands; a `blocked` outcome's line reads *blocked: <line>*. The techlead preset gained *A look handed to you*. The button and the row's two lines were not built yet.
- 2026-10-04 (TD-308, grinder-ao-1; met on a TD-297 press): the trail writes *pushed*, *answered in the terminal* and *the limit reset* from the record still standing (`_ended_by`), as the `how` list says. Until then each fell back to *resolved*, and an `unpushed` row that ended by a push read *resolved: resolved*.

## 5. Configuration

- 2026-10-09 (TD-457, the designer): `teams.<team>.on_work` defaults to `start`, and covers a finished member's lane as well as a wound-down team's.
- 2026-09-10 (TD-004): the shared `sessionorc.hosts` parser landed; in phase 1 the UI and the host agent run on one machine and read the same `local` entry (`name`, `vscode_host`, `local`, `volatile`, `repos_registry`, `runs_keep_days`); the env-var overrides were removed. The ssh entries stayed phase 2 work, becoming the node→home link of §4.4a.
- 2026-09-16 (§4.4a): `home:` was added to `hosts.yml`.
- 2026-09-21 (TD-095 second pass): `ui.yml` designed as the person's own scope, beside `hosts.yml` and `org.yml`. Built the same day (PR #390): `ui.yml` and `open_in:` for the card's button and Focus's; *edit yml* left waiting for the Commands page.
- 2026-09-21: the `cursor` preset was designed as "the same two forms under `cursor://`, confirmed against the editor's own documentation before it ships"; the check was made that day and not met (Cursor documents only its `anysphere.cursor-deeplink` links), so the preset was dropped and, decided with the techlead (TD-095), a Cursor user writes the form as a template of their own.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-100): `settings.yml` designed — the host agent's settings a person moves, first key the usage gate's reserves. The reserves moved out of `.agentorc.yml`'s `unattended:` block ("no usage gate here") because they are the person's, per profile, not the repo's. The settings page was deferred (TD-100 (4): not yet).
- 2026-09-24 (TD-120 step 2, the designer; Paul 2026-09-23: *these design items have to be generalized for any repo; the promote process needs configurable parts*): `promote: {run, check, auto}` designed as the repo's own block — this repo's `run` the pip pair of CLAUDE.md, its `check` the installed build's commit; samscrape's, contractmatch's and dev-cadence's their own. Decided here: it is the one key of `.agentorc.yml` the host agent reads, from the home's registry, because a policy that acts needs its declaration where it runs; until then the agent read none of the file (`ledger:` travels on the record). The build is TD-132.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-212, the designer): `run`'s contract narrowed from *makes the checkout's main live* to *makes live the tree it is started in*, so one script serves the promote and the rollback; `AGENTORC_PROMOTE_SHA`, `_FROM` and `_ROOT` are handed to it.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-026, the designer): `settings.yml`'s second key, `schedules:` — a start rule per team, kept with the person's settings because a schedule is a person's standing press (Paul 2026-09-22: *configurable, off by default*, from the settings page).
- 2026-09-25 (TD-100 (4), Paul: *agreed on the first 3*): §5 became one file for the settings a person moves — `settings.yml`, home-owned, four keys `usage_gate`, `teams`, `repos`, `person` — and `ui.yml` was retired into `person:`; the person's own now follows the home rather than the machine the UI runs on, a scope change Paul confirmed. YAML stays until the TD-060 rename (Python has a TOML reader in the standard library and no writer). The promote's `auto` left `.agentorc.yml` for `repos.<repo>.promote.auto`: the one part of the block a person flips, by the same reasoning TD-100 used for the wind-down figure.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-128, the designer): `usage_gate:` takes an amount per window for a metered profile, the unit saying which kind; a value that does not fit the profile's billing is refused.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-128 reconciled, with Paul): the amount is read against the account's spend, not the profile's; `profiles.yml` carries `prices:` by token kind.
- 2026-09-25 (TD-149 (2), (4), (6), grinder-ao-1): `promote:` accepted in `.agentorc.yml` ahead of TD-132 (it was refused as an unknown key, so writing the designed block broke `ao new` in that repo); the org `roles:` overlay checked key by key as a repo's is (it was taken raw); `AGENTORC_TICK` named as the one test knob.

Reading the agent's snapshot everywhere instead was the other shape the audit offered; the line was chosen because the page's per-request reads stay correct for everything else in the file.

No registered repo carried the file, so nothing live broke.

Nothing on the page reads it yet (slices 2–4).

A node's `volatile` flag under `nodes:` now places its *unreachable* card with *idle*, as §4.5's order said; until then the order had one slot for every unreachable card.

## 6. Policies (the tdgrind supervisor, generalized)

- 2026-10-09 (TD-110, the designer): rule 9's note carries §4.9a's added lines.
- 2026-10-09 (TD-459, the designer): rule 7's tick restart also serves §4.10's lapsed-cache restart, `why: cache`.
- 2026-10-09 (TD-457, the designer): rule 8 reads member by member — a crew member closed after declaring, its team not wound down, whose lane gains an id is the same `work_waiting` mark, and a start on a running team replays the named members alone (`why: work`); `on_work`'s default is `start` (Paul, 2026-10-08); rule 6's *told by neither rule* sentence replaced; a seat still holds a wind-down; rule 9's race needs no hold. Built by TD-466.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-414, grinder-ao-1): rule 6's *`lane_seen` is the lane's memory* sentence said a pruned id's drop was kept under `dropped`; `work.reread` has removed the mark with the id since TD-411, and that is the half that holds (a kept mark would make the returning entry's later drop read as already told), so the sentence now says the mark leaves with the id.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-413, grinder-ao-1): TD-410 built — a Start and Apply write a manager on call `held` (`teamrun.on_call_gate`; its line *on call — comes when a member needs a reading*), the held create moves the kept mail as a fill does, a fill's prompt ends with *[agentorc] you are filled for: <by> — <member or question>* (`agent_tick.filled_for`; `restarts` `for: seat_due`), and `docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`'s first reads moved under the readings that need them.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-411, grinder-ao-1): TD-407 built — `work.reread` prunes `lane_seen` and reads the news in one pass, for rule 6 (`_lane_news`, which writes the pruned memory for a gone member as for a live one), rule 8 (`work.gained`) and the anchor seat's `work` trigger (`_work_due`).
- 2026-10-08 (TD-410, the designer; Paul: *what does the manager still do — do you recommend moving it to Haiku?*): rule 3 gains *A Start writes the seat and never fills it* — a manager on call is created `closed` with no pane, as a held anchor is (TD-386's `create` with `held`), its id the name's so the members' `controllers` stand, and the first due reading fills it, a Start with something already due filling on the first tick by the rule alone; the fill's prompt ends with one line naming its cause; *What is left is judgement* says a fill's first reads are its brief and what the cause names, a repo's supplement naming reads for a reading and never a round. Before: `start` created the manager live first, and fourteen fills since 2026-10-03 closed as *nothing due*. TD-413 builds it.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-407, the designer): rule 6's `lane_seen` is the lane's memory, never the ledger's — an id the reading holds that the lane no longer matches is pruned on every reading, for a live member and a gone one alike, so an entry that leaves the lane and comes back (a build turned live check, then live at the promote) is news to rule 6, rule 8 and the anchor seat's `work` trigger alike; rule 9 holds no wind-down for an untold entry, since rule 6 tells inside its settle and the cases it withholds in leave the id for rule 8. Before: `lane_seen` kept the id for good, and ao-grind wound down three hours after TD-400 became pickable again, told to nobody. The promote itself tells nobody. TD-411 builds it.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-395, grinder-ao-1): rule 3's Start gate and the `none` that consumes no stretch built — `checkout_held` is `_checkout_held`'s reading exposed to `ao team start` (one function, `_checkout_why`, both roads), and a `work` seat's `none` with its tree dirty or off its default branch (`_checkout_tree`) writes `lane_seen` `{at, ids: []}`, so the same ids fill it once the checkout is clean.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-394, the designer): rule 3's checkout gate is the Start's too (one reading), and a `work` seat's `none` declared in a checkout not its own writes `lane_seen` with no ids (never `None`, which the next tick fills from the reading) so the stretch stays due. TD-395 builds it.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-386, grinder-ao-1): the anchor seat's tick built. Rule 6's lane word `anchor` (`ledger.lane_matches`) takes a pickable `Owner: anchor` entry of any kind (a live check once live), an entry blocked by `decision (anchor)`, and every work order. Rule 3's `work` trigger: `seat_due: {at, by: work, ids}` from the seat's `lane_seen`, which is written from the reading at the record's create and by the seat's own `none`, at the declaration, so an entry filed before the next tick still fills it; an idle seat with work due is closed for a fill from cold; the fill carries the stretch's ids into the new record's `lane_seen`. The fill's gate is occupancy, then a tree that is clean and on its default branch; a held fill writes `seat_held: {by, why}`, which the fill clears, as does a due that clears. The fill's gate is what showed that `workorders` imported the repo's board reader and so wrote `scripts/__pycache__` into the checkout, which reads as the person's uncommitted work; the reader is now compiled from its text.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-384 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): rule 6 and rule 8 read the repo's work orders beside its entries; `lane_matches` puts a work order in every `free-pick` lane whatever its `owner:` words; a work order is never in the first `lane_seen`, so one that sat unclaimed at a declaration is told on the next tick; a claim on `board:<key>` is a lease as any reference's.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-381, the designer): rule 3 gains the trigger `work` and `seat_held` (the fill gated by occupancy and a clean checkout on its default branch); rule 6 gains the lane word `anchor`.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-380, the designer): rule 6's `free-pick` takes every open decided line of the repo's board as `board:<key>`, which `owner:<word>` never narrows out.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-368 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): rule 6's `design-first` word takes an entry on `decision (designer)`, pickable or not, its owner read as `designer` (`ledger.lane_matches`); the grinder's lane is told of it once the decision's PR drops the item.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-367, the designer): rule 6's `design-first` word also takes an entry of any kind blocked by `decision (designer)`, pickable or not, the decision's holder standing as its owner for the lane's owner word; a decision named for the person or the anchor matches no lane.
- 2026-10-07 (TD-366, grinder-ao-1): rule 3 no longer closes an idle seat that waits on a PR of its own — open on its checked-out branch, on a claim, or a claim derived from that branch before a reading has its PR — unless it handed the PR to a reader with an `ask`, or it has sat idle `SEAT_PR_WAIT` (two hours). On 2026-10-07 test-audit-ao-1 opened #1197, launched its fact-check as a background agent and ended its turn; the tick closed it at 06:31, killing the fact-check, and the PR sat with nobody for ten hours. Of the Fix's three candidate signals the build took the open PR, since it is read from the records; a background task the tool reports needs a hook no adapter carries, and a renewable grace needs the seat to renew it.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-352, grinder-ao-1): **the tick's own replays keep the mail** — rules 1, 2 and 7 now `create` with `keep_mail`, as rule 8, a seat's fill and a person's Restart already did. Before, a wanted restart started the member with an empty mailbox, so the techlead's reply to an `ask` the run before had sent matched no thread on the new record: #1164, read and merged by the techlead, was a crossing (*merged without the techlead's read*) twenty minutes after its merge, and §4.10 *The name coming back adopts it* already listed the tick's restart among the creates that keep the mail.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-349, grinder-ao-1): rule 11's link-by-link read built — `held.read_by(s, pr, files)`, `addressed` moved into `sessionorc.held` and re-exported by `agentorc.review`, the tick passing the PR's files, and the review brief's line for an `ask` with no `pr`. Decided in the build: the older `{reader, held}` keeps its reading verbatim — any sender an ask named — because its reader is a role word (`techlead`) that `addressed` would not match against a seat's session id; only a record carrying `chain` is read link by link. The *Done when*'s scratch-home check is made by the tick-level test on `_held_pass` with `pr_read` stubbed, since a scratch home's `gh` reads real PRs and cannot be shown a merge.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-345, the designer): rule 11 rewritten for a chain of readers (§4.9c, TD-315): a held PR is read when every link whose paths it touches has its reader's reply, or a thread holds a `merged` verdict or the person's reply; before, it read *for `reader: techlead`, an `ask` … with a reply from that seat*, while §4.9c said the rule already held a chain's PR *read by a reply from any reader its asks named* — which made a chain PR merged after the first reader's `pass` no crossing, against *only the last reader holding the PR merges it*. The code (`held.read_by`) does the latter; TD-349 builds the rule.
- Until TD-095 is built, the mode toggle sits on the card's badge rather than in the card's *more*.
- `workers: [{role, lane}]` was specified to replace the bare `workers: 3` "once §4.8 lands".
- 2026-09-13 (TD-026): stop time (`run_until`) landed, with the New session form taking one (§4.5a) the same day. The gap it closed first: a session started by hand with `--unattended` had no stopper at all — nothing wrapped it up at 06:00, at a cap, or when its token lapsed — so "start it now so I can watch it" meant "remember to close it yourself". Left open on TD-026: `start_at` and the `scheduled` state, window overrides with an expiry, calendar-shaped schedules, editing a stop time from the page.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-100): the usage gate was redesigned, the shape decided by Paul the same day. Paul: *about 30% of each session and 10% of the weekly* — the reason the setting became a reserve per window label (flat, or per day) from which the line is computed, rather than a percentage typed as the line. The tool's own 100% showing `limited` was kept "as before"/"as today" for interactive sessions.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-101): a one-day override of a reserve that would expire on its own was split from TD-100 and not wanted yet; a one-day change stays by hand.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103; Paul chose option 1 of the design review): the four mechanical rules of the manager's brief — crash restart under the 3-in-2-hours ceiling, the wanted restart, seat fills, the idle nudge — become host-agent policies keyed on `unattended` and a new `supervised` field, under *a restart is not a start*; the restart ceiling and the fill ceiling become constants; `supervised`, `seat`, `seat_due`, `restarts`, `restart_ceiling`, `restart_blocked` and `nudged_at` join the home-owned fields; a launch record (`launch/<id>.json`) is written at every supervised create. Sonnet round 1 (READY WITH CHANGES, six findings, all adopted): rules 2 and 3 gained *not gated*; the manager is supervised too, and its `ao close` at wind-down is not a crash; an idle seat with `seat_due` is nudged; `restart_blocked` clears itself when the work is pushed while `restart_ceiling` waits for a person; a failed replay counts toward the ceiling with `why: failed`; `supervised` survives Resume and is cleared only by Forget. Round 2 (READY WITH CHANGES, five findings, all adopted): a failed replay keeps its `why` and adds `error`; `restart_blocked_sent_at` is home-owned; the fill ceiling's mark lands on the seat that tripped it; the mode toggle's row names the supervision rules; the stop-time policy's per-record isolation is a target, not a fact. Round 3 (READY WITH CHANGES, two findings, adopted): rule 1 excludes seats (a seat's natural exit is not a crash; rule 3 alone fills one); invariant 14 carries the caveat §4.9a had. Round 4 (READY WITH CHANGES, three findings, adopted): invariant 9 names `supervised`; rule 3 carries *not suspended*; a person's Resume clears `restarts` with the mark. Round 5 (READY WITH CHANGES, one finding, adopted): §4.8 says a director's managers are supervised and the tick, not the director, restarts one. Round 6 (READY WITH CHANGES, one finding, adopted): the launch record is written at every create whose record carries `supervised`, attended or not, so Hand back after a Resume with changes replays the latest choices. Round 7 (READY WITH CHANGES, one finding, adopted): rule 2 restarts only an idle member or a natural exit, never a kill, a Close or a session past its stop time. Round 8 (READY WITH CHANGES, one finding, adopted): §4.9a's controller rule drops `closed` to match. **Closed by Fable after round 8**: eight rounds, and from round 3 on each round's one finding was a consistency follow-on of the previous adoption rather than a new hole; the anchor judged the text implementable and recorded the override here rather than run a ninth round. Until then: §4.9a said *the host agent starts nothing by itself* and the manager's brief carried the rules.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): `supervised` and the launch record built — the field on the record (home-owned), `ao team start` setting it on every session it creates, `ao new --supervised`, `launch/<id>.json` (owner-only) written at every supervised create — at the home for a node's session, under its address — and removed on Forget. A resume keeps `supervised` from the record of its name or of its conversation, in the host agent, so no client has to carry it; a fresh, unsupervised create that takes a supervised record's id removes the stale launch record.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103 slice 2, PR #453, grinder-ao-1): rule 1, the crash restart, built on the home's tick (`_keep_running`) with `RESTART_CEILING` (3 in 2 h), `restarts` carried across the tick's own supersede and `restart_ceiling`, and the card's *restarts exhausted* ending; `seat: {trigger}` written by `ao team start` in this slice rather than slice 3, because rule 1 must not act on a seat and nothing else on the record says which sessions are seats.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103 slice 3, PR #459, grinder-ao-1): rule 3, the seats, built on the home's tick — `seat_due` from `seat`, the fill with `keep_mail` replaying the launch record (`why: fill`), `FILL_CEILING` (6 an hour over seats sharing a controller) and the idle seat's close; TD-104 folded in. Three points the design had left open were settled in the build and written into rule 3 (a steer to the techlead, default taken): `by` names what met the trigger; the count before it is met is a new home-owned `seat_count`, since `seat_due` exists only once met; the home reads `gh` in the seat's checkout at the same absolute path, and a path it cannot see gives no reading. The two-minute `SEAT_IDLE_GRACE` was added so a fill is not closed before its prompt lands. The same PR made rule 1's *not gated* read the profile's usage, since the gate clears `gated` on an exited record and the check had been true for every crash. The parked `td104-seat-due` build (PR #444) gave the outage-is-not-zero rule and the card's count.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103 slice 4, PR #461, grinder-ao-1): rules 2 and 4 built on the home's tick. Rule 2 closes and restarts a member whose `restart_wanted` has its work pushed (`why: wanted`, one count with the crash restart, fills excluded); with work left it types one line naming the counts, and `IDLE_NUDGE` later marks `restart_blocked`. Rule 4 types the fixed nudge once per idle stretch, recorded on `sends` as `system`, and the card reads *idle · open work* twenty minutes after that. Two limits the design did not state were written in: a node's member gets no line from either rule, because the composer is read on the member's host and no node act does that; and an exited member's hold clock runs from its declaration, since nothing can be typed to it. A retry was added for a wanted restart whose replay failed after the tick's own close: such a record is `closed`, which rule 2 would otherwise never look at again.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103 slice 5, the Inbox row, PR #462, grinder-ao-1): **Inbox row: restart** built, with Open, Resume, Snooze and Dismiss. Dismiss was designed as *removes the row and the mark*, which the tick defeats: a cleared `restart_ceiling` is rewritten on the next pass from the same three restarts, a cleared `restart_blocked` from the same send time, and an `early` mark is the session's own field. So it became a dismissal of that one mark's row, kept in the attention store as `dismissed:<at>`. This was sent to the techlead as a steer with that default; its reply never arrived and the steer lapsed to the default. The briefs' cut, the rest of slice 5, waits on TD-114, which reshapes the same files.
- 2026-09-22 (TD-103 slice 5, the briefs' cut, grinder-ao-1): the built-in manager preset stopped performing the four rules once the host agent built from #459–#462 was live (30ab5ad, promoted 05:34Z on 2026-09-23). Until this date `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md` filled the techlead seat by hand (`ao new --keep-mail`) and closed it, restarted a member that wanted a restart (`ao new --supervised`), and sent the twenty-minute idle nudge. Its round ended in `ao wait --timeout 540` under a ten-minute tool call. It now reads `restart_blocked`, `restart_ceiling` and *idle · open work* and boards them, keeps the nudge only for a member on a node (rule 4 types into no node's pane yet; the fact-check of PR #471), and its round ends in `ao wait --timeout 3540` run in the background, because Claude Code caps a foreground Bash call at ten minutes.
- 2026-09-23 (TD-122): the usage gate's windows named as the account's reading, read against the profile's own lines; the reserves unchanged.
- 2026-09-24 (TD-120 step 2, the designer): the **Promote** policy designed — at the home, per registered repo with a block; three readings (live from `check`, main after the home's own fetch, checks from `gh`) and three preconditions (the checkout on main's head with a clean tree, checks green, nothing in flight and no failure standing); `auto: true` promotes once main has stood still for `PROMOTE_SETTLE` (10 min); the run detached with an intent file written first, the outcome read from `check` on later ticks and never from the exit code, because this repo's promote restarts the agent that started it; `PROMOTE_BOUND` (20 min) then a failure that blocks further promotes until the person clears it. The three preconditions were named by the anchor on 2026-09-23 as *`main` clean and current, CI green on that sha, and nothing a person is mid-way through*; the third became the tree's state, since the tree is what `run` installs from. Until built, the anchor promotes by hand.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-132 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): the readings and the policy built — `sessionorc.promote` and the home's `PromoteMixin`, `promotes` on `host`. Three choices the design had left open, now written into §6: *when main moved* is its head's committer time rather than the moment the home first saw it, so a restart does not reset the settle; a head with no check runs reads `unknown`, not a vacuous green, so `auto` never promotes a commit CI has not looked at (a repo with no CI is promoted by press); and `check` is read every fifteen seconds while a run is in flight, not only on the five-minute cadence.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-132 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the press built — `promote` and `clear_promote` (person-only home edits, in `modes.HOME_EDITS`) and `ao promote [<repo>]` / `ao promote status`. From the techlead's read of slice 1: the intent keeps the run's process start time beside its pid and the pass tests *alive* before the bound, so a pid reused after a restart is neither waited on nor killed. Two things decided in the build: a press whose live is main's head already is refused as having nothing to promote, and `--sha` is refused until the design says how a rollback's commit reaches `run`, which installs a tree precondition (1) holds at main's head.
- 2026-09-27 (TD-132 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): the Inbox row built — `promote_rows` from `promotes` on `host`, joined to the state rows (the in-flight row to FYI), keyed `promote:<repo>` in the attention store (`attention_snooze` takes the kind `promote`), **Promote** and **Dismiss** through `/api/person/promote` and `/clear_promote`. A refusal is the toast every Inbox row's refusal is, the row left standing. From the techlead's read of #666: a press whose own reading concludes a run in flight files that run's *promoted …* note rather than dropping it, and the fresh reading replaces the held one whatever the press does next.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-132 slice 4, grinder-ao-1): this repo's `promote:` block — CLAUDE.md's pair as `run`, the live install's build commit as `check` — and Paul's answer to the question left open by slice 1 (the board, 2026-09-28): `auto` acts only on a live it has read; an unknown live waits for a reading, the person's press still goes through.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-212, the designer; Paul the same day: *add a design TD for ao promote --sha*): the **rollback** designed — a person's press with a commit of main, run in a detached worktree of the checkout under `~/.agentorc/promotes/<repo>/tree`, so precondition (1) is not asked and a failure standing does not refuse it; `last.json` for `--back`; three variables handed to `run`, which refuses what its repo cannot go back to; a **hold** (`held.json`) under which the policy starts nothing until a plain press concludes or the person dismisses it. Turned down: installing the wheel a promote kept (it would name pip in the core, and only this repo has a wheel), and keeping the refusal with a quicker revert (a revert waits on CI and a merge while live is broken). From the fact-check: the tree is kept until the next run, since this repo's build record names its source; `--sha` takes hex only; `--back` is refused under a hold. Left as it was: a promote of main's head still runs in the checkout, since a repo's `run` may lean on what the checkout holds. Build: TD-226.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-226 slices 1–2, grinder-ao-1): the rollback built but for its row — `promote.resolve` (hex only; an ambiguous prefix counted with `rev-parse --disambiguate`), the worktree at `promotes/<repo>/tree`, the three `AGENTORC_PROMOTE_*` variables on every run, `last.json`, `held.json` and `held` on the reading, `--back`, `ao promote clear` (`clear_promote` now also says `which` it cleared). The two slices went as one PR because a rollback without its hold would be put back by `auto` on the next pass. Two readings the design left open: `from` is empty when `check`'s last reading carries a why (a kept last-good reading is not *what is live*), and the note's *main is n commits ahead* is counted when the run concludes.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-214, the designer; Paul the same day: *it looks like the dc-grind and ao-grind teams both have work to do but are both idle — should they restart automatically?*): **rule 8, work for a team that wound down**, designed. Until now rule 6 ended *an exited or closed member is not written to and not restarted … new work waits for a person's Start*, and §4.9a said *nothing here restarts a team when work reappears*; ao-grind sat a day with ten entries in its designer's lane. The home now keeps `lane_seen` for a member that is gone and writes `work_waiting`; by default it **asks** (an Inbox row whose Start is the card's), and starts the team only where `teams.<team>.on_work: start` says so, by the schedule's replay and inside four bounds. Asking is the default because Paul decided on 2026-09-22 that a start nobody pressed is *configurable, off by default*. Two repairs to rule 6 with it: `lane_seen`'s first write is the ledger as `origin/<default>` held it at the declaration (the first tick of TD-195's promote had taken the day's backlog as seen), and a lane's `owner:<word>` narrows the match (grinder-ao-1's `lane_seen` held TD-159, the anchor's). From the fact-check: the wound-down reading is over unattended sessions and knows a seat by the record, a start is counted once however many records it replays, the settle runs from the home's first reading, and a finished member closed inside a live team is named as covered by neither rule. Turned down: matching `Owner:` against the session's role, which would key on a role (§9 invariant 9). Build: TD-227.
- 2026-09-30 (TD-227 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): rule 6's two repairs built. `lane_matches` takes the whole lane, so an `owner:<word>` narrows the other words; `_lane` sets owner words aside before its checks, keeps them last as `owner:<word>` lower-cased, and refuses a lane of owner words alone or an `owner:` naming no one. Every reader of a lane as references leaves them out through one helper, `lane_refs` — the nudge, the report line's count, the brief's `{lane}` slot — and it leaves `design-first` out too, which until then the nudge could name as a reference open. `lane_seen`'s first write reads the ledger at the last commit of `origin/<default>` before `out_of_work.at` (`ledger.entries_before`, `rev-list --before`), off the tick's loop, and an entry merged since the declaration is told on that same first tick; with no history it is the reading at that tick, and the log says which. `agentorc/org.py`'s `_lane` has no checks to set owner words aside from, so it is unchanged.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-227 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): rule 8's home half built. `sessionorc.work` holds the one wound-down reading, which `teamrun` imports for the card; at the home a seat is the record's `seat` field and a person's session is its `unattended: false`, and a test holds the two readings equal. `_work_marks` writes `work_waiting` on the `host` record after `WORK_SETTLE`, `teams.<team>.on_work` takes `ask`, `start` or `off`, and `clear_work` is Dismiss's half. Three choices the design left open, now written into rule 8: a team whose members' news is in two repos is written for the first repo by name, since the mark carries one `repo` for its links; while more ids settle, the standing mark stays with its `at`, so the row's age does not reset on each new entry; and the mark goes when the team is no longer wound down, when no id is new, or under `off`. Until slice 4, `on_work: start` writes the mark as `ask` does. Not built: the row, the card's note and the words (slice 3), and the start (slice 4).
- 2026-09-30 (TD-233, grinder-ao-1; the anchor, 2026-09-30: *the gate paused all nine grind members twice on last week's 99%*): **a window past its reset pauses nothing** built ahead of slice 4. The endpoint had refused since 23:07Z, so no reading followed the weekly reset, and the gate read the held 99% against the new week's line. `settings.lines` marks such a row `unknown: "reset"` and `crossed` passes it by. The gate and `_profile_gated` read the same rows, so neither pauses on the window nor refuses a restart on it. The row keeps the number, and `ao gate` and the Settings card print it as *unknown since its reset (was 99%)*: the review of #776 found `ao gate` still printing *now 99%* against the line, which read as a crossing the gate no longer acts on.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-233 slice 4, the host agent's half, grinder-ao-1): **the gate projects a reading older than `max_age`**. `usage.project` is the one reader, and the gate's pass, `_profile_gated` and the `gate` RPC all ask it through `_gate_windows`. `usage: {max_age}` is the fifth key of `settings.yml`, set through `set_settings`. Three choices the design left open, now written into §6. First, a pause never ends because its window went unknown for want of a rate: paused sessions report nothing, so a pause on a reading would otherwise lift itself an hour later on no news. Second, the *usage unknown* note fires for a window past its reset only once it has been so for `max_age`, since a working session reports the new window within a minute and a note at every weekly reset would be noise. Third, a metered profile's spend is never projected, since it does not age. Not built: `ao gate --max-age` and its *projected* print, and the Settings field, which are the client's.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-177, the designer; Paul, 2026-09-25: *this may lead to automatic checks by the manager for us, like the PR count*): **Balance** designed — `teams.<team>.balance`, three lines (open PRs above `n`, the oldest past `d`, the reader's queue past its bound), off until set, read by the home's tick, and acting at the claim: a new claim by an unattended member of a team over its line is refused, `--force` included, and `ao progress none` with it, so the team idles and never winds down on it. The entry had the manager *hand out no new claim*; a manager hands out nothing (its brief: *managers only manage*) and a session's `ao repo` cannot see the reader's queue, so the rule sits where work is taken and the manager is told. Considered and turned down: the manager reading `ao repo` each round (blind to the queue, and an hour late), a pause as the usage gate's (work in hand is what clears the line), and counting only the grinders' PRs (the number would no longer be the card's). Build: TD-239.

- 2026-09-26 (TD-186, grinder-ao-1): the restart ceiling was tripped by a restart's own race — the closed run's `SessionEnd` reached the successor record (same id), set the old tool id and `exited`, and the next two ticks' crash replays were refused by the anchor rule against the successor's own session; the ceiling written on the healthy member then blocked its `restart_wanted` for a day, and the team read *running* with every member idle. Fixed three ways: an exit event with another run's tool id is ignored, rule 1 waits `RESTART_SETTLE` after a successful restart, and a clean `restart_wanted` is acted on once the window has emptied below the ceiling (the §4.5a row's *never lifted by the tick* narrowed to that).
- 2026-09-26 (TD-146 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the team stop time built as a tick pass at the home. Two readings the design left to the build: a session created after the team's instant has passed is not given it (a team started again after its stop time would otherwise be wrapped up on its first tick), and **Clear** takes the instant back from the members that carry it — identified by carrying exactly the old instant — so a stop time a person withdraws stops nobody, while a member's own `ao until` stays.

- 2026-09-27 (TD-152 slices 1–2, grinder-ao-1): *Start time* in the agent — the `scheduled` state and `start_at`, `create` with `start_at` making the record now (name, worktree, launch record, the slot) with no pane, `set_start` (an acting call, gated and forwarded as `set_stop` is; `now` hands it to the next tick), `close` and `remove` as Cancel, and the tick's start by replay with `why: start`, a failed start counted to the ceiling. The replay names what it starts (`start_of`) so its own record is superseded in place with its mail. Decided in the build: a scheduled start of a resume is refused (a resume runs now), the usage gate and the stop time leave a scheduled record alone, and a person's `set_start` spends a ceiling its failed starts reached, with their count, as a person's own Resume starts rule 1's count again — a controller's is refused at the ceiling and leaves the count below it (the techlead's read of #669, which also made `start_of` a person's or a controller's act, as `keep_mail` is).

- 2026-09-27 (TD-152 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): *Start time* on the surfaces — `ao new --at` (parsed as `--until`, refused without `--unattended`, printing *starts 20:00* instead of the attach line), `ao at <session> <when> | now`, `ao status -v`'s *starts* line (`models.start_note`, the *stops* formatter's clock), the ◷ pill, the card's note and `more ⋯`'s **Start now** / **Cancel**, the New session **At** field (a scheduled Start lands on the Org, there being no terminal yet), and Focus on a scheduled record: no terminal, a banner with the note (pressed, it asks for a time or *now*), Start now and Cancel, and the page looking again every twenty seconds so the terminal appears once the agent has started it. `read_when` gained its `scheduled` row, *read when it starts, at 20:00*.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-199, the designer): **rule 7, brief changed.** Until this date a restart replayed the prompt the first start was handed, and nothing marked a running member whose brief had changed: manager-ao-1, started 2026-09-25 18:27Z, never read #600's template (2026-09-26) and its card read *round 1: … all working* throughout: a day and a half when the entry was filed, three days by this date. Now the launch record keeps what the prompt was made from and a replay reads those files again, as merged; the record carries `brief` and `brief_changed`; a working member is told on its `ao` replies and an idle one with nothing held is restarted by the tick. Of the entry's options: (a) the mark and (c) the restart are taken; (b), a `system` note naming what changed, is not — a note tells a member its brief is stale and cannot give it the new one. The manager stays outside the context bound.
- 2026-09-28 (TD-217 slice 1, grinder-ao-2): `prompt_from` built as the design had it, with two readings the design did not yet say: the seat's primer fills `{context}` with its path as text, not the file's content, so it is a `{text}` slot; and the Project block a team start or `ao new --project` puts in front of the brief rides as `prefix`, since a replay that dropped it would start a member without its reach. Three readings the design left to the build: a source's `sha` is git's blob id, the same for a file read from disk and one read from a ref, so the tick can compare either; *origin's default* is `origin/HEAD`, then `origin/main`, then `origin/master`, and a checkout with no origin is read from disk; a member on a node replays its stored prompt (`prompt: stored`), since its files are that host's and the home reads its own disk.
- 2026-09-29 (TD-217 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): rule 7's mark and the **brief changed** chip built (`brief.changed`, `_brief_pass`, detached at the reports' cadence). The mark also goes when the files read as recorded again, since nothing is then left to restart for. A replay of the stored prompt writes no `brief`, because the create would record the working tree's files, and the mark would then compare what the prompt was not made from (the techlead's note on #745).
- 2026-09-29 (TD-217 slice 4, grinder-ao-1): rule 7's two tellings built. The clause rides every `ao` reply as `mail.brief` beside rule 5's `mail.context` and is printed the same way. Two readings the design left to the build: a member at its restart ceiling is marked `restart_ceiling` and left to the person, as rule 2 does; a failed close or replay leaves it `closed`, and the tick retries it.

Under `on_work: start` the tick replays the team's ended crew records (seats included, a seat with its mail kept as a fill keeps it; none superseded, suspended, at its ceiling or without a launch record), the records named as a controller first, each `restarts` entry `why: work` with the `ids` it was started for — what the card's *started … for* line reads — and `work_started` on the home's record counts the start once. Three choices the design left open, now written into rule 8: a bound that holds writes `held: {why}` on `work_waiting`, which is what draws the row under `start`, re-read each tick; a team with nothing left to replay is held as `nothing`, since the row of `ask` is how a person starts what the rule cannot; and a member on a node whose link is down holds the whole start, unmarked, rather than starting half a team. - 2026-09-30 (TD-249 slice 1, grinder-ao-1, PR #846): each `restarts` entry carries `done: [{ref, pr}]` and `left: [ref]` of the run it replaced, written by `_replay` on every replay, a failed one's included. Settled in the build: a derived claim is not in `left` (it is the tick's reading, not the run's word) while a derived `done` is in `done` (a merged pull request is work done), a run that reported nothing carries the two lists empty, and the entry written when a wanted or brief restart's close fails carries neither, since no run was replaced and the record keeps its `progress`.
- 2026-09-30 (TD-249 slice 2, grinder-ao-1, PR #848): the `restart` declaration writes `early` and `repeat: {ref}` from the record (`agent_common._restart_reading`) and its reply names what decided it. Settled in the build: with several repeated `done` the first names the repeat; a claim left a third time is a repeat even beside new work, and one closed this run is not left; a `restarts` entry with no `done` or `left` (written before the fields, or by a failed close) says nothing either way; past `RESTART_EARLY` with no repeat the reply says nothing.
- 2026-09-30 (TD-249 slice 3, grinder-ao-1, PR #851): the ceiling's count is one function, `agent_common._counted`, and leaves out a `wanted` restart that carried new work. Settled in the build: an entry's work is new against the entries in the window before its own time, as its declaration read it; a `wanted` replay that failed counts whatever its run had done, as a crash does; an entry written before the fields has no `done` and counts; a scheduled start's own count of its `start` entries is untouched.
- 2026-09-30 (TD-249 slice 6, grinder-ao-1): the words. An early mark carries `decided`, the reading's words, and the Inbox row, the card's hover, the Focus chip's and `ao status -v` print them; the card and the chip read *repeats TD-x* for a repeat. Settled in the build, from the techlead's read of #848: the repeat test reads the run's **declared** `done` only — after a replay the tick derives the earlier run's merge onto the new record, and a run that then did nothing read *repeats TD-x: reported done by an earlier run too*; it now reads *early: nothing reported done this run* inside `RESTART_EARLY`, and past it is an ordinary restart the ceiling counts. A derived `done` with a new pair is still new work. The row's two repeat sentences are §4.9a's (*an earlier run*, *three runs running*); §4.5a's said *its last run* and *twice running*. The grinder and hunter templates say early is nothing new done, not the first thirty minutes.
