Settled in the build: a seat's record takes no default but keeps a bound its role's definition wrote (the `auditor` preset's); `ao new --role` writes a default only with `--unattended`, so a person's own session is never told it is over a bound nobody set, and `ao new` with no `--role` resolves no role and takes none; this repo's measured `roles.grinder.context` is not set yet.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-250 slice 1, grinder-ao-1, PR #862): the `restart` RPC is built (`agent_wake.rpc_restart`, `_restart_check`; `modes.HOME_EDITS`). Settled in the build: a replay at the same id does **not** keep the record's mail, as the design had said — the new record is a new record — so the restart's create asks for it (`keep_mail`), as a seat's fill does; `agent_common._counted` leaves a `person` entry out, or the press would be one of the three it lifts; the clean-and-pushed test is one function, `gitinfo.work_left`, shared by the tick's wanted restart, the RPC and `teamrun._unsafe_to_close`, and it reads a `git` with no `unpushed` count as unknown where the team's stop had read it as pushed; a record that is not supervised is refused in the words of one with no launch record; a create that fails after the close leaves the record closed with its marks and says so.

- 2026-09-30 (TD-247, the designer): *What stays the manager's* became *What is left is judgement, and a seat holds it*. Rules 10 (**the cadence check**: `check_cadence.py --json` run at the home per `done` with a `pr`, `checks` on the record, one fixed line on the first fail, the Inbox row on the second or on a merged PR, *recorded* / *read by <seat>* beside the self-attested `review` row), 11 (**merged without its read**: a merged PR's files against the record's `held:` and the reader's reply in the home's mail, `held_missed`, a line and an FYI on the first, the row on the second; the glob match moves into `sessionorc`) and 12 (**conventions relayed**: `cadence_changes.py --json` as merged, `conventions_seen` written at the create, one `system` note per new heading; the manager's `relayed.json` goes) designed; rule 4 gains the owed outcome as open work; rule 6 tells a lease a sibling dropped after the declaration; rule 3 takes the manager on call as a seat with the `team` trigger (`by`: `asks`, `pending`, `stalled`, `open`; `idle_open` on the member; `seat_filled` so a cause fills once per stretch). No permission allow-list: an unattended launch skips permissions already, and what prompts is the judgement. The designer steered *the default moves on proof: dc-grind first*; Paul picked the alternative on 2026-10-01: on call is the default from the build, and dc-grind's first run is the first look, not a gate. Not built — TD-258 (the policies), TD-259 (the seat). The round's research also found rule 1, 2 and 7 replays carry no mail to the new record, against TD-246's *the mail kept, as a replay at the same id keeps it*; left for TD-250's builder as a note on that entry.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-258 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): rule 10 built — the tick runs `scripts/check_cadence.py --pr <n> --json` on a supervised member's `done` with a PR and keeps the reading on `checks`. Until this date the manager ran the script on its round and read the rows. What the build added to the design's entry: `merged` (a merged PR's settled read is not asked for again — the script's JSON carries no head, so the head is a `gh pr view` of its own, and a record's many merged PRs would each have cost one every five minutes) and `read_by` (the reader's reply is mail, which is pruned). The line is typed only into an unattended member's composer; an attended member of a team is told by the clause.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-258 slice 3, grinder-ao-1): rule 12 built — the tick runs `scripts/cadence_changes.py --json` in each registry root that holds a member to tell, keeps `conventions_seen` on the record and sends the `system` note. Until this date the manager relayed each entry with `ao send --wait` on its round. What the build settled: the field is written at the first **reading** after the create, not the first tick, since the read is detached on the reports' cadence — an entry that lands between the create and that reading is still told, because the sort is by `landed` against `created`; the note's ref is the script's own `ref`; a run that exits non-zero for any reason is no reading.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-258 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): rule 11 built — the glob match and the files read moved from `agentorc.review` into `sessionorc.held` (`ao pr held` imports them), and the tick reads each merged PR of a member carrying `review` against its `held:` globs. Until this date whether a held PR merged without its read was a sentence in this repo's manager supplement. What the build settled, against the design as written on 2026-09-30: the PRs read are every `done` with a PR, declared or derived, because a member's declared `done` stands in the derived one's place and a rule reading derived entries alone would never see a member that reports; the read carries `mergedAt`, so a declared `done` on an open PR waits; a crossing is written only fifteen minutes after the merge, since the seat merges and then replies (#868: 33 seconds apart) and the home may read in between; the person's reply on the ask's thread is a read for `reader: techlead`, the escalation §4.9b gives; the mail is the member's own outbox and inbox plus rule 10's `read_by`; the clause is said once on the member's next `ao` reply and rides the reply's `cadence` field, so `cli.py` is untouched; what is settled is kept in memory, five reads a pass.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-258 slice 4, grinder-ao-1): rule 4's owed clause and rule 6's dropped lease built. Until this date an idle member whose only open work was an outcome owed to the person was never nudged — `ao progress none` refused it and nothing typed a line — and the manager chased the debt on its round; and a lease a sibling dropped after a member's declaration was told by nobody but the manager's second reading of the ledger. What the build settled: the owed clause follows the open reference and stands alone where there is none, three ids named at most; a seat's clause counts its outbox alone, since a handed entry had its own part already (TD-218); a drop is told in the lane note and counted with the new ids, as *TD-108 (dropped by grinder-ao-2)*; the instants are `lane_seen.dropped`, which Dismiss on a team's work row now keeps; and a dropped id that a live record has claimed again is not told while that lease holds.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-258 slice 5, grinder-ao-1): Dismiss on rule 11's row marks the `held_missed` entries `dismissed` and keeps them; the design of 2026-09-30 said it cleared them. What the home has settled is kept in memory, so an entry removed by Dismiss was written again, with its note and its line, by a home restarted inside half the mail's retention of the merge (the review of #874). The row is two entries not dismissed, and the first crossing after a Dismiss is a note and a line again.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-258 slice 6, grinder-ao-1): the manager preset's round lost its cadence-check and relay steps and the chase of an owed outcome — step 3 now reads the marks rules 10–12 leave (`checks`, `held_missed`, the `system` note) and step 2 says the reminder is the host agent's, a node's member excepted, since rule 4 types into no node's pane — and this repo's supplement lost the cadence-check, merged-held-PR and relay paragraphs with `relayed.json`. Until this date the preset still told a standing manager to run `check_cadence.py`, send the rows and relay each `docs/cadence-changes.md` entry beside the tick doing the same, so a member could be told twice. §4.8's role table and the two *not built* marks follow.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-259 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the `team` trigger built — the tick writes `idle_open` on a member still idle twenty minutes after rule 4's nudge, sets a manager on call's `seat_due: {at, by, member}` from the four readings and keeps `seat_filled` across the fill. Until this date nothing wrote `idle_open` (the page derived the slot from `nudged_at`) and a `seat: {trigger: team}` record was closed at its first idle and never filled. What the build settled: a question is a cause by its id (`ask` in `member`'s place), so one left standing fills once and a second fills again; `idle_open` is a member's alone, names no reference where the open work is an owed outcome, and stands until the stretch ends, the work closes or the member declares; and a member's reading that comes due while the seat is filled closes the idle seat after `SEAT_IDLE_GRACE` and is the next fill's, since a fill starts cold on the one reading named. The default is not flipped here: `org.ON_CALL_DEFAULT` stays `false` until the sweep's exception (slice 3) is in.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-259, the flip, grinder-ao-1): the card's *idle · open work* is read from `idle_open` alone. Slice 4 (PR #875) had kept the page's own derivation from `nudged_at` for a home whose tick did not write the field; the tick writes it since slice 2, and the page and the host agent are promoted together, so the fallback is removed.
- 2026-10-01 (TD-222, the designer; decided by Paul the same day — *lets have it do when git allows and the anchor session is idle*): **Pull** added — the home's tick fast-forwards every registered main checkout to `origin/<default>` in the promote's pass when git allows (default branch, no git operation or promote run under way, the fast-forward itself accepted: a dirty file elsewhere stops nothing and a checkout with a commit of its own is left alone) and the anchor session is idle (every occupant of the root at rest, shells included, an external session by its registry's `status`, one it cannot read taken as mid-turn, no session at all idle). Built by TD-263.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-271, the designer): rule 8 gains **A question's end is work** — the lapse of an orphaned `steer`, or the person's answer to an orphaned question, on a wound-down team writes `work_waiting` with `questions: [{id, ref, name, how}]` and no settle, then `on_work` as for ids; a start keeps every record's mail, not a seat's alone; a stopped team is left alone. Rule 9: a live member waiting on the person blocks the reading, so `finished` reads the person inbox beside the records. The build is TD-274.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-263 slice 1, grinder-ao-1): **Pull** built at the home — `promote.pulls` runs in the promote's pass at its full cadence, before the promote's readings, whose fetch of `main` it makes (`read_main` reuses it: one fetch per repo per pass); `HostAgent.pull_occupant` reads the root beside `occupants`, which keeps its anchor-rule meaning; `pulls` rides `host`; `repos.<repo>.pull` is parsed and written by `set_settings`. Two readings the design left open were settled in the build: a checkout with a commit of its own is *refused* before the occupant is read (git does not allow it whoever is there), and an occupant whose state cannot be read is *waiting* with `occupant: null` and `why: unreadable`. The origin note's tail and the Repos card's switch are TD-263's rest.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-263 slice 2, grinder-ao-1): the *behind* origin note's tail and the Repos card's **pull** switch with its reading built. The page reads `host`'s `pulls` at most once in five seconds, with the board read. The note draws no tail for a reading of *current* or *pulled*: the board read is then older than the checkout, and the next read says so. An unreadable occupant reads *waiting: a session here cannot be read, so it is taken as mid-turn* on the card.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-269, the designer): rule 3's *A manager on call is a seat of this rule* says why a person's session joins under the seat's id: the grant and the id survive the close.
- 2026-10-02 (TD-274 slice 4, grinder-ao-1): rule 8 *A question's end is work* built at the home — an orphaned question's lapse or answer, its asker's team wound down, writes `work_waiting` at once with `questions: [{id, ref, name, how, kind}]` (the kind for the Inbox row's *steer* / *ask*) and the reference under the asker's name; `_work_mark` keeps the questions with no lane news; Dismiss writes no question's reference to `lane_seen`. Rule 8's replays now keep every record's mail (a seat's alone before), and `ao team start` (and the Org's Start, Members… → Add member) asks `keep_mail` for a member whose name a closed record holds, where the one starting is a person or that record's controller (a session's start that is neither starts the member empty and says so in its notes), so the lapse note or the answer reaches the successor through the controller check (§4.9b) — the badge alone hands no mailbox on (§9 invariant 9; the techlead's read of #932 refused an earlier shape that keyed on it). The Inbox row's clause and the card note's count came the same day (TD-274's Inbox half, §4.5a).
- 2026-10-05 (TD-330, grinder-ao-1): three loose ends of *Balance* settled. Rule 8 keeps a standing balance hold beside an earlier bound that displaces it (`held.balance`); until then a usage hold took `held` and dropped it, so a failed reading when the usage hold lifted started a team that was over its line (#799's read). A turn of `on_work` to `ask` and back still drops it, written down as meant: `ask` holds nothing back, and the next `start` reads the line afresh. The `host` read carries every team's mark as `balance`, a mark with an empty `repo` included, and `ao team list` and `ao team balance` read it there; before, such a mark was said only by the refusal and the notes (#847's read). The manager's reading stays one layer deep, written down as how it is meant while nested teams are not built (#798's read).
- 2026-10-05 (TD-328, grinder-ao-1, found pressing TD-152's live check on a scratch home): *Start time* gains the two acts the design had left to the live-session code — **Kill** on a `scheduled` record is its Cancel, since a killed record read `exited` with a run that never happened and no policy would start it, and `ao team stop --now` must still stop a member not yet started; **Switch to interactive** is refused until it runs, since the tick started it unattended from the launch record anyway, undoing the switch.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-330, grinder-ao-2): the page's half of (3). The team card's **over its line** note and the Settings page's **balance** mark now read the home's `host` read as well as the `repos` reading. A mark with no `repo`, which no checkout's reading carries, is drawn there too. The Org page reads it from the `host` read it already takes, a delta reads it from the cached `host` reading beside `work`, and Settings reads it from its own `host` read.
- 2026-10-05 (TD-332, grinder-ao-1; from the review of PR #868): rule 10 asked `gh pr view` for an open PR's head on every pass, and a PR closed unmerged for the record's whole life, since only a merged PR's read stood. The repo's PR reading now keeps each PR's head (`reports.PR_FIELDS` gains `headRefOid`, the row's `head`) and rule 10 reads the head and state there first (`cadence.head_from`), asking `gh` only for a PR the reading lacks; `cadence.head` gives the state word, and an entry carries `closed` as it carries `merged` (`cadence.settled`), a PR found closed at the head it was read at marked so without running the script again.

## 7. Phases

- 2026-10-07: attachments (TD-002) built for a session on this host, so phase 1 lacks only the phone layout (TD-003).
- 2026-09-10: `ao --skill` (TD-019) was pulled forward from phase 5 and landed in phase 1.
- 2026-09-10: herdr confirmed not to replace the second-host step — phase 2 builds the ssh transport as designed ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-10-herdr-spike.md)).
- 2026-09-16: phase 2's hub-and-spoke ssh transport was replaced by the home-and-node split (§4.4a) at about the same cost; the laptop-worker-to-kmaster-manager mail test through a laptop sleep was added to phase 2 (TD-057).
- 2026-09-22: the plan was re-baselined. Phase 1 done except attachments (TD-002) and the phone layout (TD-003). Phase 2 has home/node modes, the link and a container node on the home's machine; no machine node in use yet, no phone route in, no terminal over the link, no attachments. Phase 3 has the stop time and the usage gate (in build); no run-window, stall, credential-lapse, exit-reap or stranded-work policies; the samscrape team was defined 2026-09-22 with its briefs ported from tdgrind while tdgrind's cron still runs. Phase 4 not started. Phase 5 not started; `ao --skill` and `ao team --skill` are built.

## 8. Lessons carried in

- 2026-10-08 (TD-425, grinder-ao-1): the synced-file rule's `CADENCE_ATTENTION_SCOPE` is set by every launch (§4.3); the hook's `ao status` branch stays the one exception until dev-cadence drops it on TD-425's live check.
- 2026-10-08 (TD-159, the anchor): the rule for which side a thing lives on, agentorc or dev-cadence, added from the inventory in ADR 2026-10-08. A synced file never calls `ao`, and agentorc passes a difference in by an environment variable.
- No dated facts removed.

## 9. Invariants

- 2026-10-07 (TD-381, the designer): invariant 2 names the anchor seat as the checkout's one agent only while no person's session holds it.
- 2026-10-06 (TD-345, the designer): invariant 9 extended to flows — the host agent never reads a flow, the record gains no flow or stage field, no rule keys on a flow or a stage's `name`, and `relaunch`, `sit_out` and `closed_for: {why: sit_out}` are marks a person's act writes and the tick reads as marks. §4.9c had stated the load-bearing rule (*the host agent never sees a flow, and the record gains no flow field*) and §9 did not.
- 2026-09-12: invariant 11's membership half (`controllers` on the target) proposed (§4.8, TD-036); the grant was named `orchestrate` until TD-055 step 3 renamed it `control`.
- 2026-09-13: invariant 5's refusal of any acting RPC from a session onto an interactive session became a gate in code (TD-041).
- 2026-09-14: invariant 5's "a message is not an act of control" clause and invariant 13 (the wake budget) written with §4.10; invariant 14 written with §4.9a.
- 2026-09-16: invariant 13's refill rule rewritten — restored by time and by a person only, never by anything a session does (the old rule let a controller's nudge and a non-mail turn reset it). The fourth review that day settled that a controller's `send` is never charged to the recipient's budget. Invariant 5 gained the person-inbox route for a session that wants the person, and lost the sentence saying a person's read changes state. The doorbell rules (fixed text, count only, never into a person's session or an unreadable composer) were added to invariant 13. Invariant 9's `team`-badge exception named. Invariant 15 added with §4.4a.
- 2026-09-19: invariant 15's "on one host the connecting process's pane" identity rule added with §4.8a.
- 2026-09-20: `restart_wanted` added to invariant 14 (TD-083, §4.9a); `suspended` added to invariant 15's home-owned list (§4.8a).

## 10. Open questions

- 2026-09-25 (TD-177, Paul): *what may a manager do with the repo's numbers* — a balance line (open PRs above n, the oldest past d, the reader's queue past its bound) on which the manager stops handing out claims and asks the techlead to read; the numbers made visible first (TD-176), the rule after.
- 2026-09-04 morning: name chosen `sessionherd`; 2026-09-04 evening: renamed `agentorc` to sit beside cmdorc — free on PyPI, one empty GitHub repo of that name, no dashboards among the neighbours.
- 2026-09-04: Tailscale-only chosen over a password for the UI in phase 1; superseded 2026-09-06.
- 2026-09-04: the card-grid sort was called "attention" until it collided with the Attention tab; renamed Urgent first.
- 2026-09-04: `done` state renamed `closed`, reached only by the person's Close.
- 2026-09-04: existing-worktree picker decided as "only exited/closed worktrees offered; in-use ones greyed with 'resume from the Team'"; superseded 2026-09-06 by the Where control as built (named worktree, no picker). Noted 2026-09-12: §4.5a is the authority.
- 2026-09-05: ttyd vs pty bridge — the bridge reversed an earlier lean toward ttyd. Deciding fact: the UI reaches hosts over ssh anyway, so the terminal is about 150 lines of pty; ttyd would need a daemon per host, a port forward per host and a websocket proxy on top.
- 2026-09-05: packaging fixed as one distribution with an extra, after the 2026-09-04 two-package layout decision.
- 2026-09-05: the pinned layout chosen as per-browser `localStorage`.
- 2026-09-05: the UI process runs wherever `agentorc[ui]` is installed; for Paul kmaster (no VPS existed; kmaster already lingered and held every session); the VPS, if one appears, is a session host in phase 2.
- 2026-09-06: "Tailscale only" generalised to never a bare public port; the relay transport (§4.5b) named for non-technical users and a hosted service.
- 2026-09-09 to 2026-09-10 (TD-014): the herdr spike measured herdr 0.9.0 as a `sessionorc` substrate — Claude Code's state under herdr is screen-scraped (its integration reports only a session id), the status event carries no kind and no text, a usage-limit screen reads as `idle`, an outside source cannot own a Claude pane's state, a server restart kills every pane process, and the socket API is per machine. Decided (a) independent.
- 2026-09-10: worker types question raised the day the Team first showed four workers and could not say which TDs any held. The first draft made `role` a field with a progress record behind it; the review asked whether the verbs (report what was found or finished, act on other sessions) were the real thing — a grinder also files findings, a label keyed on intent misdescribes it, and "may act on other sessions" is a grant a person should see and revoke, not a promise in a brief.
- 2026-09-10: the name-per-scope question raised when the Team showed `aotest` beside `aotest-2` and `tdgrind-ao-1` twice (one exited, one working): the `-2` suffix kept tmux happy while the record kept the original name, and `ao focus` took the full id. Resume had already superseded its exited record (PR #17).
- 2026-09-12: orchestrator membership raised — several orchestrators in sight (guardians' own, a ui orc and a backend orc for a large repo, a read-only cross-repo status orc, an orc-of-orcs Paul talks to); `orchestrate` was one bit on the caller and the gate never looked at the target, so every one would reach every session on the host. Stored on the target because the gate is then one lookup, needs no second list, reloads with the record and dies with it; the orchestrator's member view is derived, never a cache. Prior-art survey ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-12-orchestrator-membership-prior-art.md)): OTP, systemd and Circus each arrived at a restart ceiling the design had nothing of; Kubernetes allows several owners but one managing controller, and no supervisor surveyed supports N controllers per unit; systemd's `StopWhenUnneeded=` collects a unit nothing depends on — both deliberate departures. The "3–5 agents" coordination knee did not survive its fact-check (ADR §6); the gap it pointed at is real and unmeasured. Written 2026-09-12, go from Paul 2026-09-13, TD-036 steps 1–3 landed the same day, go/no-go: go, six steps in order.
- 2026-09-13: "two controllers disagree" raised by Paul. What existed: a worker reached upward only through `ao progress` / `ao finding` (declarations about references, not messages), two orchestrators reached each other only by `ao send` typing into the other's terminal — permitted by the grant by accident and closed by TD-036's gate. §4.8 left "not double-nudging" to briefs, which is no rule at all. Paul's direction: the worker should put the conflict to both controllers and have them resolve it together, and only when they cannot does it go to a person as a board line. To design under TD-039: a conflict report naming both instructions and both controllers, delivery to every controller, a bounded exchange between them, escalation to `user_attention.md` with the exchange attached; not agreed whether the exchange is a session channel or the worker's Focus thread, or who writes the board line. Answered 2026-09-14 as §4.10 — the gap was a missing concept (sessions could not message each other at all), not a conflict feature.
- 2026-09-13: nouns above a session decided with Paul ([ADR](decisions/2026-09-13-org-teams-projects.md)); guardians is five repos; sequencing in TD-040 after TD-036; the Team page becomes Org when teams are definable; §4.9 written the same day.
- 2026-09-13: product name raised by Paul; agentorc.com is a pre-launch business workflow product, agentorg.ai is live (checked 2026-09-13).
- 2026-09-13: devcontainer question raised from the `guardians` constellation — five repos under `GuardiansoftheHeart/guardians-devenv`, worked in a VS Code devcontainer at `/workspaces/guardians` (`~/dev/guardians` outside), not on kmaster (only its D1 backups were). A session inside is in another mount namespace and process tree; the hook inside must reach the agent's socket outside, which is the state feed (§4.2). Three shapes weighed; (b) a second host chosen by the anchor session while writing §4.9, revisable; guardians stays off kmaster and its team start waits for phase 2's transport.
- 2026-09-13: Herd page renamed Team by Paul.
- 2026-09-14: Paul: *"we have multiple cases for it now: orc → worker, worker → orc, orc ↔ orc. It seems like worker ↔ worker will be desired as well"* — agent-to-agent messaging made first class. The review found one mechanism (`ao send`, keystrokes into a pane) serving four cases and working for one; the cause a conflation of *may A act on B?* with delivery. Revised the same day on Paul's three objections to the first draft: the control/message line was "pretty thin" because it named the mechanism rather than the principle, so §4.10 states mediation and attribution and a scale of intrusiveness; the mailbox was checked for portability across model types (data neutral, surfacing not), so `mail` joined the §4.3 contract with a pane write as the honest fallback; and "a message never starts a turn" was withdrawn as too heavy-handed — it banned the common case (a session talking to an idle one) — replaced by a wake budget restored by any turn mail did not start (itself revised 2026-09-16). Bounds left without values, the way §4.8's restart ceiling was taken from prior art rather than invented.
- 2026-09-14: Paul asked whether a team winds down when it runs out of work — "no production system to keep checking, no TDs". It did not: what looked like wind-down was three brief paragraphs agreeing with each other (*stop when your lane is done*, restart a worker that exited *with lane items still open*, *stop when every member has exited*), which holds for a fixed lane and fails for free-pick, the lane ao-grind actually runs. §4.9a written; built under TD-053; the three guards (a reason required, the lead's second reading, an early declaration reported rather than acted on) carry no numbers yet, deliberately.
- 2026-09-16: Paul: *"should we have our mail system notify an idle agent … when mail is received for it?"* and *"do we have a lifecycle for the mail items?"* §4.10 had named no wake mechanism for Claude Code and had not said what `read_at` meant. Doorbell and lifecycle added. An independent Sonnet review shaped five rules: no sender-written text in the line (`about` is free text, and a pasted-and-submitted `about` would be a `send` with no gate); `stop_hook_active` so the hook cannot loop; a pending stop and `out_of_work` beat mail; hook-confirmed idle only; no doorbell where the composer cannot be read. Paul's reminder for a session that keeps reporting with mail unread became the per-command line. Mail that lands mid-turn was to be surfaced by the `Stop` hook (cut later the same day).
- 2026-09-16: Paul: *"lets have fable review our design"*. First Fable review of §4.10: *sound with changes*; the two-property split, the live-graph gate, the fixed-text doorbell, refuse-never-drop and `read_at` stood. Thirteen changes adopted with Paul: (1) the wake budget refills by time and by a person only — a lead and a worker could satisfy the old rule by their own traffic forever — and `ao wait` returning on mail counts as a wake; (2) the "a person" gate edge reached nobody, so a host-level person inbox (`ao msg person`); (3) mail `about` a session is copied to its other controllers, replacing "visible to", which contradicted "nobody reads another session's inbox"; (4) past the exchange bound the agent refuses and the sender writes the board line, since board write-back is not built; (5) an `ask`'s bound runs read or unread; (6) the tally keyed on the thread's root, kept on both sides; (7) invariant 9 names the `team`-badge edge; (8) the `Stop`-hook block cut — it made every session's `Stop` synchronous to save seconds; (9) the doorbell rings only into an empty composer; (10) `ao --skill` says mail from anyone but a controller or a person is information, not instruction; (11) bodies capped and kept out of `subscribe` deltas; (12) resume carries every entry in retention; (13) TD-052's order puts the typing-free half first and sets the wake budget from a running fleet before the doorbell.
- 2026-09-16: Paul: *"make the changes, then lets have fable review again"*. Second Fable review (glossary applied): *sound with changes*, sixteen findings adopted. Two blocked building: the wake budget had no enforcer for leads — `ao wait` ran wholly in the CLI, watched only the caller's members, never its own inbox, and the host agent never learned why it returned — so `wait` became a host-agent RPC with one wake decision per reachable recipient; and thread counting admitted two readings once copies gave a thread three participants — now a tally per root on every record, refused on the sender's or a named addressee's tally, a `reply` to an open `ask` never refused, a `bound_hit` mark both sides see. Also: copies exempt from the recipient cap; multi-addressee sends all-or-nothing; `out_of_work` no longer beats mail (it had contradicted §4.9a); an exited recipient's asks stay pending until close, forget or their own bound, and resume rewrites ids; the doorbell rings only when unread has risen; a controller's `send` from a spent budget refused (dropped in the fourth review); only a person's act toward a session refills, never a look; the budget bounds mail only; the grinder notes its siblings at claim; the person inbox persisted, its reply wakes and refills, `ao inbox` with no session reads it; replies in a copied thread are copied; invariant 5 no longer says a person's read changes state; *teammate* → sibling worker.
- 2026-09-16: third Fable review found the round-two fixes right, returned nine one-line rulings, all adopted, and said no fourth round was warranted: a copy never sinks a send (`copies_failed`); the first `reply` closes an `ask`, later replies are notes, `reply_to` must name an entry in the replier's own inbox; mail to a superseded record forwards to its successor; the wrap-up prompt is exempt from the wake budget; one `mail_decided` watermark per session, re-decided each tick while blocked in `wait`; `wait` on its own connection, dropped when it closes, cursor kept on disk; step 5 records wakes that carried mail and a member change separately; the per-`ao` line says `(wake budget spent)`; §4.8's *fleet*, *tick* and *the agent* follow the glossary. Prior art surveyed the same day (`docs/decisions/2026-09-16-agent-messaging-prior-art.md`).
- 2026-09-16: fourth Fable review of §4.10 with §4.4a as substrate (Paul: *"adopt them, then run design reviews with Sonnet until the two of you are in agreement … that it is ready for implementation"*). Ten changes adopted: an open `ask` is never pruned (retention and the bound had been independent, so a read `ask` could be pruned before it was answered and the answer refused); the pair tally for reply-less mail is windowed, not lifetime (a daily `note` would have made a pair deaf in weeks); a decision not to wake leaves `mail_decided` where it was, so a refilled budget rings; an identical repeat is refused, never dropped, and a nonce retry returns the original verdict; every `send` recorded on the target as `sends`, a `conflict` citing two by id instead of guessing who typed; the round-two controller `send` charge dropped as redundant with the lead's wake charge and as the source of a refusal mode and a `wait --timeout 0` hole; `ao --skill` displaces rather than grows past 120 lines; a person gets a Message control, not only Reply; an `ask`'s bound is wall-clock only; a person-inbox refusal names the board. Sonnet round one (*ready with changes*, four findings adopted): a `conflict` is an `ask` for every rule and escalates by the generic `bound_hit`/expiry path; `sends` joins §4.4a's home-owned list and invariant 15; a person's message into a thread is uncounted and resets it; *reachable* enters the glossary. Round two (*ready with changes*, three adopted): `sends` moves on resume with the entries that cite it, and a `send` to the superseded id is refused where mail forwards; a `conflict`'s first reply closes every addressee's copy; `bound_hit` enters the glossary. Round three: *ready*, no findings — TD-052 step 1 may start.
- 2026-09-16: Paul: *"Seems like we need to solve the cross-machine design now or the comms between agents are left funky"* (TD-057). Phase 2's hub-and-spoke transport gave mail no route between hosts; a Fable review reframed the gap as a session graph spanning hosts — the gate, copies, tallies, budgets, `wait` and `_supersede` all assumed one process held every record, so cross-host control was equally unanswered. §4.4a written; kmaster is home; rejected the UI host as router (a stateful, sleeping client tier), a host-agent mesh (split tallies after a partition; a laptop that must listen), the relay now. TD-052 step 1 proceeded single-host with the address and id-minting choices made so nothing migrates. A Fable review of §4.4a as merged (*sound with changes*, not overbuilt for kmaster + a laptop), all adopted with Paul: a request's identity comes from its channel, never a field (a same-named laptop session could have passed the gate as kmaster's lead); each field has one owner and invariant 15 says so, since a Sonnet-added policy rule had made the node a second writer; only stopping policies run on the node offline, starting ones at the home; nodes keep their records as a replica, the home adopts unknown reports and is rebuilt from replicas, mail and budgets are what a lost store loses, a nightly tarball is the backup; a person may create a session on an offline node; link keys bound to a host name; permission prompts wait on the node and fall back to the tool's own dialog when the link drops; one reachability source; reconnect is snapshot then events; cross-host teams refused while a host is down; the terminal and attachments keep the UI's own ssh; and on Paul's *"won't the inability to reach home mean the UI will not render?"*, `ao` and `ao ui` on a node fall back to that host's own sessions, labelled *offline*.
- 2026-09-17: devcontainer follow-up — Paul: contractmatch cannot be ground by a worker on kmaster, the Flutter SDK is in its devcontainer; *the grinder can wait*. After two Fable reviews the same day and Paul's steer to the long-term shape regardless of the work, the (b) answer stood and §4.4a *A container node* was written: the same absolute mount path (or git worktrees break across the boundary and invariant 2 has no identity to compare); the home provisions the container (generates its definition from the repo's with the person's mounts dropped) rather than a person installing `ao` inside (drifts, dies with a rebuild) or the Dockerfile carrying it (couples the image to every promote); `docker exec` is backwards for the link though right for the terminal; the person's own VS Code container is theirs to keep clear.
